Baba would say, "You... let go of the stick."

DUTY OF A SANNYASI

The worker would reply, "No, I won't leave the stick." Baba asked three times and all three times this worker refused. There was a little change in Baba's tone. It softened and he asked, "Why don't you want to leave the stick?"

"Because you hit me," the worker replied like a small boy. He further said, "I will release the stick only on one condition and that is if you give me promise that you won't hit anymore."

Baba gave the assurance and the stick was released. Baba's face was amused and became normal.

We simply could not control ourselves and burst into laughter. By that time Baba was back sitting upon His cot. He was completely normal. He was smiling. The absolute peace and tranquility of pure spirituality was radiating from His Face. We all felt that He was the embodiment of Bliss (Anandamurti). How we enjoyed! Whatever my reaction might have been, it was silenced by just one glorious smile of His.

DUTY OF A SANNYASI

Then over a period of time we became eager to get beaten by Baba. They are bestowed with the highest blessings who have got the darshan of the magical stick of Baba. The future of such devotees truly blossomed. Still today, by His grace, His disciples can get danda darshan (darshan of His stick; divine scolding). May all who wish to be more sincere disciples be blessed with it.

This was how the concept of collective punishment as an indispensable part of a cult taught to Bhairava by Lord Shiva was introduced in Ananda Marga and continued until 1990 and how we enjoyed it, loved it.

As Baba said,

"If the time does come when the advancement becomes too slow, someone will be needed who can give society a hard hammering. Whether people like this person or not, love him or not — whether they feel love, devotion, respect or fear — under his fast-tempoed hammering the society will move ahead. People basically want to feel the blow that will move them ahead. The blow will be painful, people fear it, but in their minds they want it, because only under this hammering will they decide to move forward. It is a strange condition — 'I feel afraid, yet at the same time this is what I want. Or, no, I don't want to move ahead — I'll go a little later' — but then one is forcibly pulled ahead. The feet do not want to move ahead, they tremble, but the minds want to move ahead. The need for this kind of treatment arises when people collectively are not able to advance towards the goal under their own internal power...
When for the benefit of human society, for human welfare, something has to be done which is not so easy to do, which is rarely done, which will deliver the necessary blow to the human race — when the right entity or being is needed to do this work — what will Parama Puruśa do? He will create a body for Himself with the help of the five fundamental factors, and He will come. He will say, 'I will awaken the práńa (vital energy) of these dying people with a timely slap, I will stir up their práńa.' He will create vibrations in their práńa. He will not tolerate idleness. He will not allow us to waste this valuable human life." ("Kŕśńa Unparalleled", Discourses on Kŕśńa and the Giitá)

NOTHING BETWEEN BABA AND I

Baba has always enjoyed the company of His workers. Many times, like a father with a small child, He used to play with them and enjoy Himself. One favourite sport was wrestling. Whatever their age might be, even those older than Him, he used to ask them to take part in wrestling matches where He would be the referee. To the amusement of those watching, devotees used to eagerly take part in such matches. These joyful pranks of Baba were loved by the devotees as just another chance to please their loving Baba and none else.

I was also part of the drama of doing vipariitakaranii mudra for thirty minutes. This mudra is performed by lying on one's back with the hands raising the lower body upwards in a straight line such that the entire weight of the body is on the shoulders like sarvaungasana. The feet are kept together with the chin on the chest and one's concentration on the navel. If I felt tired or stressed doing this continuously for such a long time, I would check and see when Baba was absorbed in some matter and then silently lower my legs. Sometimes, Baba would catch us and say, "Who is there, beat him." Then again we would keep our legs raised in this mudra. Workers were on both sides of the room and when Baba was looking at one side, the workers on the other side would lower their legs until Baba would catch them. This is how we played with Him. This playing hide-and-seek with Him we all enjoyed so much. It was really the state of madhuvan — the sweet stage of cosmic love where there is no reservation or hesitation. To gaze at Baba in this mudra was a special grace. In this mudra, body consciousness is lessened. The mind moves upwards and one is able to feel His bliss

much more strongly. This liila of Baba continued for many days and months during the early 1980s.

If Baba praised a worker for his work in front of everyone, it was a danger signal or indication of possible downfall. Baba used to almost never praise a worker in front of them. Behind their backs, however He used to praise them. To their face, He would mostly condemn them for failing to meet superhuman work targets. In order to make the workers superhuman, He would give so much pressure, scold and beat them. But the workers knew that Baba could not live without them, just like a child being scolded knows that his mother adores him.

NOTHING BETWEEN BABA AND I

Once, Baba's wife complained about Baba beating me too much in Ranchi. I was beaten for the mistake of the junior worker under my supervision. Due to the severe beating I refused to go see Baba. Baba called me many times to His room and I refused to go to Him. When Baba's PA convinced me to come, then I went to Baba. I was standing outside His room when He came out to take me inside the room. Baba's wife and the PA were there. Baba's wife again asked why Baba beat me so much.

Baba replied that this was between Him and me. Then Baba caught my hand and took me inside His room and bolted the door Himself. I was in His room for more than four to five hours. For that sweet bliss I thanked so much that junior worker for making the mistake that ended up with my punishment and then the bliss of Baba's close contact.

But just see how things changed? Later, how we hankered to get a beating from Him. Blessed are those who had the chance to be beaten by Him.

OPPOSITION AND VICTORY IN UNA

Our pracar work was gaining momentum in Himachal Pradesh. I was the provincial worker and a unit was established in Una, the headquarters of the present-day district. Mr Khazan Singh Negi was the deputy commissioner and Mr. M. C. Chouhan was the head revenue administration officer of Una. A good number of new persons had joined Ananda Marga. I used to stay with Mr Chouhan.

When the magistrate of Una came to know that a sizeable number of government staff had joined Ananda Marga, he warned the persons working under him in writing that disciplinary action would be taken against them because they had joined Ananda Marga.

I procured a copy of this letter. This really scared our margis who were government employees. When Baba came to Delhi and was told about the letter, He immediately called me and instructed me to rush back to Una and see that a case was filed in the court against the magistrate.

I did not attend DMC and went back to Una. When I reached there, I found that the person who had issued the notice had been transferred and M.C. Chouhan had been promoted as the magistrate of the area. This was a great surprise to me. Later Ananda Marga developed very much there. Many people took initiation into meditation.

OPPOSITION AND VICTORY IN UNA

We distributed the uniform prescribed by Baba to children in many government primary schools. This was black trousers and red shirt for boys and a black skirt and red shirt for girls. This was highly appreciated.

It was in this way, by constant opposition, that Ananda Marga progressed so much in the state. Baba always taught us never to take any challenge lying down but to challenge the challenger.

WE ARE SMILING BECAUSE BABA IS SMILING

This sweet memory is of one DMC in Varanasi. The environment was so soothing. I am talking about those days when Baba and full-time workers used to live together in the same building. Sometimes Baba himself in lungi (sarong) and undershirt came to the kitchen to see what was cooking. As the head of our family, He personally took care of those who were cooking food and how they were cooking. Those sisters who were working in the kitchen found themselves truly blessed. Even through the simple chatting about cooking, Baba would not just teach them and make them laugh, He would also thrill the mind with the spiritual exhilaration of His love. We five to six workers were all happily talking to each other in the room of the same house. There was all-round ecstasy as Baba was there with us. On some topic we were laughing loudly. Suddenly Baba entered the room and we all stood up in respect. Baba was in domestic dress—in lungi and undershirt. This sudden visit of Baba was surprising as well as pleasant. We all were laughing freely and still our face was in that mood only. Suddenly Baba talked to me—because I was standing near the door and I was first from the right side.

Baba asked, "Why are you smiling?"

I told Him the reason but Baba replied, "No, this is not the answer."

Baba asked this question to the other dadas also. They also told the reason as per their conviction. But Baba always said, "No, this is not the answer."

WE ARE SMILING BECAUSE BABA IS SMILING

Similarly, Baba asked this to all the dadas and everyone answered this question in their own way. But Baba was not satisfied. At last

one dada was left. When asked, he told Baba, "We all are smiling and laughing because You are laughing, You are smiling."

Smilingly, Baba replied, "This is the correct answer."

Then Baba further revealed, "This world laughs, birds chirp, flowers blossom, rivers meander, why? You know! Because Parama Purusha (Supreme Entity) is smiling. In His love and bliss, everyone is laughing, smiling. I wish that you all should keep smiling and laughing like this." Gracing us with this beauteous blessing, Baba went back to his room.

WE ARE SMILING BECAUSE BABA IS SMILING

All the happiness in the world is but a fraction of the Macrocosmic Bliss and that divine bliss is His Form (Anandamurti). When we leave the small joys of material life and go inwards through meditation we come in contact with that divine bliss. Then we have the capacity to get the darshan of Lord Anandamurti's ever-smiling face.

Baba explains,

As much as the sádhakas are able to establish their heroism after escaping from the serpent-noose of Avidyá, the Extroversive Force, so much Ánandasvarúpa (essence or natural form of bliss) or Supreme Exaltation they experience; they then become Ánandasvarúpa Himself, the Ánandamúrti (form of bliss) or the very image of Ánanda or Bliss. ("The Intuitional Science of the Vedas – 6: Avidyá", Subhásita Saḿgraha Part 3)

MONKEY DRAMA

I was posted in Himachal Pradesh in March 1964 and worked there until December 1968. There was DMC in Nahan on 23 October 1967. Baba came by road from Chandigarh (Punjab) on the way to Nahan. In discussing the word kákarúha (orchid), in discourse twenty of His encyclopedia Shabda Cayanika Part 3, Baba beautifully described His trip in that Himalayan region. Baba said that this was an ancient kingdom and that Srimaori (the local language) was a most ancient language.

The arrangements for Baba's stay were made in the former royal palace of the king of Nahan. It was lying vacant and the caretaker of the palace was a good margi, so the arrangements were easily made. Baba was very happy staying there.

When Baba arrived, He called me and asked me to see to it that His clothes were properly washed. Then Baba Himself counted how many clothes were there. I told Baba that in all there were fourteen articles of clothing. Then again Baba counted the clothes to see if there were fifteen and then asked me to count all over again. Baba instructed me that He should have all His clothes properly washed, ironed and ready on time.

All the clothes were washed and spread for drying. After some time, there was a sound outside. When I went outside to see what was happening, I was stunned to see that some monkeys had picked up Baba's dhoti (men's lower body garment) and climbed into a tree with it. These monkeys were playing joyfully with the dhoti. The dhoti was torn into pieces.

MONKEY DRAMA

Then hell exploded inside my head. Now I could understand why Baba had repeatedly counted one to fifteen. Now, all together there were fourteen cloths. Vishokananda, Baba's PA, and myself went to the market to buy another dhoti of the same size and with a

black border of Bengali design. We did not find any. We purchased a dhoti with a green border.

We gathered our courage and told Baba about the monkeys and the dhoti. Baba was very angry with me.

The most interesting part of the incident was that when Himachal Pradesh margis there heard about this, they started struggling among themselves to grab as much as they could of the pieces of Baba's dhoti that had fallen from the tree. Still today, some margis have preserved those dhoti pieces. When a dada from the Archives asked for those pieces for Baba's Museum, they refused to part with them.

MONKEY DRAMA

Today when I think about it, I remember that in the last discourse on Gurukula, Baba said,

You people know that when clothes are woven white ants and mice try to eat them. So the cloth has to be saved from the destructive activities of white ants and mice. So whenever you make something, establish a protective fence to protect it from the evil forces.
In days of old, when womenfolk used to prepare dried mango cakes, the young boys used to sit by the side to keep the crows away with a stick in the hand. Similarly you should be careful and alert always. ("Gurukul: History and Planning", Adorning the Dawn: Discourses on Neohumanist Education).

Despite this warning on September 29 1990, we have failed to properly preserve Baba's gifts to humanity.

However, one beautiful event happened in Nahan. The revolutionary marriage of Miss Kanee Fatima from the Laddakhi Muslim community and Omprakash from the Brahmin community of Jammu was arranged at this DMC in front of all the margis. Later the bride was given the Sanskrit name Savitri. Baba was present as witness to the marriage and gave His blessings to the bride and groom.

READINESS TO SACRIFICE

There was Ananda Purnima DMC in Allahabad on the 28 of May 1967. Ananda Marga was growing rapidly. Baba had called a workers meeting.

Baba was simply sitting in one of His unforgettable blissful moods. All the workers were sitting around Baba. Baba was not saying anything. We were all very sad about our brothers who had been killed. On March 5th, our five brothers had been murdered defending Baba and Ananda Nagar from an attack by the communists. Baba started talking very simply to us. The depths of affection and the moving way He spoke made us all feel so close like a grieving family sitting around the mother. A wave of intense feeling washed over us and we all leapt onto Baba and were holding parts of His body like a crowd of small children embracing their grandfather who has arrived. We were full of such feeling that the security guards of Baba had a hard time freeing Baba from our embraces and finally taking him inside His room.

When Baba left the room, one central dada explained to us about the legal situation at Ananda Nagar, our global headquarters. Dada asked us if we workers could provide some financial help for the court cases against those who tried to kill Baba. Suddenly one dada proposed that why not, if any worker has a wristwatch, they can give it and the sale of the watches can be used for the court cases.

Our brother's appeal was so touching that everyone who had a watch, including me, immediately took off their watches and gave it with joy.

READINESS TO SACRIFICE

Such was the spirit of brotherly sacrifice in those days. I hope by His compassion, those days will come again in the new generation of His workers.

KAPALIKA DIIKSA

I was sure that the recommendation of the DPS (Dharma Pracar Secretary) and GTS (Global Training Secretary) would not work in my case. Our Baba had gradually introduced the system that if a candidate wanted to become an avadhuta, he had to produce a substantial amount of success in his area of posting — such as school, children's home and creating new monks. The recommendations of the DPS and GTS were also required. The final authority was revered Baba Himself.

In my case, the recommendations did not materialize. In addition I was well aware that many persons who joined the organization later as monks had already become avadhutas. Many workers of my year of training had also become avadhutas, but not me. I knew full well that it was only the wish of Baba that would be of any help in my case. Within my mind, I felt assured.

Then came the Ananda Purnima DMC of Allahabad in 1967. As usual, I was unsure if I would become an avadhuta. But lo! Revered Baba inquired about me and said that Vishvabandhu should be in the group for kapalika initiation. The others included Tadbhavananda, Bhaskarananda, Parasattananda, Nityashuddhananda and a few others.

It was only after we were ordained with kapalika diiksa that we were given the avadhuta oath. I was feeling internally uncomfortable with the name Vandanananda, but later I came to like it very much. Reading our Guru Puja being described as Guru Vandana in Caryacarya Part 1 and reading about "Guru Vandana" in Shabda Cayanika Part 25 made me very happy that Baba gave me this name.

KAPALIKA DIIKSA

Baba explained the difference between Guru Puja and Guru Vandana explaining that puja is when we with our mental force do any work to satisfy our Ista (Ideational Desideratum). When with one-

pointed determined longing and action we strive to please the Guru through shravana (hearing and singing His name, stories of Him), manana (meditation) and nididhyasana (concentrating all propensities, all mental force into one point and surrendering it to Him within one's I-feeling) — this is Guru Puja. Baba said that without Guru Puja, success couldn't be achieved in the physical world, psychic world or the spiritual world.

Baba then said that puja can be completely done in the psychic sphere and it can be carried out through righteous actions. Vandana however, is beyond this. In addition to what is done in puja, vandana is a special form of pleasing Him wherein the puja is done drenched with para stuti or shuddha stuti.

KAPALIKA DIIKSA

Stuti refers to praising the Lord and is usually a form of flattery to the Lord to gain material and psychic treasures. In Ananda Sutram, Baba clearly rejected stuti as a false form of devotion. In the lower stages of prema there is more duality and hence one will express prema in a flow of unfolding feelings (bhava photaye dhárá) like in a bhajan. However in the stage of vandana or para (higher) stuti, the inner self (antar karana) has already been offered in surrender (nivedana). Para stuti in Tantra has been defined as being possessed by the Lord (samáveśa) as here the devotee in entering into permanent union (mahámilan). In normal, lower devotional love (prema), one has union (milan) and then follows a crude/normal state of consciousness but in the state of shuddha (pure) stuti, milan may become less or more but the devotee is always in partial milan at least. In the ecstasy of this union, the devotee cannot express an unfolding flow of bhava like in a bhajan. Instead, in this state of vandana, the devotee expresses in language short verbal explosions of love while in different states of being possessed by Him (samavesa). This is why the writings of such devotees seem to be like religious (apara) or lower stuti. The objective of this ecstatic, adorative praise of the Lord is to enable one to achieve the highest stage of pleasing the Dhyeya (goal of dhyana). And this is Guru Vandana. The Dhyeya is Baba as the beloved fountain of love we experience in our dhyana.

However, this was all far beyond me at that time. I soon began to realize how much I had changed when I returned back from Himachal. Since I was first posted in Himachal, there was a strong unit of margis and a good number of students had learnt meditation. The town of Bilaspur was newly established in the upper part of this region. The old town had been submerged in Govind Sagar Lake. In summer, when the snow is gone the submerged town appears like a ghost town.

KAPALIKA DIIKSA

There was a Tantric whom I had seen earlier who stayed there. He wore only a laungotá (yogi underwear) even during the cold Himalayan winter. He had a long beard and vermilion was always smeared on his forehead. The local people believed that because the Tantric stayed there near that underwater town, that he must possess occult powers such as the ability to walk on water. He was known as Aghori Baba. Aghora in Indian popular culture means an extreme monk who will eat anything including human flesh. So after sunset no one dared to go to that part of the abandoned town. Everyone in that town had great respect for him — not out of love but out of fear. Many times we had met each other and we both greeted each other. He used to stare at me scornfully.

The day I stepped down from the bus at the Bilaspur bus stand after my kapalika initiation, this Tantric was there at a distance of fifty feet. To my surprise, he prostrated facing me. Then he shouted something that I clearly heard. "Now I have to leave this town, another kapalika has come."

The next morning, he retreated backwards with big, open eyes staring at me. Thereafter I never saw him again in Bilaspur.

TESTERS BEING TESTED

There were two more sannyasis in Himachal Pradesh whom I came in contact with. They were very much impressed with the meaningful changes in behavior and lifestyle of many persons, especially youngsters, who learned Ananda Marga meditation. One of them always wore a white dhoti and the other wore an orange lungi and gown. Both spoke good English and were knowledgeable persons. They were friends and lived together.

I developed a close friendship with them. I used to sit in meditation, which they appreciated very much. They used to perform some kind of prayer rituals. We soon became close companions. The orange-clad monk was clean-shaven and his name was Swami Omkareshvaranand. One day this monk expressed to me the desire to learn meditation and so I initiated him.

Acarya Rudrananda, secretary of SDM at the time, came to Himachal. When I met him in Simla, I told him about these monks. He too expressed interest in them. At that time, Baba was coming to Delhi for DMC soon, so we both planned that since these monks were young, educated and renunciates, why not inspire them to join the Ananda Marga system of sannyasa (renunciate life). We both thought that this would add a feather in our caps.

Accordingly I conveyed to these monks the news about Baba's visit to Delhi and Swami Omkareshvaranand agreed to accompany me to Delhi. In Delhi, there was a line of margis for Personal Contact with Baba. I had earlier explained to the swami about this.

TESTERS BEING TESTED

When he had moved up to being third in line, he said to me that he wanted to go to attend Nature's call. I advised him to come soon, as this was such a divine chance to be alone with Baba — the ultimate Guru.

The swami had already seen some margis who had come out of Baba's room and noticed that they were coming out crying. I felt

that he was becoming uncomfortable and fearful. Thus, he had left the line on this pretext in order to leave and never return.

I was at a loss and so was Rudrananda Dada. He was annoyed with me for not taking proper care of this swami. When the PCs were over, I straightaway entered into Baba's room and with an air of indignation told Baba about how this swami came for PC but then ran away.

TESTERS BEING TESTED

Baba said, "Vandanananda, he wanted to test Me and at the same time, he was afraid of his own misdeeds. He felt that Baba would expose his mistakes and so he ran away."

I was both unhappy as well as happy at this turn of events.

ENDLESS WORK, ENDLESS JOY

In those days Himachal Pradesh was a Union Territory directly controlled by the Government of India. During one DMC in Delhi, Baba asked me to start a school in Dharamsala. A new worker was given to me to help in this work. We both went to Dharamsala and opened a school in a rented building. We were able to collect three children to start with.

Then such a bad misfortune came as the new worker left the place and disappeared. When this was reported to Baba, He sent another worker. With good certificates we started a school at another place. That worker was most hard working and sincere. He later became Acarya Viitamohananda. We had very cordial relations. In 1967, when we came to know about the Ananda Nagar incident where the communists murdered five sannyasis in broad daylight, we were shocked. Together we shed tears. That was the first time we realised that Ananda Marga as a movement was not only being opposed by right wing religious chauvinists but by left wing ideologues as well. That opened my mind so wide that I started to understand the marvel that is PROUT.

I am happy to write here that this Dada and I worked together in Chandigarh, Mumbai and Jaipur region and that too for a long period. Once I was asked by Baba to cover every single district with at least one AMPS unit. In order to reach the common people we must do social service. Baba introduced Paincaseva or five kinds of service. Namely, 1) General — supplying rice, clothes, fuel and oil, 2) Poor Students — books, uniforms and fees, 3) Medical Aid — camps, dispensaries, 4) General Narayan Service — cheap kitchens 5) Special Narayan (Divine) Service — free food to the poor.

Since I was not a worker of ERAWS (Education, Relief and Welfare Section), I felt that I could donate some clothes and books. I was informed that the best kind of service was the distribution of newly

ENDLESS WORK, ENDLESS JOY

sewn school uniforms to poor children. At that time, the margis of Thanedhar were inspired to start an "Apple Growers Forwarding Agency." This was created to eliminate the middlemen who were linking the farmers with the consumers and exploiting both.

We also tried to raise funds to help Ananda Marga at the state and central levels but were unable to proceed very far, although the sincere struggle brought much satisfaction. I had some uniforms for boys and girls distributed to a good number of students in Una. Mr. M.C. Chouhan was the senior officer in the local administration and was a good margi. He started Ananda Marga activities and a good number of people joined Ananda Marga. He took the Marga to Choupal also and opened an AMPS unit there.

In order to reach Choupal, I had to walk sixty-five kilometres, as there was no regular road from Sion to there. One brother asked me to go to Chamba. I agreed, as Baba wanted a school in every district. One spring when I boarded the bus from Pathankot to go to Chamba, I was warned by many people that it was prohibited in the Puranas (Hindu mythological scriptures) for a Vedic sannyasi to go to that area which was the land of beautiful women. If any sannyasi went during Spring, he would invariably fall and settle in family life.

As a Tantric sannyasi, I took this as a challenge. I went there and started a unit of AMPS. One day, I moved further along the river Ravi just to see the beauty of the area. I moved on foot up and still further upwards into the hills. I came in contact with many sannyasis that had left the path by marrying the girls of that area. The beauty along the river Ravi was so enchanting that any person visiting in Uttarayana (January to July) would lose himself or herself in the beauty of that area. It is known as "Chamba ki kalii." The kalii (buds) of Chamba indicated the local women of Chamba. I enjoyed my meditation there and after a week, I returned back. Thereafter I had no chance to visit.

ENDLESS WORK, ENDLESS JOY

Once later, I asked Baba how to overcome such attraction to material life. Baba told me to never think about this and to always remain engaged in more and more service work. Service work shows us the suffering of society and gives us more joy than many lifetimes of worldly happiness.

MOUNTAIN ADVENTURE

One adventure of that time in Himachal Pradesh cannot be forgotten. Our Baba has mentioned about Kinnaur saying,

In Kinnaur the skin colour of the people is yellowish or brown and their noses are flat; the people are as attractive as angels. The derivation of the word Kinnaur is kim plus narah meaning, "Are they human or angels?" ("Geology and Human Civilization")

In obedience to Baba's instruction to cover all districts, Acarya Nityashuddhananda and myself decided to go to Kinnaur for a look at the famous mountain of Kinnaur Kailash near the town of Kalpa. We made a plan and convinced Shrii P.C. Stokes and one other margi from Thanedhar to accompany us.

Winter had already knocked on the door. But who cares when the mind is already set upon doing something and that too when Baba and the ideology are adding fuel to the fire of our desire. So we both boarded a bus to the last stop of Thanedhar. This was the place where English apples were brought for the first time in India by the Stokes family some two centuries ago. We had one school there and at that time a jagriti was being built up. Baba later visited there and conducted a DMC after His release from jail.

MOUNTAIN ADVENTURE

When we boarded the bus, we had no idea that the evening would come with such bad weather. Narkatia is a place high in that range of mountains and from there is an eleven kilometre descent to reach Thanedhar. When our bus reached Narkatia, it was already covered with heavy snowfall. The ongoing road was covered with a tall layer of packed snow. The driver declared that the bus could not go further.

Where to stay? That was the paramount question on our minds.

No lodge, no hotel and no sleeping bags were there. We decided to follow the road leading to Thanedhar on foot. Every person there warned us that we were risking our lives. We paid no heed to their warnings.

We moved, moved and moved, just talking about Baba and His work. We had lathis (staffs) in our hands. That helped us a lot to balance ourselves. Many times we noticed that the snow on the road was knee deep. By the time we approached the first house of a margi, Shrii Kapoor Singh, it was 10:30 p.m. It had taken us a long four hour ordeal in the snow. It was only after reaching the house that we realised how we had risked our lives. In truth, it was only by Baba's grace that we reached safely. The margis gave us hot water treatment for our numbed legs.

MOUNTAIN ADVENTURE

We stayed two to three days and as planned moved by car with Stokes and another margi to Kalpa. We had to stay the night at Rampur to obtain the permission from the government to enter the district of Kinnaur, which had been declared a sensitive area due to national security reasons. After the war with China in 1962, the military was constructing roads and no tourists or any other persons were allowed there. Since Shrii Stokes was a known and respected person in the state and was accompanying us, we obtained the permission.

The next morning we started for Kalpa. By that time, Mr T. S. Thakur, an IAS (Indian Administrative Service) officer, had joined Ananda Marga. He was then single and an honest officer who had helped us procure permission to visit the area. He was in Simla. How he came to Ananda Marga is a unique story. A boy near a village near Morang halfway from Rampur to Kalpa in Kinnaur District had joined Ananda Marga and become an avadhuta. He had been a student of agriculture in Kanpur and later became Acarya Asiimananda Avadhuta. Baba declared that he was the greatest agro-scientist in the world. He was in charge of Baba's garden in Tiljala, Lake Gardens and the vast region of Ananda Nagar. When he was killed in 1990 by the CPI(M) (Communist Party of India – Marxist) at Ananda Nagar, Baba declared that this was a great loss to agriculture. Shrii Thakur was related to Asiimanandji and thus had come to Ananda Marga. Asiimananda Dada had a great liking for Himachal and as the general finance secretary he designed a great plan to raise funds for the centre. All Himachal margis liked him very much due to his humble, unassuming and hardworking nature. He procured two master units (ecovillages) in the state – one in Choupal and the other at Matiana. The master units showed much promise but alas today are barely surviving like orphaned children.

MOUNTAIN ADVENTURE

We reached Kalpa and stayed for two days in the government circuit house and created a base of margis there. What beauty was there to behold! The colour of the Kailash Kinnaur Mountain, which is always covered with snow, can be distinctively seen from there, and with the setting of the sun, a vivid panorama of colours is reflected on this mountain that is considered to have been an abode of Lord Shiva 7500 years ago. Indeed, the spiritual atmosphere is unforgettable but very few persons are allowed to visit there. The small town of Kalpa was covered with snow. When we came back, three young boys joined us to come to Ananda Nagar to study at our school. They were the first children in the valley to leave for studies. Alas, after a few days feeling the heat of Ananda Nagar, they returned back. Their simple mountain minds could not adjust to the complexities of the plains of North India.

When Mr Chauhan from Una was transferred to Chaupal in Mahasu District, he invited me to start an Ananda Marga unit there. This was in the forests and mountains and no roads were there. As mentioned, I had to walk sixty-five kilometres to reach there. A unit was started and it proved to be one of the best units in Himachal Pradesh. Later two young sisters became sannyasis – Avadhutika Ananda Vibha and her sister Avadhutika Ananda Niitishuddha.

KARMA SANNYASA

I would like to relate a story that contradicts traditional beliefs and new age practices. Either one must find a silver line between the two and harmonize both or risk losing both. There is no more natural clash than that between work and worship.

When I was in Himachal Pradesh, a young avadhuta was sent to me with clear instructions that I had to take care of him. I was the only worker in the early years of my posting. This dada was Acarya Nirmeghananda and he was the national secretary of PFI. He was qualified academically and was soft-spoken and a good orator in English and Hindi.

Upon inquiring, I came to know that this dada wanted to first pursue intensive sadhana and attain spiritual realization. Only after spiritual realisation, did he want to perform service in the world, as then it would have more impact. He felt worldly work to be a hindrance and obstacle in the path of spiritual realization. He expressed this to Baba and Baba sent him to me.

He was with me for some three to four months. He was sitting in sadhana for long periods. We went to many places in Himachal Pradesh — Simla, Bilaspur, Mandi, Dharmsala among others. I found him to be a nice and loving dada. One day he expressed a desire to see Baba and then shortly afterwards he left, never to return. Later I learned that he had entered into family life.

KARMA SANNYASA

My mind started searching for a link between the traditional idea of a conflict or dichotomy between work and worship.

I had heard of the Niśkáma karma (desireless action) that Krsna had given in the Giita. This said that we should act without desiring the fruits or results of our actions. However this is just a philosophy or concept and the actual spiritual practice behind this is not known today.

Aside from our practices of meditation, Guru Mantra (mantra pro-

tecting one from creating new samskaras) and dhyana (ideational absorption in the Supreme Beloved), Baba prescribed three practices for acting in a pure manner.

KARMA SANNYASA

  1. Phalákáḿkśá Tyága or renouncing the mental desire and determination to enjoy/obtain the result of the action
  2. Kartrtvábhimána Tyága or abandoning the vanity or egotism that we (a person with a body and many good and bad qualities) are performing any action. In reality, He is the Omnifarious One — He does everything. As Baba says, “Our hands and feet are not ours, they are His, and by serving Himself with those hands and feet, He sports with Himself.”
  3. Surrendering all actions unto the Supreme or surrendering the very process of acting or carrying out any work unto the Supreme.

Baba also gave the practical spiritual practice for the ideal of Karma Sannyasa (renunciation of action; cardinal spirit of action).

KARMA SANNYASA

One can execute some ordinary types of work with one’s own little intellect. But if one aspires to undertake any noble ventures, then one has to unite one’s own intellect with the Cosmic Intellect, and work with the help of the Supreme knowledge of Parama Puruśa. This is the spirit of karma sannyása.
Now what is the method to merge the unit mind in the Cosmic Mind? As the basic trend of the evolutionary process is from the crude towards the subtle, therefore in order to make one’s own intellect subtle, one is to take the ideation of the subtlest entity, not to remain engrossed in crude objects. With the constant ideation of the Supreme, the mind will gradually attain more and more subtlety and will ultimately be converted into the subtlest entity. After this the sense of separation in the microcosm ceases to exist, and it becomes one with the Cosmic Mind. When the unit intellect arrives at this highest point, there is no doubt that it has become one with the Supreme Intellect. This state of pinnacled intellect is called karma sannyása. So wise people should be ever vigilant regarding karma sannyása…
My opinion is that it is proper for human beings to leave everything to Paramátmá, because those who are truly devotees know that Paramátmá (Supreme Subjectivity) is much wiser than they. This is what is known as prańipáta (surrender), and this is fulfilled by parábhakti (higher devotion). There is no alternative. Through parábhakti, karma sannyása is also possible; hence karma sannyása is only possible for those who have acquired parábhakti. Those who have attained parábhakti will be great men and women of action. The strength of their devotion will establish them in their action, and this will benefit the world. (“The Cardinal Spirit of Action and the Supreme Stance of Devotion,” Ananda Marga Ideology and Way of Life in a Nutshell Part 10)

KARMA SANNYASA

Acarya Nirrmeghananda, as per traditional yogic thought, wanted to get personal spiritual realization and then based on this realization do service to the world. Actually most such yogis after having spent years as spiritual capitalists devoted to their personal welfare, later spend little time in service to society. They certainly never take up Dharmayuddha (war to establish society based on spiritual righteousness). So this is a self-centred, individualistic approach. The primary goal of this approach is to satisfy oneself.

The spirit of our karma yoga is working to please the Lord. Baba wants to build a society based on collective labour, struggle and sacrifice. So disciples are enjoined to devote themselves to action as per the will of the Lord. This will lead to their developing devotion and spiritual realization. Baba gave the ideal of marte marte kám karo, kám karte karte maro [“Die while working, and work even while dying”]. Devotion is love in action and to work even while dying means one will die in the highest state of devotion. Thus the collective, united endeavours of many disciples will create a powerful force in the society. This will lead to dynamic transformation in the collective mind and society, either in the form of a wave of evolutionary change or revolution to create Dharmarajya (society based on the rule of sublime righteousness). So this is a God-centred approach, as it is based on first surrendering to the will of the Lord and then merging into the Lord.

In reality, one cannot attain the ultimate spiritual realisation without service to the world. Baba created a new approach blending devotion with service as an inseparable part of spirituality. This Neohumanistic mission of creating a genuinely benevolent society based on spirituality should not be abandoned. Even a highly developed yogi in the end, will have to take to the path of service and the years he spent without serving were essentially wasted.

Later in jail, Baba gave a very powerful message that was later published in the July 1978 issue of Crimson Dawn. It said:

KARMA SANNYASA

“I am Karma Brahma
I am Karma Brahma —
Only by action can you satisfy Me.
You can please Me only by actions.”

COMMUNAL FORCES OPPOSE ANANDA MARGA

The Sitamarhi DMC in mid September, 1968 was unique because it could not be held. It showed how acrimony with anti-ideological forces could lead to open war on the streets. Indeed, the year 1968 was a special year for me for it was then that I learnt why fight is necessary for the organization. I had heard there had been fighting between the margis and goondas during the construction of the Jamalpur jagriti.

It should be clear that Baba never compromised on the principle that Humanity is One, Human Society is One, Dharma is One and God is One. Hindu chauvinists, particularly the Rashtriya Swayamsevak Sangh (RSS), had not welcomed the removal of the sacred thread and tik (tail of hair at the back of the head) from Brahmins (Hindu priestly caste) who became part of Ananda Marga. Nor did they appreciate our revolutionary marriage (inter-caste, inter-religious and international marriages) system, and opening of English-medium schools by our organisation. The RSS feel that they are the protectors of Hindu Dharma. Completely ignorant of the real significance of yoga, tantra, bhakti, spirituality and Dharma, they had been and are still to some extent opposing Ananda Marga. Baba's firm condemnation of communalism only added fuel to the fire.

We were few workers in number when we assembled at Sitamarhi, a small but renowned town in Mithila, North Bihar. We had no inkling of possible events when RSS thugs surrounded us on the street.

As was the common practice, we were leisurely leading a procession down the streets. I was in front of the procession. The uniform in those days prescribed a trishula (trident) for nuns and a dagger and lathi (stout stick) for monks. First was myself, then came Acarya Cinmayananda, Acarya Shantatmananda in the front row with the margis following. Acarya Premananda and Acarya Ru-

COMMUNAL FORCES OPPOSE ANANDA MARGA

drananda were riding atop an elephant behind us all. It was a grand procession. It was a colourful programme that was very successful in attracting the public. The cries Ananda Marga Amar Hai (Ananda Marga is Immortal), Manav Manav Ek Hai (All Human Beings are One), Manav Dharma Ek Hai (Human Dharma is One) filled the air of the normally quiet town as we moved through the main bazaar. The public was standing on both sides of the procession. Some persons armed with lathis (staffs) in the crowd started to raise slogans against Ananda Marga, saying "Death to Ananda Marga". I started shouting loudly Ananda Marga Amar Hai. Finally when they did not stop, I took my stick and hit one of them. And thus, a struggle broke out as Baba's children — though outnumbered — fought for Baba's honour and Baba's Ananda Marga. Then the police came and promulgated a curfew order and thus DMC could not be held. They asked workers and margis to board the buses and thus we left for Muzaffarpur, a nearby town. All of us later found out that this disturbance was the handiwork of Hindu fundamentalists. When we reached there, we found Baba was already there, as He had been sent earlier.

Baba asked, "Who was the first to hit?"

Shantatmanandji replied, "Baba, Vandanananda was the first."

Baba then with a tone of strong appreciation gestured with His hands and said, "Very good, very good. Atatayii should be treated like that. Move ahead and what will happen? They will suffer the agony and pain of defeat."

COMMUNAL FORCES OPPOSE ANANDA MARGA

In A Guide to Human Conduct, Baba explained atatyayii saying,

Kśetradárápahárii ca shastradhárii dhanápaháh
Agnidagaradashcaeva śadete hyátatáyinah.
"Anyone who, by the use of brute force, wants to take possession of your property, abducts your wife, comes with a weapon to murder you, wants to snatch away your wealth, sets fire to your house or wants to take life by administering poison, is called an átatáyii."

We fully enjoyed the joyous presence and hearty approval of our Baba. Even other DMCs faced attacks by other dogmatic organizations such as the Arya Samaj. In the Ananda Marga archives there is a document in Baba's own handwriting that states that communalism (religious extremism) must be unequivocally condemned whenever it raises its serpentine head. Baba's final discourse on October 20, 1990 said in part,

COMMUNAL FORCES OPPOSE ANANDA MARGA

Communalism is unnatural. Again today, the foolish leaders, the political leaders, are encouraging this very sentiment. And I fear that if it is not controlled in the proper time — it is the most opportune moment to control it — if it is not controlled in the proper time, the country will face further disintegration... (At the time of the Communal Award in 1922) Most of the minorities could not rely on the Hindu majority of the country. That is why — out of fear complex — they wanted to divide the country. The Hindu minorities of Bengal and the Punjab could not rely on the Muslim majority. That is why they wanted the partition of those two provinces. The same psychology is prevalent today. At that time there was not even a wee bit of political education or political consciousness in the country... Serpents are exhaling venom everywhere... It is the right moment. It is the proper moment. It is the most opportune moment.
Náginiira cáridike fushiteche bishakta nishvas
Shantir lalit vani shonaibe bartha parihas
Bidáy nebar belá tai dák diye jai
Danaver sathe járá samgrámer tare prastut hateche ghare ghare.
"Serpents are exhaling venom everywhere. The sweet gospels of peace sound like empty mockery. That is why on the eve of my departure from this world, I send out a clarion call to those who are preparing in every house to fight against the demons in human form."
Serpents are exhaling venom everywhere. Now at this critical juncture, should we go on preaching the gospels of peace? No, no, no, no! So, before my departure from the world, said Rabindranath, I have made the necessary prep-

arations for the fight against these demons. Do you all follow? What Rabindranath said sixty years ago is also true in this last portion of the twentieth century." ("The Dangers of Communalism", Prout in a Nutshell Part 18)

DIGNITY OF HIS ORGANISATION

During one seminar period in Himachal Pradesh, Acarya Nirmalananda came as central trainer. The renowned Indian Institute for Advanced Studies in Simla was under the control of the university in Chandigarh (Punjab). The dean of the Institute was from Bengal. When I heard about him, I made an appointment and went to see him with Nirmalananda dada. Although the dean did not take initiation, the meeting was much appreciated by him.

When I mentioned this meeting to Baba, He became angry and said, "Why our trainer from the Centre went to meet him? The position of our central trainer is very high. He should come to meet our trainer." Thus Baba wanted to maintain the dignity of the organization blessed by His divine mission to save humanity.

The head trainer of a training centre is called a shramańa. This word comes from the verb shram, which means "to exert, strive, struggle or undergo austerities." A trainer is a labourer who teaches others how to be a labourer for the welfare of all beings in the social, economic, political, cultural, ideological and spiritual realms. Historically this word was used for non-Vedic spiritual traditions based on spiritual experience more than rituals and scriptures. One meaning of this word for trainer is one who carries out the shamita (destruction, subduing or control) of all pápa (vices, sins) both within oneself, within one's spiritual order and within one's society or samaja. The other meaning of this word is someone who performs tapasya through great struggle. Tapasya means to willingly undergo hardship, suffering and persecution in order to liberate suffering beings in the society. We can understand why Baba used this term for the trainer of His acaryas when we read the following passage,

All the psychological diseases of humanity are the result

DIGNITY OF HIS ORGANISATION

of not having bhúmadrśti (cosmic outlook) in life. The diseased persons do not consider others, but think only about themselves. They are busy thinking about their own families, employers, etc., and they forget everyone else. This is a terrible malady. But it is a mere expression and symptom of narrow-mindedness, the real root and reason for psychological disease. The cure is to reverse the trend of behaviour to rid the fallen human beings of narrow-mindedness. The medicine is one and only one: Brahmabháva [ideation on Supreme Consciousness]. But just adopting the supreme goal does not immediately establish one in that. It is the work of the ácárya to recognize if a person is on the right or wrong path and course of action. The ácárya should give direction and guidance in every work, big or small. When he or she is to impart any duty to anyone, he should be strict, strong and exact, like a diamond. He or she should never consider social position, wealth, rank, etc. Only those who are established in Yama and Niyama [moral code] and practise sádhaná can be given any responsibility.

Remember, Ananda Marga is a man-making mission. Both quantity and quality are important. Everyone should be free to join Ananda Marga, to take part in social functions, etc., but only those established in Yama and Niyama may be given any position to work in society. Just to satisfy someone and compromise [with] sin is unthinkable. We can in no case ever compromise with injustice. An employer and a worker may compromise after a struggle. But Ananda Marga is fighting for Satya (Absolute Truth) and unless and until we get total victory in both individual and collective life we must not stop the struggle. Compromising with injustice during battle is asatya [untruth]. Achieving only seventy-five percent Satya and twenty-five percent asatya is no victory. Quinine suppresses the symptoms of malaria, while the disease still remains in the blood. But the disease must be destroyed. Therefore, until you banish asatya you shall not stop your battle.

DIGNITY OF HIS ORGANISATION

Society is the collective name for those who want to move ahead together. The struggle against sin must be waged individually as well as in a well-unified way in collective social life. There is a story in the Márkańďeya Puráńa. In it, the demons defeated each of the gods individually, but then the gods united all their individual strengths and formed a tremendous collective force, thereby routing and destroying the demons. It is a very good story and there can be practical application of its meaning in every collective endeavour. It is the duty of an ácárya to develop this great collective force of society by awakening the masses to unite and destroy the evil, demoniac forces existing in society. The struggle between good and evil forces goes on. Sound an urgent clarion call in this battlefield of life. Ácáryas must give such inspiration to the people. You know, a son must remove the debt of his deceased father — he is morally bound to do so. Similarly, you are to purify society by purging it of sin. It is more than an obligation, it is your bounden duty. You must do this or total destruction is inevitable. To take leadership in society you must be established in Yama and Niyama in just such a strict way. (The Conduct of an Acarya)

KAJAHILL

It was 1967 and I was posted in Himachal Pradesh where I had been since 1964. As mentioned previously, Himachal Pradesh was a union territory (directly controlled by central government) at that time, and in those days there was a movement within the state of Punjab to bifurcate it into two parts — Haryana and Punjab. The regions dominated by the adherents of the Sikh religion of Guru Nanak and Guru Gobind Singh believed they should have a separate homeland. During the Partition of India, Punjab was divided between India and Pakistan. In West Punjab, Muslims carried out massacres of Sikhs and Hindus and in East Punjab, Sikhs and Hindus carried out massacres of Muslims. Twenty years later, the Sikhs felt that, like Pakistan, they should have their own religious state (Khalistan) rather than live under Hindu domination. This programme started covertly with a movement to divide the Punjab state into two parts — one Punjabi (Sikh) and the other Haryanvi (Hindu). Clearly the disease of religious nationalism (communalism) that divided Punjab in 1947 was still infecting the collective psychology two decades later. The Hindus also demanded a separate state of Haryana and religious sentiment took a dangerous turn there also.

Kangra, Kullu, Lahaul Spiti, and Simla District were in Punjab but were culturally and topographically the same as the union territory of Himachal Pradesh. The spreading movement in Punjab and Haryana provided an opportunity for ambitious leaders to demand a full-fledged state of Himachal including the areas then in Punjab, like Shimla, Kullu, Lahaul Spiti and Kangra.

During one RDS, dearest Baba called Acarya Cinmayananda, Acarya Parashnathji and myself. They were posted in the state of Jammu & Kashmir. Baba told us to immediately go and communicate to the people of J & K and Himachal Pradesh His proposal.

KAJAHILL

The sad part about J & K was that the Indian leaders had accepted a dual-rule system in J & K. Two flags are hoisted together in the state representing two separate territories, without any common sentiment of one country and one people. All states of the entire country must have the constitutional right to common privileges and powers. To give special privileges of economic and cultural autonomy to three states (J & K, Manipur and Nagaland) causes resentment and hostility. So either all states of India should have the same privileges as that of J & K — of having their own constitution and freedom from the economic control of outsiders — or none should.

The basic stance of PROUT is that all states should enjoy economic rights and freedom from the exploitation of outside capitalists. J & K had been under a puppet government for some time and in the 1960s, in reaction to the Hindu politicians demanding full-scale annexation of Kashmir by the central government, Muslim extremism had begun to rise, and along with that, movements began to try to merge Kashmir with Pakistan based on this religious sentiment. In addition, the puppet government had removed the Kashmiri language from schools — students were taught Urdu. This weakened the Tantric and Sufi culture of Kashmir or Kashmiriyat.

So this was a time of all-round religious sentiment. I should mention here that when I visited Kashmir in 1973, I could feel and the people also said that Kashmir was not a part of India. I felt quite uncomfortable with the mindset of the people there.

Our Baba said, "You immediately go and tell all concerned that the people of Himachal, Laddakh, Jammu and Kashmir should raise their voices to form one larger state." Baba gave the name of the new state as KAJAHILL ($\text{K} = \text{Kashmir}$, $\text{J} = \text{Jammu}$, $\text{H} = \text{Himachal Pradesh}$, $\text{L} = \text{Laddakh}$).

KAJAHILL

Baba noted, "The bigger state will have a larger number of non-Muslim MLAs (member of state legislature). With an overwhelming majority they can then pass a resolution to abolish the special status of J & K." Baba said that each socio-economic unit of KAJAHILL — Dogri, Laddakh, Kashmir, Srimaor, Pahari, Kinnaur — should have economic independence from outside exploiters and should work to become economically self-reliant. Baba said that this economic sovereignty for each samaja (a socio-economic and cultural bioregion) would strengthen the solidarity of the region and the country. Baba told us to contact all the leaders, prominent personalities and the common people to form one state — KAJAHILL. Baba was so positive and caring for the welfare of India that he said that once the special status of Jammu & Kashmir is removed, Rajputs and other warrior communities should be encouraged to buy land along the border with Pakistan. This would help solve the problem of infiltration of militants from Pakistan.

In addition, Baba stressed that we should open schools all along the Pakistani border on a priority basis, in Jammu, Kathua, Ferozpur, Amritsar, Bathinda, Sangrur, Rajouri and Kashmir, to educate people in this line of thought. Although as a rule, we were to first cover other areas of the dioceses of the country, but keeping in mind the danger of infiltration, Baba gave priority to the districts along the Pakistan border.

We came out of the meeting in a state of wonder at this amazing new idea of Baba. I met some persons in Himachal, including Hindu politicians. They could not comprehend the dimensions of this idea and declared that it was a big joke. Even today they say it is impractical. In reality, they were guided by their own vested interests. The root reason for their desire for ending the economic independence of J & K, is that as agents of the capitalists they wanted to open J & K to the capitalist wolves to devour and exploit. They had no interest in economic sovereignty either in Himachal or elsewhere. Had they accepted Baba's proposal, the problem of Kashmir would have been solved forever.

KAJAHILL

Much later, the issue of Khalistan, the proposed separate Sikh religious state of Punjab, became a strong movement among the youth and politicians. Their aspiration to be a new, separate nation like Pakistan and Afghanistan posed a big challenge to the Indian government. Baba then gave a wonderful strategy to handle the situation. Some of the points Baba gave were:

  1. Much before the issue of a separate nation was raised, Baba had advocated the concept of samaja. A samaja is a social,

economic, cultural and ecological region with its own distinct identity. Baba gave the mission of Asi Punjabi as being to unite West Punjab (in Pakistan) and East Punjab (in India) to form one samaja. The glorious culture, language, food habits, heritage, and spirituality are the same for both. The name Punjab means the "land of the five rivers" and the Sindh, Jhelum, Ravi, Sutlej and Beas Rivers are the basis of Punjabi civilization.

  1. If Punjab, already divided once, would be divided once again on the basis of religious sentiments, it would cause tremendous harm to the region.

KAJAHILL

  1. Baba said that there should be a campaign in Punjab to make people realize that it will not be advisable to demand a separate nation on the basis of religion for the following reasons:

(a) It has no mineral resources of its own. The raw materials that are used in the hosiery and steel-based industries (such as cycles, motorcycles) come from other parts of India. If independence is granted as a separate nation what will happen to those industries?

(b) Punjab has no access to the sea. Hence for trading it has to depend either upon India or Pakistan. This will further lead to subjugation. Furthermore all the agricultural produce of Punjab would no longer be sold in India cheaply as they would face import duties from India.

(c) As a nation, Punjab would have no electricity because the Nangal Dam is in Himachal Pradesh. If Punjab were to leave India, then India would divert the waters from that dam to the River Yamuna by canals. However if Punjab remains part of India, there is a lot of potential in Himachal Pradesh to generate electricity and if that is harnessed it can provide electricity to the whole area.

KAJAHILL

Fundamentally all extremists, whether Hindu, Muslim or Sikh, had one common interest only — to aid and accelerate the exploitation of the capitalists.

The same is the case today. Today's religious extremism has exploded due to the vacuum created by the demise of communism. Throughout His life from childhood to His last discourse, Baba, uncompromisingly waged ideological warfare against these religious agents of capitalist exploiters. As Baba explained:

In most cases religions took advantage of human weaknesses, crippled the people's rationality, and sapped their strength, thus facilitating their exploitation by the religious traders. When the vitality and strength of the masses remains unexpressed, the exploiters will be able to exploit them in the economic and other spheres of life for a long time with relative ease. In the annals of human history it can be seen in all ages that the various exploiters were the patrons and the preachers of religion.
Dharma is altogether different from religion for it teaches human beings to advance while mercilessly smashing all obstacles that thwart human progress. Its inherent qualities are subtle analysis, ideological strength and the brilliance of valour. That's why the vested interests cannot tolerate it and use all their strength and machinery to oppose it. They claim they are protecting religion. The greater the conflict between person and person, between community and community, between state and state, the greater is their advantage. But when many people form a collective mind through sincere sacrifice and ardent practice while advancing towards the realization of oneness with the Supreme, it will be difficult to continue exploiting people on any pretext.
In fact the ignorance of Brahmaváda (intuitional science) and dharma has done enormous harm to the human race. In the modern civilized society the same thing should not be allowed to be repeated. By accepting the all-pervasive and all-permeating Brahma (the Great) as the primordial factor for the crude, subtle and causal worlds; by realizing Brahma as the nucleus of the enjoyer, the enjoyed and the enjoyment; and by keeping the Supreme Cognition fixed before the mind, human life should be rebuilt on a strong foundation. All spheres of life should be completely restructured. All feelings of differentiation — his god, her god, the Hindu's

KAJAHILL

Hari and the Muslim's Álláh — should be abandoned and that Supreme Entity accepted from the core of one's heart. That Supreme Imperishable Entity is the final desideratum, the culminating point of all entities. We should not bother the least who addresses Him as Hari or who addresses Him as Álláh. ("Form and Formless", Subháśita Saḿgraha Part 5)

TRANSFER TO BIHAR

Baba introduced a system whereby all workers were required to have the experience of all the wings of Ananda Marga, such as ERAWS (Education, Relief and Welfare Section), PFI (Proutist Forum of India), SDM (Seva Dharma Mission) and VSS (Volunteer Social Service). Since I became a worker in Himachal Pradesh, I had been a worker of SDM.

Now, I was asked to go to Bhagalpur in Bihar and work as an editor of the Anga Duta magazine of Angika Samaj. That was an opportunity for me to learn about PROUT. I stayed with Shrii Naval Kishore, the main Proutist in Bhagalpur. He was the elder brother of Acarya Prakashananda. When I had just begun this work, I was reposted as DS (diocese secretary) of Patna. I stayed in the school there that had only a few children. After being there for around twenty days, I was posted as CC (circle commander) in VSS with my headquarters in Ranchi. That was a great opportunity for me to spend time with Baba who was also there. Thus within two months I was transferred to these different wings of Ananda Marga and gained experience of how they function.

During this period of more than two and a half years, I had a great chance to work with Baba and understand His mission a little. So many times Baba called me into His room — in the jagriti or His quarters — and blessed me in special ways. 1969 was Sadhana Year in which Baba gave so many spiritual demonstrations every day. In this way Baba inspired the margis to do longer sadhana. At that time, Baba was performing many demonstrations of various samadhis. Anindananda samadhi, raganuga samadhi, tanmatrka samadhi and dharmamegha samadhis were performed upon me. Once Baba demonstrated parakaya pravesha (entry into someone's body) by touching my ajina cakra (between the eyebrows) and asking me to enter into another man's body through that man's

manipura cakra. Baba asked me to see the colour of the man's chest and mind. I saw that the man had TB and reported this. Then Baba questioned the man and his symptoms were revealed. Then Baba touched his chest with His stick and cured him and prescribed some food and asanas.

TRANSFER TO BIHAR

The most important demonstration was on extra-cerebral memory. It revealed my previous lives.

I was sitting in meditation and Baba asked, "What do you see?"

"I see You Baba."

Baba asked me, "Go back in your previous life. What do you see?"

TRANSFER TO BIHAR

"I see You, Baba," I said. I saw nothing but Him in His present form of Lord Anandamurti.

Then Baba said, "Go back into the life before that. What do you see?"

"I see You, Baba," as I saw nothing but Him.

Baba said that I was sannyasi in both lives. However, Baba said to the margis, as I was told later, that in those lives I had an ordinary (madhyama) guru. However the ultimate reality of those lives was nothing but Baba, as that is all I saw. Also somehow whichever guru I may have had, somehow it was connected with Baba.

TRANSFER TO BIHAR

After the demonstration was over, I heard Baba saying, "In those previous lives he attained the last stage of spiritual realization to merge in Parama Purusa but he was very selfish. He didn't do social service. He was doing sadhana for his personal salvation and that is why he has taken birth — to do maximum service."

Baba later termed such yogis as spiritual capitalists. Baba explained that this selfishness prevents the attainment of equanimity, which is crucial for spiritual progress. Without it one's spiritual progress will be hindered at every step. Baba gave a clarion call to these spiritual capitalists saying,

Today there is an urgent need for an all-round spiritual revolution in individual and collective life under the leadership of a group of accomplished and idealistic leaders. These leaders should be endowed with spiritual power, and their ideology should be based on the solid rock of spirituality. Such highly qualified leaders are called sadvipras. They will ensure social progress in all countries in all ages.
All genuine spiritualists will have to adjust with the level of the dusty earth inspired by the spontaneous love of their hearts. They will have to share the wealth of their developed intellects with others to lighten the sorrows and sufferings of humanity. Through their guidance and leadership, human thinking will take a new turn and move along an entirely new path. The latent spiritual power in human beings will be awakened. Through their effort and inspiration, the new people of a new generation will be armed with a bold new optimism and vision of the future, and march forward triumphantly. ("Capitalism in Three Spheres")

DIFFERENCE BETWEEN PROUT AND COMMUNISM

In 1968 Baba was living in Ranchi. He used to go for morning and evening walk regularly. One day during evening walk, Baba suddenly stopped and asked, "What is the practical difference between Prout and communism?"

After getting no answer, He replied, "Communism is successful in scarcity. Where people are getting four rotis (flat bread), then they will snatch two rotis so that people will move towards communism. Its objective is to create scarcity. Where people are happy and wealthy, there, communism will not get birth. This means snatching others' happiness and wealth is the basic principle of communism so that people are motivated to accept it."

Actually the communists used to burn the rice paddy of the landowners. Then there would be a shortage of food. Talking of the scarcity, the communists would try to incite the farm labourers to revolt and join their party. Similarly, they used to deliberately shut down industries by strikes, which would cause unemployment. Taking advantage of the penury of the workers, they would instigate them to join the union of their party.

Baba then said, "So what will PROUT do? Where people are getting four breads it will increase the number and quality of bread. It will add butter and cream to it and increase their quantity also. It means from four breads to make it five or six. It will increase their amenities and make their lives more joyful. So people will immediately accept PROUT."

DIFFERENCE BETWEEN PROUT AND COMMUNISM

PROUT will create movements to guarantee the minimum requirements like bread and also provide the maximum possible amount of amenities like butter. These movements for food democracy will

attract people to PROUT. PROUT sponsors labour movements to transform industries into cooperatives.

Once in 1969 a communist MP from West Bengal raised questions in Parliament about Ananda Marga. This CPI(M) representative was concerned that a sizeable number of young boys and girls were joining Ananda Marga. Then the home minister, Mr. Pant, said, "Ananda Marga should talk about Dharma but should not interfere in politics. Dharma and politics will not go hand in hand."

When Baba was told about this, then Baba explicitly stated, "Then adharmic people will rule the country! What will happen to society and how will the country function?" Lord Krsna also united Dharma and politics to create the mission of Dharmarashtra (government of the moralists). Baba alone has taken this mission seriously.

LIKE SON, LIKE FATHER

It was sometime in Ranchi, maybe in the year of 1968-9. As I was the CC of Patna and the state of Bihar, most of the time I was blessed to be with our dear Baba. Once in the morning, I accompanied Baba for field walk. Shrii Rajender Kumar Asthana, the brother of V.K. Asthana (a prominent margi at that time) was also there with me. Kumarda had one leg shorter than the other in length and hence used to limp while walking.

To my surprise, I noticed Baba walking in the same style as Mr Asthana — that is, limping. Asthanada was so engrossed in talking with Baba, that he did not notice. I noticed because I was walking behind them.

Still today, I am not in a position to know, why Baba was walking in that limping way. Was Baba taking on some samskaras? Was Baba so full of loving closeness with Asthanaji that He felt Asthanaji's ailment as His own?

Baba explained that each created being has a special connection with Him known as Ota Yoga. However, Baba said (in "Intuitional Science of the Vedas Part 5") that when one is initiated and becomes a devotee one has more subtle connections with Him. The first is Anujinátá Yoga, or wherein by the practice of ideating on Him in worldly life (brahmacarya) one attains a blissful union with Him. The second is Anujiṋá Yoga or the deeper union one finds in dhyana. In all these ways one becomes very close to Him and then He becomes more and more able to merge with the disciple. Then He will take upon Himself more and more of their propensities, joys, sorrow, hopes and aspirations.

LIKE SON, LIKE FATHER

His radiance is being refracted through the veins and venules, molecules and atoms of every evolved object. Treating every microcosmic tendency as His own object for the

good of the world, He has been afflicting Himself day in and day out with the painful affliction of their lives — suffering for them, and His diverse, manifested organisms are bowing down in reverence to His glory. ("Desire and Detachment," Subhás'ita Sam'graha Part 3)

UNDERSTANDING BABA’S PHYSICAL BODY

This reminiscence is from when I was worker at the state level in Bihar. By the grace of Baba, I was always blessed with the opportunity to live near Him. I got the opportunity to be all alone with Him for more than two or three hours. I was endlessly thrilled for nearly three years with His selfless benevolence and His resplendent tenderness. But these incidents of mine are personal and so my lips remained sealed. I am sorry that it will remain unpublished. Let His songs speak for me:

ANTARÁTMÁ ANTARERI,
NIKHIL PRIITIR KANDA
JÁBO ÁBÁR ÁSABO ÁMI,
TOMÁR CARAŃA REŃU CÚMI (4547)
You are the innermost soul, deep within
You are the foundation and support of all love
I go and again I come
Kissing the dust of Your feet

Due to much proximity with Baba, I started giving more importance to His physical body without really realizing its spiritual status. I took Baba’s body to be the physical body of someone I loved dearly. I was more interested in visiting the jagriti, the office, Baba’s quarters, wherever dearest Baba was present, rather than in taking interest in work or sadhana. I felt so much happier in His physical presence. Baba often used to send me within Ranchi and outside in Bihar by assigning me some work. I would go to a seminar and other work but my soul was always in Ranchi and I was always restless and eager for His physical darshan (contact). Sometimes I was completely lost in meditation. I do not know what to say of this situation. But my soul was so blissful.

UNDERSTANDING BABA’S PHYSICAL BODY

One day when I returned to Ranchi from Saharsa, Baba called me and told to sit in front of him and do sadhana. While I was doing sadhana, He touched all my eight cakras and kept his hand on my sahasrara cakra and murmured something I was unable to comprehend.

Pointing to His body, He told me three times, "Baba is not this body."

Then He said, "This body will not remain forever. If you want to love Baba then do it here." Then He touched my anahata cakra (heart), ajina cakra (between the eyebrows) and sahasrara cakra (crown of head). He had used very similar words in his room during the Mumbai DMC.

Here I want to clarify one thing that whenever there was a DMC, I used to sit in the front row. For this I used to go there very early. However, after Baba told me such things I used to sit in the last row. I deeply enjoyed Baba's darshan even more than when I was physically close to Him. I stopped running after Baba's car. Even during RDS reporting I used to sit in the last row and this continued till the last. But I did not feel any decrease in my bliss; rather it increased. Many people did not feel good about this habit of mine. They used to ridicule me. I never paid heed to such things and always sat in the last row and was filled with joy.

UNDERSTANDING BABA’S PHYSICAL BODY

Baba said in jail (see August 1974 issue of Crimson Dawn magazine):

"If you want to see Me, do My Mission
Because I am merged with My Mission.
I am not this physical body,
This physical body is not Me.
I am within Your heart and
You are within My heart.
Devotion alone
Can demand
My physical presence."

This mysterious quote seems to contradict itself because on the one hand it is saying that Baba's physical body is not who He is and at the same time it says that only devotion can demand His physical presence.

Earlier, when I was closer to Baba physically, I enjoyed Baba more physically. By elevating my mind by sadhana I learned to see the spiritual effulgence of Baba's physical form. The purpose of seeing in sadhana the absolute effulgence of His Paramatma-rup (form of the Supreme Self) is to realize how this same effulgence can be realised within His physical form. This is why Baba told me to go deeper to the psychic and spiritual level. When one realizes this, one will feel irresistible attraction to Baba's physical form. Yogis spend lifetimes seeing the inner form of the Lord in dhyana before they are graced with the darshan of His physical body. With folded hands I ask Baba to bless the devotees of the future with the darshan of His physical body.

BRAHMAEVA GURUREKAH NÁPARAH

Baba was inevitably the heart of every gathering. So many times His jokes convulsed us with laughter until tears came. Whenever the situation was tense, He would either make a joke or He would become more serious and perform a demonstration of spiritual bliss (samadhi). But while His demonstrations and talks were often beyond our capacity to absorb, His jokes were readily understood. In 1969, He used to come to the Ranchi jagriti every morning and evening. There was a special DC (deputy commander) who was in charge of His security. He was under VSS and was supposed to arrange volunteers for Baba's security and to accompany Him during field walk, General Darshan, etc.

Acarya Shyamananda from Punjab was the DC. Once there was an evening General Darshan attended by margis and dadas. Baba came and sat upon the dias and surveyed the minds of the assembled devotees. Shyamananda Dada was very stressed in discharging his responsibilities due to the pressure of obtaining staff and money. In addition he had a prickly personality that embroiled him in many unnecessary arguments.

Baba called this dada who was standing nearby and asked him to explain the meaning of Sutra 3.9 of our philosophical scripture, Ananda Sutram.

The sutra is, Brahmaeva Gururekah náparah (Brahma alone is the guru, no one else.)

BRAHMAEVA GURUREKAH NÁPARAH

Dada said in Hindi, "Brahma ek Guru hai na pahra." Dada had confused the Sanskrit word "parah" meaning "no one else" with the Punjabi word "Pahra" which meant "security." Immediately Baba took over the talk.

Baba said, "Look here, he is the security-in-charge and what he

says is, 'Brahmahi ekah Guru hai to pahra dene ki jururat nahi hai.'" (Brahma alone is the Guru and requires no security personnel.) Upon hearing this, everyone exploded with laughter. Dadaji could not understand what was happening and became visibly perplexed. Then Baba said, "All right! You are in VSS, yes, yes". Then Baba made a gesture with His hand to indicate that VSS doesn't need intellect. In this way Baba always created drama that left us laughing to lift our spirits.

PERMANENT ADDRESS

Once Baba called a boy forward and asked him, "Where do you live?"

The boy gave the name of a place in Bengal.

Baba then called me forward and asked me to sit in meditation. Then Baba touched my ajina cakra (between the eyebrows) and my sahasrara cakra (crown of the head). He asked me to go one hundred years back and see the past life of that boy.

I told Baba that I saw him in some place. Then Baba asked me if I could read any signboard there to find the name of that place. My mind found a signboard and I told the name of the railway station to Baba. Then Baba said that this was a place in Uttar Pradesh – i.e. the Hindi belt.

PERMANENT ADDRESS
