Baba then asked me to go one hundred years back and see where this boy was. I saw a man with a Tibetan prayer wheel. Then Baba said, "Look here, this boy was there in Tibet 150 years back."

Then Baba pointed to the boy and said, "Oh my little boy, what is your dress? Then, what is your address? What is your permanent address?"

Baba explained, "Now you are in Bengal. A hundred years back you were in Uttar Pradesh. One hundred and fifty years back, you were in Tibet. So you have no dress, no address, no permanent address. So then what is your permanent address? You can say, 'Care of (c/o) Parama Purusa' because He is here with you in Bengal, He was there with you in Uttar Pradesh and He was also present with you in Tibet. So your permanent address is Parama Purusa."

says is, 'Brahmahi ekah Guru hai to pahra dene ki jururat nahi hai." (Brahma alone is the Guru and requires no security personnel.) Upon hearing this, everyone exploded with laughter. Dadaji could not understand what was happening and became visibly perplexed. Then Baba said, "All right! You are in VSS, yes, yes". Then Baba made a gesture with His hand to indicate that VSS doesn't need intellect. In this way Baba always created drama that left us laughing to lift our spirits.

PERMANENT ADDRESS

Once Baba called a boy forward and asked him, "Where do you live?"

The boy gave the name of a place in Bengal.

Baba then called me forward and asked me to sit in meditation. Then Baba touched my ajina cakra (between the eyebrows) and my sahasrara cakra (crown of the head). He asked me to go one hundred years back and see the past life of that boy.

I told Baba that I saw him in some place. Then Baba asked me if I could read any signboard there to find the name of that place. My mind found a signboard and I told the name of the railway station to Baba. Then Baba said that this was a place in Uttar Pradesh – i.e. the Hindi belt.

PERMANENT ADDRESS

Baba then asked me to go one hundred years back and see where this boy was. I saw a man with a Tibetan prayer wheel. Then Baba said, "Look here, this boy was there in Tibet 150 years back."

Then Baba pointed to the boy and said, "Oh my little boy, what is your dress? Then, what is your address? What is your permanent address?"

Baba explained, "Now you are in Bengal. A hundred years back you were in Uttar Pradesh. One hundred and fifty years back, you were in Tibet. So you have no dress, no address, no permanent address. So then what is your permanent address? You can say, 'Care of (c/o) Parama Purusa' because He is here with you in Bengal, He was there with you in Uttar Pradesh and He was also present with you in Tibet. So your permanent address is Parama Purusa."

WEARING OUR HEARTS ON OUR CHEEKS

When Baba was staying in Ranchi, we had one room and a big verandah in front of Baba's quarters. It was rented. Baba was also staying in a rented building.

As a rule, the volunteers of VSS used to serve as a guard of honour to Baba when He went out and when He would enter the building. Once, while coming back, He asked the driver to stop the car in front of the gate of His quarters.

I was on the verandah and rushed to Him. Baba alighted from the car and started to inspect the uniformed volunteers standing in a row.

He went to each one of them and scrutinized everyone's face with attention. Baba then turned to me and said,

WEARING OUR HEARTS ON OUR CHEEKS

"That person with fair and smooth cheeks, you should understand that he is lazy."

What Baba was trying to say was that a soldier who was performing all his duties full-heartedly with all her/his strength would have red cheeks due to the strenuous exercise. And the soldier who was merely doing the minimum and mechanically performing duties would have fair or smooth cheeks. For devotees serving the suffering humanity in the form of Baba, more than 100% effort is appreciable. If it is less than 100%, it is completely intolerable. Baba has emphasized this time and again.

With cosmic ideation, set out from the starting point of moralism and advance towards supreme realization. Your feet may bleed, cut by the thorns scattered on the road; the sky, rent asunder by the lightning and crashing thunder of the fearful storm, may fall on your head, but proceed you must. You are a born fighter. To flee the battle in fear and hide like

a corpse in the hills is ultravirous to your existential vitality. You must advance towards the Supreme Entity, your original abode, smashing all obstacles on the path. From time immemorial you have been listening to the sweetness of His divine call. Can you remain oblivious to it, engrossed in your little world created by Máyá (force of illusion)? ("Microcosm and Macrocosm," Subhásita Saḿgraha Part 5)

DHARMA IS AN ORDER ITSELF

Parama Purusa, the Supreme Consciousness, is an embodiment of love, Dharma and bliss (anandam). And it is His special expression as Mahasambhuti Anandamurti that has always been the living example of Dharma, love and bliss. This story clearly reveals how He teaches us to firmly hold onto Dharma.

It is true that the communists, the CPI(M) in particular, never liked Ananda Marga. It had always opposed tooth and nail the spread of Ananda Marga. Since the province of West Bengal was the main base of the CPI(M), it was always trying to thwart the activities of Ananda Marga. It became known that when the headquarters of Ananda Marga was shifted to Ananda Nagar (Purulia District, West Bengal), the CPI(M) became scared. Our social service in one of the poorest regions of India, where illiteracy, poverty and malnutrition were rampant, opened a new chapter in the hearts of people. Pundag, once the haven of communist activity, became a centre of Ananda Marga. Young men and women joined Ananda Marga in good numbers from West Bengal. The reality is that Ananda Marga thrives on spirituality and positive solutions and communism thrives on illiteracy and penury.

The best course of action for the communists to adopt was to eliminate our leader, Baba. This is the practice of rulers all over the world and history bears witness to this. Lord Anandamurtijii, not Ananda Marga, was the threat to the CPI(M), particularly in Bengal. The sublime ideology, the selfless service rendered by Ananda Marga volunteers, the young band of dedicated workers and of course PROUT — Baba's radical programme for socio-economic revolution — were becoming an eyesore for the communists. The CPI(M) was looking for the best opportunity to materialize their design to eliminate Baba.

To camouflage their nefarious designs, they planned to create a

DHARMA IS AN ORDER ITSELF

public programme of propaganda against Ananda Marga and then try to justify their violence against the supposedly "dangerous" activities of Ananda Marga. It was not just Bengal, but everywhere that such malicious plots were tacitly practiced. The media under their influence would then depict these crimes as if they were the victim's fault. Thus the crimes against our organisation were justified by media saying that Ananda Marga invited the wrath of the outraged public.

On March 5, 1967 they tried to kill Baba with an army of goondas. As a result five dedicated workers were killed: Baba's PA, Acarya Abhedananda Avadhuta, Acarya Sacidananda Avadhuta, Bharat Kumar, Prabhas Kumar and Avadh Kumar. Then, when the workers went and told Baba what was happening, Baba came out of His quarters and stood in front of the armed mob. As He stared in silence, the mob began to retreat and gradually dispersed. The communists had intended to create a fear psychosis in the cadre of Ananda Marga but the death of their brothers, who were called dadhicis, only emboldened them. Commemorating this day became a permanent observance of the organization.

The communists however did not give up. Jyoti Basu, the communist home minister for Bengal, had always been vehemently against Ananda Marga. Under his rule, the communists began to be more determined to assassinate Baba.

During the August 28, 1969 DMC in Cooch Behar (North Bengal) — a strong base of the CPI(M) — the margis, volunteers and workers were attacked and one margi named Shrii Ravi Sarkar was killed. Revered Baba was arrested and there was a public outcry. When He was released on September 2, He came to Patna. As the incharge of the VSS activists, I received Him at the airport.

DHARMA IS AN ORDER ITSELF

When Beloved Baba entered His room in His Pataliputra quarters, I followed Him. After I performed sastaunga pranama (full prostration) and stood up, He asked me, "You know, I was arrested by atatyayii (highly inimical people) in Cooch Behar. One of the margis (Ravi Sarkar) was killed."

I said, "Yes Baba."

"How did you react to that?" Baba asked very seriously. I told Baba that I did not like this.

"Is that all! You did not feel good."

DHARMA IS AN ORDER ITSELF

Then I asked, "What should I have done, Baba?"

"You should have organised lakhs of moralists from Bihar to march to Cooch Behar to teach a lesson to these atatyayiis. They have committed injustice and crimes against Dharma and Dharma Guru."

Though it was foolish and shameful on my part, had I been ordered to do this, I would not have carried out such orders for it was a tremendous task to organize so many people to march all the way to North Bengal. Seeking for an excuse to weasel out of my responsibility, I told Baba that there was no such order for this programme issued from anyone (i.e. higher organizational authority).

Upon hearing this foolish statement of mine, dear Baba simply said,

DHARMA IS AN ORDER ITSELF

"Dharma needs no one's instruction (order). Dharma is an order in itself."

Dharma rakśati rakśitah — "One who protects Dharma is protected by Dharma."

DUTY TO GIVE THANKS

In 1970 Baba started the seminar system. Baba introduced seminar programmes covering all areas down to the village level, especially in eastern India. This created a strong ideological wave among the most humble people in some of the poorest states. Baba emphasised that all disciples must have an understanding of the spiritual and socio-economic philosophy of Ananda Marga. Until there was a clear understanding, doubts would persist in their mind. Without a clear discussion, the implications and dimensions of different aspects of the philosophy could not be understood and therefore could not be implemented in the society.

Every margi must have an organisational responsibility. They were known as optees of one particular branch of Ananda Marga. Baba explained that when a person has taken the responsibility to serve society, they opt or willingly undertake social service—they became optees. Optees who attended higher level seminars were given the responsibility to carry our seminars to lower levels such as in remote villages. By becoming teachers of the ideology they were forced to learn the ideology inside and out and to meditate on the deeper meanings so as to interest and enlighten the audience. The slogan was "Seminar, seminar, seminar — more and more seminar — village level seminar."

I was posted in the VSS Department. Ac. Citeshananda Avt. was the PS (provincial secretary) and the Acarya Dineshvaranandji (martyr or dadhici in 1973) was the CO. Baba gave a lot of importance to carrying out seminars and explained in detail the system by which it was to be carried out at local, block and diocese levels.

When there was a seminar in Gumla, Baba asked the three of us to go there and declared that the best of the cadre among the seminar participants should be given to the Prout department. Accordingly we divided the participants into four groups. Twenty-five

DUTY TO GIVE THANKS

percent of the best participants were declared to be in the Prout department. When we got back to Ranchi and gave the news to Baba, He was most unhappy. He said that out of the one hundred participants, the best one should have gone to Prout, the second best to another, the third best to still another and so on. We were then asked to go back to Gumla the very next day and select the seminar trainers and their departmental duties in this manner. Baba was very particular about this system and the rules governing it. This incident happened during the time period when the seminar system was first introduced. In the next seminar session another department was emphasized instead of PROUT.

DUTY TO GIVE THANKS

Once on a Wednesday morning, in the central jagriti, Baba asked me to go to Saharsa immediately. I did sastaunga pranama to Baba and left for the station. When I arrived at the train station, I found out that there were no trains to Katihar or Saharsa. Then I went to the bus station and I was told that there was a bus that went directly to Purnea and that this bus would leave at 7 p.m. I came back to the jagriti to join Baba's evening darshan at 5 p.m. before going to the bus station. When Baba saw me in the jagriti, He asked me why I was still there and had not left for Saharsa. Baba said the seminar participants would be waiting for my arrival. Baba instructed me that I must reach there before the seminar started — on Friday morning — by any means necessary. I told Baba about my visit to the train and bus stations and about the bus I was going to take that would take me to Mansi at 2 p.m. the next day where a train would leave at 5 p.m., getting me to Saharsa by 4 a.m. on Friday morning. Baba replied that this is the only bus that connects South Bihar (present day Jharkhand state) with North Bihar. When I reached the bus station I was shocked to see that the bus was already full of passengers and that the front and back doors were already shut. Another ten minutes were left. The inside space between both the doors were also jam-packed with passengers. I became frantic and like a madman started peering inside through the bus windows to see if I could get the chance to somehow sit inside. One gentleman who was sitting in the aisle next to the window asked me what I was looking for and where I wanted to go. I told him that I had to go to Mansi. He told me to give my luggage to him through the window and come inside through the driver's exit. I did as he asked. That kind man told me to take my luggage and sit on his seat. Thus I had managed not only to get on the bus but had found a seat so that I did not have to stand throughout the journey. I sat down and remembered Baba. I slept on the bus and dreamt of Baba — a sweet dream that I no longer remember.

I reached on time and conducted the seminar. I was there for three days and on the Monday I started back for Ranchi. When I arrived back, Baba wanted to know about my journey. I replied that it was very blissful.

DUTY TO GIVE THANKS

Baba said, "You worthless chap; why did you not thank the person who offered you the seat on the bus?"

Baba had taught me a very important lesson. If we are working for Him and His Mission, He sends help through different people and that help gives us tremendous bliss but we must not forget to thank and love the messengers of His grace. He is always there to take care of those who work for Him.

FEVER DRAWS HIM NEAR

Seminar year (1969-70) had started. Baba was pleased with my work and so He gave me a bigger responsibility. I was posted as VIM (volunteer incharge of miscellaneous) of the same VSS department. Once I fell sick and came down with high fever.

Our rooms were adjoining Baba's quarters. Normally, every morning I would escort Baba on field walk. That day, when I did not come, Baba started enquiring about me. Someone told Baba that I was sick with fever.

While returning from field walk, Baba came into my room.

Baba asked, "Are you not well?"

FEVER DRAWS HIM NEAR

I tried to get up but could not sit properly because I was so weak.

Baba said, "Keep lying down."

Baba then asked what was wrong. When Baba heard about my high fever, He prescribed a simple medicine, turmeric mixed with hot milk two to three times a day.

I carried out Baba's instructions and became well the next day.

KURÚH VISHVAḾ AVADHÚTAM

Baba had such deep concern about the fast deteriorating standards of morality in individual and collective life. This was reflected in His famous Vanii given on the occasion of the commemoration of His birth in 1958.

In the transitional period of civilization, honesty in individual life is a prime necessity. We shall have to remain ever vigilant that the darkness of petty self-interest may not shroud this supreme human treasure. With the very extinction of honesty, civilization too will not survive; the long sádhaná of the human race will go in vain, and all intellectual achievements will become meaningless. Book knowledge that cannot be utilized in life has no value.

Once during Ananda Purnima DMC in Ranchi on May 24 1971, Baba called the avadhutas and avadhutikas and explained that it is the responsibilities of avadhutas and avadhutikas to save the humanity. For this He gave the target that sadvipra samaj is first to be established at Ananda Nagar, then India and then the world. Then Baba said in such a powerful and moving way, "Humanity is bleeding, it is not just crying. It is the responsibility of you all to save humanity."

Just remembering that mood of Baba when he said this, still today makes my hairs stand on end with the thrill of bliss of Taraka Brahma. Then to manifest this mission as soon as possible to save as many people as possible from agony, Baba gave the command given seven thousand years ago by Lord Shiva,

KURÚH VISHVAḾ AVADHÚTAM

Kurúh Vishvaḿ Tántrikam

(You shall make everyone in this universe Tantrics.)

Baba later explained this saying,

To become a Tantric means to fight directly against the opposing forces and obstacles on the path of progress and to preach the lofty gospels of universal humanism. That human beings are the greatest of all living beings is to be proven in action, not in mere words or theoretical moral principles. ("Bhaerava and Bhaeravii," Discourses on Tantra Volume Two)

KURÚH VISHVAḾ AVADHÚTAM

But to initiate everyone into Tantric sadhana was not enough. Baba needed adepts in Tantra. Therefore Baba gave another command saying,

Kurúh Vishvaḿ Avadhútam (You shall make everyone in this universe avadhutas.)

The role of the avadhuta/tika is to see that Baba's mission reaches all the people in each and every nook and corner of this planet. Their only duty is to establish His mission on this earth. For this they have taken the oath and owe an irredeemable debt to Baba for this blessing. Their responsibility is to make sure society is run based on morality, equality (sama-samaja), justice and Neohumanistic love. Like a Taoist sage, they are not to be a ruler or commanding leader but to see that society is ruled by morality. Rather, due to their absence of ego they are to be a invisible or inconspicuous leader. They will work quietly and will only take firm action when society is greatly endangered. Lord Shiva spent much time in meditation and spreading knowledge and culture. Only in times of crisis or moral decay would He appear to take action to restore Dharma. Baba explained this stance of the avadhuta/tiká saying that it means one who has conquered the shaktis of the universe. The first is bhavanii shakti or the shakti (divine energy, power) of the aham (doership, ego) and the citta (objectivated Consciousness or mind-stuff). The citta consists of five layers, all of which have to be purified, sublimated and merged into Consciousness.

The second is bhaeravii shakti or the shakti of the mahat (existential feeling of "I exist"). The third is the kaośikii shakti or the shakti of the Cosmic Nucleus or ultimate witnessing entity. The avadhuta/tiká is one who has conquered all three shaktis within himself/herself, conquered these shaktis within the collective mind and conquered them within the Cosmic Mind and thereby merged in the feet of Taraka Brahma. Baba said:

KURÚH VISHVAḾ AVADHÚTAM

The attainment of Brahma signifies the victory of one’s ideology; first the victory of Bhaeravii Shakti and then the victory of Kaośikii Shakti. Or put in another way, first the defeat of Bhavánii Shakti, then the defeat of Bhaeravii and Kaośikii Shaktis at the hands of Puruśa. When one becomes the master of Bhavánii, Bhaeravii and Kaośikii Shaktis, one attains enormous power to subdue the wicked forces and protect the forces of righteousness in the external world. One who does not practise any active sádhaná to control these three shaktis and expand one’s cognitive faculty cannot fight against anti-social forces. This process of bringing these three shaktis within one’s control is called sádhana-samara (the battle of intuitional practice). This battle should end in one of two ways: in victory or in death. One should never undergo the ignominy of defeat. If one dies while seeking the Supreme, one will surely attain the Supreme after death. One who is totally absorbed in ideation on the Supreme becomes one with the Supreme at the time of death. This is certainly true in the case of devotees. The duality between the one who serves and the entity served ceases to exist. Ultimately the one who serves, the entity who is served, and the action of serving become fused into one. If one’s goal is right, one is sure to attain the Supreme Stance. And one who attains the supreme stance will never make the mistake of differentiating between high and low, literate and illiterate, touchable and untouchable.
Shmasháne vá gſhe, hirańye vá tŕńe,
Tanoje vá ripao, hutáshe vá jale.
Svakiiye vá pare samatvena buddhyá,
Viráje avadhúto dvitiiyo Maheshah.

(The sádhaka who views everything with equanimity, be it his home or the burial ground, gold or grass, his own children or his enemies, fire or water, his own property or another's property, lives in the world as an Avadhúta, as if the second manifestation of Shiva.) ("The Devotee and the Lord," Ananda Marga Ideology and Way of Life in a Nutshell Part 9)

KURÚH VISHVAḾ AVADHÚTAM

Shiva created avadhutas who were completely free from all worldly bondages and it was the mission of Lord Anandamurti that these avadhutas go everywhere inspiring, sacrificing, fighting and dying for suffering humanity. The mission of an avadhuta or avadhutika's life will not be fulfilled and their duty will not be over if even a single person is being exploited or made to suffer deprivation. An avadhuta remains unchanged and immersed in Baba at all times while continually moving, inspiring, serving and fighting in countless ways in countless places.

The grandeur and sacrifice of Baba's avadhutas must be restored on this planet. As the writer on the website Ananda Marga Bhagavat Dharma has revealed, in the RU speech on June 2, 1990, Baba said: "These are the new avadhutas/avadhutikas — They will be going to different corners of this Earth, to propagate the Gospel of Peace, to propagate the ideology of Neo-Humanism. They will be representing the Crimson Dawn and they will create a New World in near future."

KURÚH VISHVAḾ AVADHÚTAM

To quote more from the above mentioned website which records notes from what Baba said on various occasions: "They have no other relative in the universe except the Supreme Father — Baba. He alone is everything for the avadhuta/tika. Baba takes the place not only of a father but also of mother, brother, sister and friend. He is the only rudder in the avadhuta/tika's life. The dagger, hanging all the time on the shoulders of an avadhuta is symbolic of their undertaking to constantly and relentlessly fight the foes of evil and immorality. Their duty in life is hence an unremitting fight against everything that makes life static (tamasik) and dull (crude) under the influence of the pseudo-sense (feeling) of momentary peace. The avadhuta's dagger is a sign and incessant reminder of their pledge to continue that fight. The staff or trishula that an avadhuta/tika carries with them, is the sign of the victory of Dharma (righteousness) over adharma (injustice, inequality, exploitation, immorality, crudity), the victory of vidya (enlightenment; conscience) over avidya (nescience; without a conscience) and the victory of morality over immorality. They have to be unbending like their staff and with it, stave off even the momentary inroads of the forces of evil that may tempt them towards a compromise. In no circumstances can avadhuta/tikas tolerate any anti-ideological activity."

By His grace avadhutas and avadhutikas will lead the revolution in samajas all over the world to establish sadvipra samaja and a devotional revolution on this planet. Let us now feel in the marrow of our bones, the call Baba gave to us in Venezuela,

The entire human society is anxiously waiting for you, for your service. You will have to shoulder a great responsibility for the suffering humanity. You know, actually humanity bleeds, and you will have to save them. You should remember that if you don't help humanity, who else is to come and shoulder the responsibility? It is you — you are to do everything. You are the torch-bearers of human civilization. You are the pioneers of human march, and you are the vanguard of a new civilization. ("The Glorious March," Ánanda Vacanámrtam Part 14)

MUMBAI DMC

Mr C. D. Munshi, a very senior and leading margi of Mumbai, had a spacious building at 20 Altamount Road. He was by nature a mild and soft-spoken margii. His family had migrated from West Punjab during the partition of India and Pakistan. He was the chairman of PBI (Proutist Bloc of India). For some years the front portion of the ground floor of his house was used for security purposes and the back portion was occupied for PROUT activities. The first floor of the building was reserved for Baba's visits to Mumbai. As an agreement, the regional secretary (RS) of Mumbai region (at that time it included Gujarat, Maharashtra and three dioceses of Andhra Pradesh) was to pay one hundred rupees rent to C. D. Munshi. Though this was the agreement, everyone by then had come to know the place as Baba's quarters. The building held special and sentimental place in the minds of the margis and workers.

Baba, before His imprisonment, had stayed there many times. I remember when I also happened to be there once on June 26, 1969 during DMC. I was posted as a sectorial worker in the security department. One highly significant incident took place during the DMC.

First and most importantly, although it is said that our wondrous kiirtan BABA NAMA KEVALAM was given recognition by Baba in Amjharia (Latehar District), in fact this is not entirely true. Yes, it was in this building that a few margis and a few workers started singing with tabla drums, "BABA... BABA... BABA NAM" as the chorus of a devotional song. This continued for several hours. During that song, the word "kevalam" was not sung but we all felt tremendous spiritual vibrations.

Baba, who was staying on the next floor, sent someone to call me. When I met Baba in His room, He said, "How nice, let Me listen to this tune." Baba even asked me who were present in the singing. I

MUMBAI DMC

remembered a few names: Acarya Acintananda, Acarya Japananda Avadhuta, Ac. Vitashokhananda Avt., Ac. Amreshananda Avt., Ac Vimuktananda Avt. and a few more workers and margis whom I was not acquainted with since I was new to Mumbai.

I earnestly say that the origin of our kiirtana started from there in seed form.

What I personally feel is that Baba as Cosmic Regulator leaves the vibration and then reflects it upon the created beings, who throb as per that regulated vibration that is of Cosmic origin. This later takes the shape of a system and a synchronization of different sentiments and people. This was also the case regarding our blessed mantra of Baba Nama Kevalam and all other mantras including our Ista Mantra (mantra of the first lesson of Ananda Marga meditation), Guru mantra (second lesson mantra) or Dhyana Mantra.

DOMINANCE OF MONEY NO MORE, NO MORE

We knew Baba used to give special attention and care to wholetime workers — not because He loved them more than the householders but so that His mission was established faster. The householders deserve the credit, for they are the ones who in the beginning created wholetimer monks (dadas) and nuns (didis). Sadly many dadas and didis don't understand the reality and undermine the householder's dignity and sacrifice. Anyway, as a rule (to encourage youth to become WTs) Beloved Baba used to spare more time in the form of reporting with the WTs. I want to emphasize also that didis should not feel that they did not get equal place in Baba's mind because the dadas got more time for reporting.

In Mumbai, Baba called workers meetings thrice a day — morning, noon and evening — and took the report. Baba fixed two ideals for us to understand:

(1) The domination of money in our lives no more — no more (2) Fight, fight and fight for the righteous cause.

I do not know why Baba emphasized these two ideals that time in Mumbai. This inquisitive mind of mine was trying to fathom the reasons. It was clear that for the spiritual growth of a devotee, money must not play a predominant role in one's life.

DOMINANCE OF MONEY NO MORE, NO MORE

Ac Vimuktananda, the editor of Cosmic Society (a journal of the Renaissance Universal of Ananda Marga published from Delhi), was also present during that reporting session. He was in Mumbai. Shrii Mangal Bihariji, Shrii Asthanaji and Shrii Raghubir Prasadji of Delhi were high officials and had good contacts among the business community in Mumbai. With the referral of one of them, Ac Vimuk-

tananda Avt was assured a pledge of Rs. 2000 from one businessman in Mumbai, who was to deliver the amount in the evening. Ac. Vimuktananda told Baba about the Rs 2000. Baba then reminded him about the theme of that DMC that He had floated in our souls — "The Dominance of Money no more, no more." We all were very serious about it and so was Baba. During noon reporting Ac Vimuktananda said, "Baba I presented my situation to that gentleman and said that I would not be accepting the money. When the gentleman heard me, he increased the amount from Rs. 2000 to Rs. 3000." Baba said this was good and told us to stick to these two points of this DMC.

Actually Dada refused out of sincere adherence to the ideal set by Baba about being free from the domination of money. The businessman however thought that the monk was insulted by too little money and hence he increased the amount of the contribution.

In the evening reporting session we came to know that upon rejecting the Rs. 3000, the amount had been increased to Rs. 5000. Baba remained silent, smiling mischievously. At night, Dada was in a fix about whether to accept the money or not. Baba's theme was in his mind but the need was urgent. In fact he had not expected such a large amount — in those days Rs. 2000 was a good amount. The next morning in reporting, Baba asked Dada directly what progress he had made. Dada said, "Baba this gentleman is coming soon and I am going to accept this money."

DOMINANCE OF MONEY NO MORE, NO MORE

Baba smiled and said that the first theme of this DMC is that money should be utilized and not hoarded. Dada was very happy, because had Baba not created this drama, he would not have received Rs. 5000.

There was also conflict between the DS (Diocese Secretary) Acarya Acintanandji and several rich margis who always tried to get a chance to drive Baba in their cars and thereby persuade Baba to stay in their houses. The DS felt that money issues were dominating his life as a worker. So Baba with one stroke in declaring the theme of the DMC solved all his problems. Thus Baba has given us this ideal and if we fully accept it all our problems will be solved.

BABA TEACHES US TO FIGHT

The Mumbai DMC was especially blissful for me for so many reasons. I could feel that everyone there enjoyed Baba's pressure. The next day was the day of departure from Mumbai to Delhi. We all went to the airport to see Baba off. As per airport regulations, we each bought the entry ticket of Rs. 4. Baba was sitting on the sofa and we either sat or remained standing around Him. He was always the centre of attention and adoration from both margis and non-margis as well.

Suddenly the airport authority announced that Baba's flight had been cancelled. The passengers were requested to get their tickets endorsed for the next day. We had been blessed with one more day to be with our Baba. How very happy we felt I cannot express. We came back to our place. Baba had His own routine and we had ours. We received one dictum from Baba that when we go to the airport, we should not buy the entry tickets. Ananda Marga is a poor organization and it is the fault of the airport authorities that they cancelled the flight, so why should we pay for entry tickets a second time.

As planned, we all assembled at the airport gate. Three or four persons bought tickets and went inside with Baba. We started arguing with the security guard to allow us to go inside. They were insisting that we should buy entry tickets. We were in large numbers and from inside Baba was watching us. A melee ensued, with everyone pushing, and one dada's watch fell because the chain broke. That was the last straw and we all entered inside. Straightaway, we went to Baba. He enjoyed the drama. This is how He encourages us to fight for the right cause. Baba's reply reminded us of the importance of struggle, as He Himself has said:

You should know that the nature of the world is such that nowhere in it is there any permanence or freedom from

BABA TEACHES US TO FIGHT

struggle. There is always unrest and conflict. If anyone thinks that there should be peaceful coexistence, and that that is the greatest human treasure, they will be making a mistake. It is not the greatest human treasure, because it goes against jaeva dharma (the nature of living beings)... Hence, non-struggle — the absence of struggle — is but another name for death. It has been my constant endeavour to arouse this spirit of struggle in you; I have never encouraged aversion towards struggle. ("Spiritual Lessons of the Giit\u00e1 \u2013 2," Discourses on K\u1e5b\u015b\u1e47a and the Giit\u00e1)

DON'T COMPLAIN AGAINST YOUR JUNIOR

One morning in 1969, Baba came to the office and transferred me as RS Chandigarh. At that time a few workers had been sent overseas, so the organization had become global. This development underscored the importance for me of strictly following yama and niyama in organizational life. I had gone to Dharamsala (in Himachal Pradesh) to participate in a seminar. Acarya Sambudhananda Avadhuta had also come there to take over my post as the global head of VSS (Volunteer Social Service). Acarya Vitamohanananda Avadhuta was the DS of Dharmsala diocese.

A heated argument had taken place between myself and Sambudhananda Dada regarding collecting donations from non-margis. He wanted to know whether I had approached the deputy collector of Kangra District in Dharmsala under a false pretext. I replied that I had not and that I felt that doing this would have lowered the dignity of Ananda Marga. I had already approached him with Vitamohananda and said that we were running a school and doing social service such as food distribution and clothes distribution to poor students in government schools.

I suspected that Sambudhananda wanted to extract the money by threatening to implicate me on false charges. He threatened me that he would bring the case to Baba's knowledge and make sure that I was punished. I told him to do what he liked but I wouldn't succumb to his false allegations and give him money in this way. Sambudhananda had taken over my central post. Mr Shrish Jaiswal had donated Rs. 10,000 to me that I had kept in the Central Treasury, then under Acarya Shraddhananda Avadhuta. Shraddhananda was not giving the money back to this department. After my transfer, Sambudhananda also approached him but he refused to give the money to him as well. This was why Sambudhananda was trying to make me get more money from Mr Jaiswal under a false

pretext. But I was adamant not to be cowed down by his threats to bring false allegations against me.

DON'T COMPLAIN AGAINST YOUR JUNIOR

When I reached Ranchi for RDS I came to know that he had already complained to Baba about me, alleging that I had asked the DC for a donation on false pretences and thus as an acarya of Ananda Marga I had degraded myself and damaged the reputation of Ananda Marga.

Dearest Baba, in the name of punishing me, brought up this allegation to me during reporting. I refuted the allegation and told Baba that it was a complete lie. Baba then called the central Dharma Pracar secretary and asked him to make an enquiry into this matter. Baba cautioned that if the allegation was proved to be true then action would be taken against me and if not then the same actions would be taken against Sambudhananda. I readily agreed to this.

Two days later, Baba called Sambudhanananda and myself. Baba said, "It was not good on your part, Sambudhananda, to lodge a baseless complaint against Vandanananda. You should feel ashamed."

Baba further said, "Even if this had happened, you should have settled the matter among yourselves. Avoid lodging complaints against your junior workers or workers under your supervision. It is unworthy of the supervisor or senior to file a complaint against a junior worker under your supervision. If a supervisor launches a case against a worker under him it means that he is an inefficient administrator and is trying to shield his inefficiency through the complaint."

BEING BEYOND TIME

I was transferred as RS of Chandigarh, I was supposed to submit a report about the seminar held in our region. We had to write the names of villages, panchayats, blocks, districts and dioceses for every state in our region and deposit three copies on time. If this was not completed, the ISMUB (Inspection, Seminar, Movement, Utilisation Boards) departmental secretary would not sign our tour program to authorize us to leave. There was a dearth of money as always.

But one wonderful thing I want to mention is the bond of love and affection among workers who were there. In Ranchi we sat writing the list and we bought chickpeas and puffed rice, mix it with mustard oil and green chili and enjoyed it together.

We were always having Baba in our minds. We were always talking about Baba and working for Baba. Like this our days and years went by. We did not care for the past or future. We never made any train reservations or planned for our food or anything else. We were truly beyond time, living like blissful cosmic children.

Lord Shiva said, Varttamáneśu vartteta ["Live in the present"]. To live in the present moment is impossible. Only those who are in a transcendental spiritual flow attain this stance. Such people remain unaffected by past or future. This is just one beautiful facet of being part of His liila. This bliss of living in the moment was not only possible in the past when Baba was living physically with the devotees. It can be experienced today as Baba guides the manifestation of His mission on this planet. And it will be experienced by saintly beings in the future just as Miirabai experienced Krsna's enchanting liilas (divine plays) hundreds of years after Lord Krsna's time on earth.

BEING BEYOND TIME

I remember once, when we decided to sleep after writing for a long time, we thought we should go out anywhere to the bathroom so

that we could sleep in the room or on the verandah. We noticed that not only had the sun set, but at that moment it had begun to rise crimson red in the eastern sky!

Such was the life — with no sorrow, pain or grievance. May Baba bless all to know the joy of this collective working — day and night — of those days.

HELP FROM BABA

When I was RS, the workload was intense. I used to come to Ranchi every month for RDS. There had always been a dearth of money in Ananda Marga. But Baba's work never stopped because of paucity of funds. If you receive more than what you need, then it leads to luxury, misuse of money and moral corruption. If one gets less than what one needs, then one commits crimes out of desperation. Baba explained that these are two causes of vice in the discourse "Three Causes of Sin."

However, in the field of social service, if there is a shortage of funds to fulfill the needs of society, that will keep you busy with missionary zeal. That will be the way to properly utilize food and money. This is why Baba always gave bigger and bigger projects or targets beyond our capacity — so as to keep us busy in service to society. This will bring us in contact with the suffering and exploited people and will radicalize us to fight to manifest the economic liberty of PROUT.

Once I boarded a train for Ranchi at Chandigarh after the IRSS (Inspection, Review and Structural Solidarity). With what little money I had, I was able to buy the ticket up to Delhi only. The idea came to me that I could go to our Delhi quarters in South Extension and get some money from the dadas there and then go to Gaya from where I could catch a bus to Ranchi.

The timing was such that if I missed the Kalka Mail train, I would not be able to reach Ranchi in time and would be punished for coming late. What was I to do? I had no money to buy the ticket, thus I got off the Kalka Mail and I was just walking on the road outside the railway station to get the bus to go to South Extension when a car pulled up and stopped before me.

HELP FROM BABA

A gentleman came out of the car and said, "Are you Acarya Rameshananda?"

"No," I replied.

"Yes! I met you in Singapore and attended your lecture. I was very much impressed."

He took out Rs. 200 from his pocket and gave it to me and left. What a great help from Baba. I went back to the station, bought a ticket for Gaya and boarded the same train. I reached Ranchi in time. This is how He fulfilled our needs without giving us more than we needed.

SAVING A BROTHER

There was a DMC in Western UP in 1970 and I could not attend it. Since I was the RS it was considered my duty to attend it. There was a miscommunication regarding the particular area from which workers were expected to attend the DMC. When Baba did not see me there, He transferred me and posted me as DS Ludhiana.

I later realised that the transfer from central VSS to the regional level was a great blessing. So long as Baba was in Ranchi, before going to jail, I had the chance to be around Him, and then just before His arrest, I was transferred to a very safe diocese.

The Ludhiana unit was a very strong unit and it still is today. I worked there and the numbers of students increased in the school. I was there for three years in charge of the children's home, hostel and school. By His grace, we had much success propagating the ideology there.

During 1971-2, Acarya Sarvatmananda, the central dharma pracar secretary, was on the run. He was perhaps being sought by the CBI (Central Bureau of Investigation — the "intelligence" agency of India) to be tortured into testifying against Baba. On December 29, 1971 Baba was arrested and thereafter began His long ordeal in jail. At that time, Acarya Prakashananda had already shifted his loyalty to the CBI, who were supporting the organizational rebels against Baba. He phoned me several times and asked about the whereabouts of Sarvatmananda Dada who was very close, personally and organisationally, to him in the past. His aim was to convince dada to leave Baba. It so happened that one morning in January Sarvatmananda Dada was outside my school. When I went out to buy some fruits, he was standing there covered with a blanket. His face was visible but his whole body was covered. He approached me at the nearby shop.

SAVING A BROTHER

In the beginning, I ignored him, thinking that he was a Sikh beg-

gar. When he talked to me with fear in his voice, I was able to recognise him. I assured him that he was safe with me. I brought him to my room and then shifted him to a safer place in Ludhiana. He remained there with full care and love for one week and then as per his desire, I managed to send him to Mumbai. But I never let him come in contact with Prakashananda so as to save Sarvatmananda from leaving Baba. This was all possible by Baba's grace.

The situation in the organization was very turbulent, with times of great joy and great sorrow. Baba says that everyone is moving around the Cosmic Beloved in waves of ups and downs. Baba said that when the ups and downs become gigantic in a short period, this is called His Tandava Dance.

Throughout this time and later when seven persons immolated themselves, the margis of Punjab and Chandigarh Region stood firmly with the mission. Margis and workers stood by in solidarity with the Centre and contributed generously in those challenging times.

SAVING A BROTHER

I made good contacts with many people in these states who helped me so much during the Emergency. I stayed there for some eleven to twelve months. When I was at Ludhiana, I went for kapalika sadhana at least once a week. That kept my spiritual flow high. I want to thank them with all my heart. Some of them are Shrii Raikhy, Shrii Tarsem Lal, Shrii Siddu, Shrii Jagannath and Shrii N.K. Jolly of Patiala. It was in the month of February 1973 that I was transferred to Mumbai.

SIXTEEN POINTS

Initially, although Baba had prescribed various rules for margis and monks, we were not strict in following them. We were required to wear laungota (yogic underwear). The laungota protects the genital area, prevents excessive sexual stimulation and diverts the mind upwards. However we were not strictly wearing laungota at all times.

Similar is the case with fasting. Most often we were taking fruits on fasting days though we were not having rice, bread and other regular food. Actually many people in India have this practice of taking healthy food on fasting days. Also we used to take food in hotels and restaurants. We also used to drink caffeinated soft drinks like Coca Cola. We were also allowed to have more than four items per meal and eat more than four times a day.

In July 1971, Baba introduced Sixteen Points and became very strict about our following proper diet and other points. Baba explained that if a monk took food in a hotel it was considered tamasik (crude, static). Caffeinated drinks such as tea, coffee and soft drinks were also not allowed for monks. Baba used to scrutinize monks during meetings and RDS. He used to strictly correct the smallest lapse. Bathing was to be done preferably twice a day and asanas were to be done twice a day. In this way, workers realised how Baba was watching them at all times. In addition, Baba used to check the Sixteen-Point charts of workers and margis. He also taught the local worker in-charge to perform this duty.

Baba would scold more seriously those who had committed more severe offences. Baba said that when a person's actions harm society this is a punishable offense. He said that if the person's actions did not harm society but were ignoble or degrading, they were condemnable.

SIXTEEN POINTS

Once a worker said that he had some health problem and that is

why he was doing liquid fasting. Baba replied that fasting without liquids would cure his disease. He explained that He had prescribed fasting after careful consideration and so the workers and margis should follow this strictly.

Six months later – on December 29 – Baba was imprisoned and so many struggles began for His disciples. Baba said that in order to face such tough opposition we must be strong enough to face it. That is why He introduced Sixteen Points, so that the disciples would become stronger than the power of the repressive forces. Thus considering the present precarious state of the Ananda Marga movement, all disciples should become adamantly strict in following Sixteen Points and conduct rules. Baba explained this:

What is the best way to strengthen the mind? The best and proper way is to strictly observe the 16 Points [physical, mental and spiritual exercises for the all-round development of the individual]. The more rigidly you follow the 16 Points, the more your mental strength will increase. It will increase so much that it will be more powerful than the collective mental strength of 100,000 people. In a psychic clash they will have to concede victory to you. A person who is physically strong can fight against eight, ten or twenty people at most, but no more than that. A psychically developed person, however, is able to fight against many more people. So you must endeavour to develop your psychic strength – this is your bounden duty. The more rigidly you follow the 16 Points, the more psychic strength you will acquire. It will not take you long. The only way to develop spiritual strength is to surrender everything to Him – the more you surrender yourself to Him, the more you will develop your soul. Such development will lead to not only 10,000 people surrendering before you, but the entire universe. ("An Exemplary Life")

TRUEST FLORAL TRIBUTE

This unforgettable memory is from 1971. Preparations for the Ananda Purnima Dharma Maha Cakra (when Baba would bless the devotees with a special mudra) on the occasion of Baba's fiftieth birthday were in full swing. A delightful environment filled the hearts of the devotees in Ranchi. Everyone was preparing for this pious occasion with full devotion. In those days Baba was living in a rented house in Ranchi. Ananda Marga's central office was located just behind his residence.

All margi brothers and sisters were in bliss to celebrate Baba's fiftieth birthday. Why should they not celebrate? It was Baba's fiftieth birthday. At some distance there was a ground in which a tent as large as a palace was being erected. People were astonished to see this huge beautiful and attractive tent.

Being in the Social Security Department, I had the primary responsibility. I had the opportunity to live near Baba with easy access to Him. To sit for hours in front of Him, to do sadhana and get the bliss of sadhana – it was an inexplicable experience.

To bring me back to a normal state, Baba suddenly told me to go towards the tent and tell Him how it was being prepared. I ran there and then returned to Baba and explained whatever I understood, after seeing the situation. I found Him deeply absorbed and it seemed that He was looking at something in another realm, as if He were peering into the future. What else could I understand of the mysterious import of His intense gaze that seemed to be creating the distant future itself.

TRUEST FLORAL TRIBUTE

The next morning, Baba's birthday, Acarya Raghunathji, Acarya Shivananda Avadhuta and I were standing by the door of Baba's room with garlands waiting in our hands. We had woven the garland with all our love. We were filled with delight.

The door to Baba's room opened and Baba came out, wearing beautiful cream-coloured clothes.

I should let you know that to the left of Baba's room was an adjoining room where Baba's PA and others were staying. There on the other side of Baba's room, His PA was standing. As Baba came, we did not know what to do. There was no clear pre-planning. Therefore we stood completely still like statues. At the same time, we were delighted to bathe in Baba's sweet smile. Standing like a statue, we were thinking about what to do.

Baba asked, "What are you thinking?"

TRUEST FLORAL TRIBUTE

I immediately placed the garland at Baba's feet and performed sastaunga pranama (full prostration).

Baba said with crisp grandeur, "It was rightly done," and blessed me. Acharya Raghunathji and Acarya Shivanandji did the same and then Baba went to his room.

I was in a state of indecisiveness, wondering whether it was right to garland Baba around His neck? Who can ever think to greet Guru or Parama Purusa (Supreme Entity) on His birthday like an ordinary mortal? Did our greeting please Him?

Other works of preparing for Dharma Maha Cakra had started. Baba was repeatedly asking about that tent. Suddenly at four p.m. in the afternoon black clouds filled the sky. Heavy rain and a thunderstorm started and... the decorated tent crashed to the ground. Later on I understood why Baba had asked repeatedly about the tent.

TRUEST FLORAL TRIBUTE

After the fall of the tent, DMC was held in the open field. Baba sat on a wooden cot and blessed the devotees. In the open sky, the moon was shining with full lustre and below our earth was clean and gleaming with the recent rain. Our Beloved Baba was sitting there and we all were so very happy. It seemed like the open sky was our tent and in this environment, Baba was blessing us with varabhaya mudra. There was no consideration of gatepass and thus margis and non-margis mingled in that gathering.

The very next day multifarious attacks on Ananda Marga started. Was that storm a precursor of this? Did His gazing into the distance signify an awareness and preparation of the disciples for future events? This was all too mysterious to me.

The uprooting of tent, the thundering of clouds and the advent of a storm during the golden jubilee celebration of Baba's birthday was a sign of upcoming struggles for Ananda Marga. One by one, struggles arose on many levels.

On the second day, a bloody clash with the RSS, a violent Hindu organization took place in Ranchi during our march to celebrate Baba's birthday, which started from Ratu Road. On that particular day, lathi attacks (baton beating) and gunshots from the inimical forces were seen on the streets of Ranchi. Some of our avadhutas had to be hospitalized. The police came and imposed a curfew. The communists attacked us out of fear that Prout would attract the progressive intellectuals, whereas the RSS acted out of fear that our strict humanistic spirituality would attract the people of India. Corrupt officials worked with these reactionary forces to malign and persecute Ananda Marga.

TRUEST FLORAL TRIBUTE

There is another interesting point that I want to explain here. Wherever Baba went and started his work, most of the local people did not like it. Whether it was Jamalpur, Patna, Ranchi, Jodhpur Park, Lake Gardens or Tiljala, this was always the case.

Baba's devotees, Sadashiva's ganas (indigenous Indians persecuted by the Aryans) and Shrii Krsna's Pandavas were filled with social consciousness and swayed by the Lord's sweetness. Yet this great awakening was usually not liked or appreciated by the local people. They would try to hinder the activities of Ananda Marga in big or small ways. In this way Baba created polarization wherever He went. Thus Baba taught us by His actions.

A Tantric is called, a sádhaka is called, a soldier. (Sádhanásamara) ("The Battle of Sádhaná"). Samara means "war", "battle", "fight". The sádhaka is engaged in fight. It is for the brave, it is for courageous people. Sádhanásamara. And this cult is the cult of Tantra. He who wants to keep himself away from fight is unknowingly committing suicide, mental and spiritual suicide. Each and every man and woman should be ready for fight — fight in the mental stratum, fight in the family stratum, fight in each and every stratum of life. This is Tantra. ("Fighting at Each and Every Step," Ánanda Vacanámrtam Part 34)

Because of this brewing situation and negative media reports about Baba and Ananda Marga, we workers became afraid and sad. We thought Baba would be arrested.

TRUEST FLORAL TRIBUTE

Baba came to the office in the morning and we greeted him but our hearts were hurting and in a state of panic.

Baba said, "Oh, why are you people sitting so sadly?"

The environment was indeed gloomy. We all kept silent and showed Him the newspaper.

Baba went to his office room and sat. Then Baba called all of us inside His room. With a few jokes, all became happy, for such was the infectious bliss of our Supreme Optimist.

TRUEST FLORAL TRIBUTE

Baba again remonstrated when we started to leave, "No not that way. Stay! You people start kirtan and I will sit in the centre."

We all started kirtan happily. Baba immediately drove all fear out of us and prepared us to face the coming cyclone of vilification and persecution of Ananda Marga. A Tantric disciple starts his/her mission by offering his/her head to the Mahakaola (Great Tantric Guru). These forms of sacrifice are the real offering a devotee offers when putting flowers at His feet. Baba creates opposition just to prepare and force us into this state of high surrender.

BABA ALWAYS PROVIDES

I was the principal and DS of Ludhiana school in Chandigarh region of Punjab state. In 1973, I was transferred as the RS of Mumbai. Ac Nityashuddhananda was the RS of Mumbai and he was posted as the DS in Ludhiana. I knew this dada very well and he is a good dada. We had worked together in Himachal Pradesh for nearly four years in the mid-sixties.

At this time my dear Baba was in jail. The reason for my transfer to Mumbai was puzzling me since the work was going well in Ludhiana. The number of students in our school had increased. I was also helping the central organization with money and supplies. The children's home and hostel were also running well. The Punjabi newspaper of that samaja, Asi Punjabi, was being published periodically.

Anyway, I went to my new posting with enthusiasm. The day I reached Ahmedabad — our headquarters in the province of Gujarat — religious riots broke out. Hindus and the Muslims were fighting and killing each other in the streets. I was not able to leave the railway station. The state of Gujarat was under Mumbai Region as per our organization. I had no option but to try to get a train for Mumbai. The train was overcrowded, as people were frantically trying to leave Gujarat.

Somehow I was able to reach the city of Surat near the Maharashtra border when suddenly to my great shock I found that someone had pickpocketed my wallet. With no money in my pocket and no ticket, I came to Mumbai and reached Altamount Road metro station and started walking. I vaguely remembered the way to our office, as I had been there for DMC in 1969.

BABA ALWAYS PROVIDES

When I arrived at Baba's Quarters, I got the key, bolted the door and slept for the whole day and night. I was completely alone in a place where I knew next to no one. I felt desolate and lonely. No

food to eat, no person to talk to, no money to buy anything. Once or twice, the owner of the building, Shrii C. D. Munshi knocked at the door but I simply said that I was sorry, I wanted to rest. I wanted to withdraw from everyone.

The next evening at around 7 p.m., after thirty-eight hours of isolation, there was a knock at the door. When I opened the door, a person pushed himself inside and sat on my sheet. I was not able to fully grasp what was going on. He looked around and took out Rs. 500 from his pocket and placed it in my hand. "Look here, Acaryaji," he said.

It seems that four years ago he had come to this place when my Baba was here. He enjoyed the atmosphere and learned meditation and shortly thereafter left overseas for Dubai. He said that he was again leaving tomorrow and had just wanted to visit this place one time. Without saying any more he got up and left, leaving me all alone in the room, dumbfounded and trying to understand what had just happened.

RESPECTING MARGIIS

I was Mumbai RS until 1975. I purchased Baba's Quarters in Andheri, although I faced a lot of opposition from other workers. I had a great desire that Baba should stay in that building. It was purchased with a large percentage of the money coming from Shrii C. D. Munshi. I collected money from other margis also. It should be mentioned here that Munshiji had approached the local court against us to force us to vacate Baba's Quarters. One floor of his building was rented to the RS and it was to be for Baba's Quarters only. Baba had stayed there many times before His arrest. Since that time, the RS posted there had not paid the rent for five years and hence Munshiji had gone to court. The previous RS had given in writing to the court that he would vacate the premises within a month. When it was reported to Baba, I was sent to Mumbai as the RS.

I told Munshiji that I would not vacate the place until other arrangements were made for a place to stay and the money was raised for this. Munshiji provided a large sum, and with this and other funds the Andheri building was purchased. This was reported to Baba. I should mention here that Munshiji was a great devotee of Baba and had wanted to renovate the entire building. Had he been properly and respectfully convinced, his relationship with the organization would not have become strained. The ground floor of that building had been generously given to various departments of our organization. After Baba's arrest, some workers took the cash and left the ground floor without any explanation. We dadas must be on guard not to take for granted or abuse the kindness of margis and the general public. This kindness comes from Baba's grace and to abuse that kindness is an insult to Baba's grace. But really I cannot fully blame that absconding worker or anyone else, as it was indeed a difficult time for all of us. When Baba came out of jail,

He chose Shrii Munshi to be one of the devotees who accompanied Him while coming out of jail.

At that time, Baba's Quarters in Andheri (Mumbai) was registered in my personal name because the members of the housing cooperative society in Mumbai from whom the flat was bought objected to having it registered in the name of AMPS. Much later, after the Emergency was lifted, it was registered in the name of Ananda Marga.

RESPECTING MARGIIS

When Baba came back from his world tour in 1978–9, He refused to stay in the residence of any margi of Mumbai. He said that He had His quarters and would stay there. He stayed there and I know that He faced difficulties staying there. But He fulfilled my desire and the desire of Shrii Tekchandji—a margi who helped me so much to maintain the sanctity of Baba's room and to preserve Baba's personal articles there. This was the reward for all the opposition and criticism I faced from many workers. They had spread rumours that I had registered the property in my name so as to take it and leave the organization. May Baba bless them. But this period of slander ended with Baba's visit. And this was the blessing for me.

RECOGNIZING BABA ALONE

It was Baba's ideas and words that occupied my mind. Whatever challenges came, I took it as Baba's work. Acarya Rakeshananda was posted in Jamnagar (Gujarat) as DS. He is really a senior and hard-working monk. He too had managed to survive in this hard and difficult region and time period. He had worked in Ajmer also. When Dada was in Jamnagar, he and I planned to visit Dwarka, the ancient capital of Lord Krsna. Much later, I came to know from Baba that the Dwarka of Krsna's time was submerged beneath the sea. This was also discovered later by the Archeological Survey of India. We went there well before Lord Krsna Janmastami (Krsna's birthday). In my early days as a Hindu, I used to enjoy going to the temple at this time. The Radha-Krsna temple was being decorated with lights. During evening worship, there was a queue. As a rule, those who paid more than Rs. 500 were allowed to go near the idol and the general public were not allowed to go near the altar. For some strange reason, I wanted to go inside the temple where only priests were allowed. Only they were allowed to wash, clean and clothe the idols. No woman was allowed.

These restrictions I didn't like. I insisted that I should be able to go inside the inner part of the temple. The discussion became heated, a scuffle ensued and the head priest came running to see what had happened. The priest asked me what my family clan was. I replied, "Look here, I am a sannyasi and an avadhuta and I want to go inside."

The priest replied, "Avadhutas do not wear clothes and you are well-dressed. Hence you are a fake." Avadhutas as per tradition are barely clad or naked monks who live in a state of enlightenment free from all mundane obsessions. The head priest asked the other priests to throw me out. All the while, Dada Rakeshananda had been observ-

ing this ruckus from a distance, disapprovingly. Realizing that the situation might take an unpleasant turn, I left the place, declaring that my Krsna was inside my heart. This is what I was forced to learn by this incident. It is very difficult in practice to fully root out attachment to the religious sentiments of one's early days.

RECOGNIZING BABA ALONE

Now the question arose about where we would stay. We came to know that there was an ashram for sannyasis only. We went there. The iron doors of the boundary gate were closed. There was a sannyasi sitting on a chair who was reading a book. I tapped on the gate and the sannyasi asked what we wanted. I replied that we wished to spend the night there. Glancing at us, he said the ashram is for sannyasis only. I replied that we were sannyasis.

He said, "It looks like you are from Ananda Marga."

"You are right," I replied.

"We do not recognise Ananda Marga sannyasis as sannyasis. You have no place here."

RECOGNIZING BABA ALONE

This ignited my feelings. Hitting the gate with my leg and shoes, I said, "Whether you recognise us or don't recognise us, who cares? My revered Baba recognizes us."

We left that place where we had been forced to realize the difference between a Hindu sannyasi and an Ananda Marga sannyasi. Rakeshananda was not feeling good about the situation. I said, "Dadaji, Baba will manage, or else we will pass the night in the burial ground. If this is the wish of the Lord, what can we do?"

We were able to locate the cremation ground and did our evening meditation there. While going to the market to search for some food, one old lady came running and stood in front of us. Stretching her arms wide, she blocked our way. She said "For many years I have been dreaming to see Krsna and Balarama (Krsna's brother) and lo here you are."

We both stood dumb with surprise. She forced us to follow her to her home. We stayed the night there and went out to do our kapalika sadhana. The next day we came back. She was profusely crying and weeping. "My Krsna and Balarama, don't leave my home."

TEMPLE TRAUMA

I was always asked to go to fields where there were lots of struggle. Vellore in Tamil Nadu is no exception. Baba was in jail. A lot of endurance, patience and positivity were needed to cope with the situation created by the crisis of Baba's imprisonment. When my Baba was poisoned, many demonstrations were carried out all over the world in protest. Some disciples, unable to bear Baba's suffering, committed self-immolation but Baba still remained in jail. Many workers and margis started to ponder deeply about this predicament. The dark clouds of doubt started hovering over the minds of the devotees.

While talking about Baba's suffering, one crucial point is that despite the attempt to kill Him, despite being bedridden due to fasting, despite facing life imprisonment, Baba became more and more optimistic. His smile kept on beaming and He would always rouse visitors out of depression and would not discuss His own hardships. He took these hardships but only gave the nectar of spiritual bliss to His visitors and to some of the jail staff.

The Brahmacari posted in Vellore as DS became the worst victim of the turmoil of the situation. Our well-reputed local school with 250 students had to face the crisis of the government persecution of our organization. It was in a rented building. The Brahmacari, who was newly posted there, closed down the school, sold the furniture, collected the fees in advance, and left with this money for his native village. When this was reported to the central headquarters and Baba came to know, I was asked to rush to that place. I was the RS of Mumbai Region at that time.

Acarya Viitamohananda was the DS of Nagpur in Mumbai region. We had worked together during difficult days in Himachal and Maharashtra. As per Baba's directive from jail, I handed over the

TEMPLE TRAUMA

charge of Mumbai and proceeded to Vellore. That was my first posting in South India.

When I reached there, I found nothing. No place to stay, no margis with whom to discuss the problems, no Dharmacakra (weekly meditation) and above all no support. When I reached Chennai, I met Acarya Cidananda and Acarya Venkateshvarananda. When we came back to Vellore, Shrii Sanmugam, who was a teacher, showed us two desks and two benches kept in a small room. Where to go and where to stay was a big question in our minds. I thought of Baba and managed to find a place to stay.

I stayed in a temple owned by a priest from North India. With what little support I could get from Acintananda (DS Chennai) and the Bangalore RS, Cidananda, I could sustain myself. I was there for a few months. I enjoyed the hospitality, food and company of the priest. He was not an educated person. He used to charge the high sum of Rs. 800 from people who came for treatment at the nearby Christian Medical College. Almost all patients from North India visited that temple. While there, I addressed the Rotary Club, the Lions Club and indeed it was Mr Pattabhiraman who had made the arrangements for me to stay in the temple.

Then the worst thing happened. Ac Nigamananda — the DC (Deputy Commander) of South India came to Vellore and told me important news. Baba had told a dada in jail that no sannyasi should take food from dharmajiivis (those who live by performing religious rituals), kalahajiivis (those who make a living by making people fight; eg. lawyers, police) and mrtajiivis (those who earn money by performing funerals). That was a clear instruction from Baba for me. I was enjoying the good North Indian food from that priest. When the priest asked me why I was no longer eating, I told him that from now on, I would take sprouted beans and some fruits I collected from the market. He of course did not like my explanation. Anyway, I continued there for a few days when suddenly the priest became sick and was running a high fever. He was not able to perform the Hindu ritual worship. A large number of people had come to offer him money in order for him to perform rituals so they could get the god's blessing. Actually the priest hardly knew any Sanskrit and used to recite the prayers hurriedly but the people were not concerned about this.

TEMPLE TRAUMA

When he found himself unable to perform that day, he asked me to perform the rituals, thinking that I was a Hindu priest like him. He did not know that I was no longer a Hindu and was a monk of the universal Ananda Marga mission. All the idols were there in a row. I told him that I could not do this since I was a yogi and did not indulge in such worship. He became furious and even with his bad health he picked up my bag and threw it out of the temple, telling me that thenceforth I had no place in his temple.

It was raining. I collected my belongings lying outside the gate and started walking in the rain. Hardly had I gone twenty metres when Mr Pattabhiraman came running from a shop and took me there. He inquired what had happened and I told him everything. He was angry and said, "I will take you to the temple and see that he allows you to stay there." I said no because I could not go against the wishes of the master of the temple. So Mr Pattabhiraman took me to his house. I then initiated him. The next morning I left for Chennai.

EMERGENCY IMPOSED

A National State of Emergency was instituted in India by Prime Minister Indira Gandhi in 1975. The Tamil Nadu government (DMK) was opposed to the Emergency. I came to Chennai. We were asked by the local police to think carefully and close the school, which had a large number of students. Cidanandji, Acintanandji and I, after a lot of discussion and consideration, handed over the building of the Chennai school to the owner and left the place for as-yet-unknown destinations. We stayed for a few days in a hotel near the railway station. We kept on changing hotels and were in civilian dress. In order to escape arrest we shifted to Vijayawada for some time. We did our usual kapalika sadhana on the banks of the Krishna River and then came back to Chennai. It was hard financially and psychologically to adjust with the situation created by the Emergency. After a short stay, I left for Mumbai. I was not in avadhuta uniform and straightaway went to the house of Mr. Srivastavji, a margi living alone near Vikroli. I stayed the night with him. As we were very close to each other, he allowed me to stay at his home.

The next morning, after breakfast, I got ready to go out. Hardly had I stood up to go out, when someone knocked at the door. Without any hesitation, I opened the door and made a welcoming gesture. I noticed from the face of Srivastavji that he had not seen these people before. They introduced themselves. They were from State Intelligence and were looking for Vandanananda. They asked Srivastavji whether he knew the whereabouts of Acarya Vandanananda. With all my control and composure, I interrupted and asked permission from Srivastavji to leave because I was late for some urgent work. And lo, this is how Baba saved me.

It was during the Emergency that to the heartache of all the disciples Baba was put on trial. The trial was conducted in such a manner by the government that it was condemned by international

legal observers like William T. Wells from England and Claude A. Sheppard from Canada. Baba was found guilty and sentenced to life imprisonment. While the disciples were crushed, Baba calmly announced that now the tables would turn, as there was no more the government could do against Him. A radiant future was expressed in His talks to us at that time.

EMERGENCY IMPOSED

Through legal contacts, a message from Baba came to us that was published in September 1976 in Harbinger magazine. It said:

"1. Do not fear anything or anyone. I am always with you.

  1. You must so live that people trust you.
  2. Difficulties can never be greater than your capacity to solve them."

After wandering to various religious places disguised as a pilgrim, I moved to many places and then to Punjab where I stayed in a rented building. I met my old friend Mr R. Raikhy, the owner of Raikhy Theatre. He gave me an ID card as an employee of his company and told me that I would be able to move anywhere with it and no one would doubt me.

EMERGENCY IMPOSED

Later, I met Acarya Nityashuddhananda when he came to court during his hearing. Many times, margi families became angry with me because their relatives were in jail while I was outside and free. Here I will mention one incident.

Mr. Kuldeep and his brother were arrested because they were active margis in Moga, Punjab. His wife was also a teacher in a municipal school. When I went there to enquire about his well-being, she was so furious that she asked me to leave the place immediately. The family were all my initiates. She even shouted that she would call the police and get me arrested. She blamed me for being the cause of her husband and brother-in-law being arrested. She did not allow me to enter the house.

I understood that they were in jail and there was no chance for their release, since it was the Emergency and a fear psychosis had gripped the country. When the Emergency was lifted, the opposition party formed a new government. Both brothers got their full salary for the time they spent in jail. They were very happy as it enabled them to buy their own house. They were so happy that they even thanked Baba for the Emergency being imposed and lifted so soon. In these ways, the Emergency proved to be a boon for those who suffered. Even now, those who were in jail at that time are getting ten to fifteen thousand rupees a month as compensation.

When the Emergency was lifted, I went to Mumbai. While there, I went with Shrii Rajesh Vaish and Shrii Prabhakar Sandu to Jaslok Hospital where Jayaprakash Narayan was being treated, to ask for his help with Baba's case. However, he refused to help us.

EMERGENCY IMPOSED

Baba's Quarters in Andheri was handed back to us by the police. The police had ransacked the ashram during the Emergency, trying to find incriminating documents. They took all documents, except one — my acarya certificate. I found it lying on the ground.

GAP BETWEEN CUP AND LIP

The following story was narrated to us in Delhi by Nageshvar Prasad, who was Baba's senior lawyer during His trial in Patna. When he undertook Baba's case, he made the decision that this was to be the final case in his legal career. He had great respect and love for Baba. During the Emergency period, the judgement was going to be delivered by the lower court after the weekend. Acarya Ramtanukji, who was a kapalika and a lawyer, was assisting Mr Nageshvar Prasad. On Saturday, two days before the day of judgement, Acarya Ramtanukji went to jail to meet Baba and other dadas who were also in jail.

Acarya Ramtanukji was very disturbed. He had been able to find out about the judgement the judge would deliver and was sure that Baba was going to be hanged. He went to the jail and conveyed this to one of the dadas who were in jail. Baba noticed that this dada's face became gloomy and sad. Baba asked him, "What is wrong? Today you are not greeting Me with a smile."

Dada broke the news to Baba. Baba said, "There is a gap between the cup and the lip." Then the dada and Baba went to their respective jail cells. What Baba meant was that there were still forty hours left before the judgement would be delivered. Anything could happen, so why worry?

Acarya Ramtanukji went to Shrii Nageshvar Prasad's chambers that same evening and told him what he had come to know. Nageshvar simply told Ramtanukji to go back home. Then Nageshvarji instructed the members of his house that no person should come to see him. He instructed them that they should just bring food and his personal articles to his room, which was attached to his chambers. Shrii Nageshvarji remained alone for more than forty hours. Saturday night and Sunday night passed by. On Monday, he came to the courtroom a bit late. The courtroom was fully packed with security

GAP BETWEEN CUP AND LIP

personnel. Later we found out that this was because they were worried that if any margis were among the audience, they would become very angry after hearing the judgement and would create trouble in the courtroom.

When Nageshvarji entered the courtroom, Baba was lying there on the stretcher and the four co-accused were also present. There was pin drop silence when Nageshvar entered. He surveyed the situation and paid his respects to Baba and sat on the chair reserved for him.

The judge said, "Mr Prasad! Now the judgement is going to be delivered. Are you going to say anything?"

Nageshvarji stood up and said, "Yes, my lord." Then he went to the open space between the judge and the audience and said,

GAP BETWEEN CUP AND LIP

"Your honour, I am making an unusual request for the first time in my life today. I pray your honour that my client be hanged."

There was pin drop silence in the courtroom as Shrii Nageshvarji went on.

"In each and every age, people have been crucified for the service they have done to humanity, standing up for Dharma and righteousness — Christ was crucified and Socrates was killed because they stood for the truth. In the same way, Mr. Sarkar, my client, will be worshipped only after He is hanged.

Hang my ailing client who can't even stand up. Do not even wait for Him to recover. Take the stretcher to the gallows and hang Him. That's what the government wants.
