Only by His death will His greatness be realised. Hence I do not want mercy for my client. My lord, He should be hanged by you yourself, so that history will also remember you.

GAP BETWEEN CUP AND LIP

I won't say anything more."

Shrii Nageshvarji told us that he spoke for fifteen to twenty minutes and then sat on his chair. What next?

The judge went into his chambers and changed the doctored order from the CBI for Baba to be hanged into a sentence of life imprisonment. Then he came and announced the judgement.

The rest is history.

BEING BLESSED WITH TB

When the Emergency was lifted, I returned to my posting in Vellore after eighteen months of physical and mental suffering. I ran the school there in a rented building for over a year. To my deep anguish, Ananda Marga again became the object of criticism in the public eye and I now had to work in a very hostile environment.

During the Emergency, a sweet relationship had developed among our monks, margis and the members of the opposition parties who were also imprisoned with us. These leaders had closely observed the conduct of Baba's disciples and they promised to give justice to Ananda Marga if they came to power.

They came to power after the Emergency was lifted; Morarji Desai became prime minister. Many incidents occurred, however, in which margis and workers were blamed for violent attacks in different parts of the world. One such famous case was the Hilton bombing in Australia. Three Australian margis were convicted of the crime, only to be found innocent after many years in jail. Further investigation revealed the pre-knowledge of the Australian intelligence agencies in this case. This and other cases caused further controversy. The new Indian government was also under pressure from the Soviet Union (as revealed in Soviet files later) and so turned against Ananda Marga. Workers who had suffered so much during Emergency, were anguished to find that the vilification and assaults on Ananda Marga still continued. It was another testing time for Ananda Marga.

The heartache was compounded by Baba's firm stance. After being acquitted and freed by the High Court, Baba refused to leave the jail. He refused to come out until the cases of His co-accused were decided in the High Court and they were released. His argument was that He was not a criminal and had done no wrong and so, He should come out with honour.

BEING BLESSED WITH TB

In Vellore, I also became a victim, psychologically. I received no warm welcome from my old friends in the town who had been so near and dear to me. I then started going to the burial ground every night for kapalika sadhana. I used to look at the stars in the sky and pray to Baba, "Baba, please come out and everything will be alright." This was my prayer every night to Baba.

At that time I was running a school with four children on the outskirts of the small city of Vellore. I had a very tough time financially. Acintanandji became sick and so did I. I was diagnosed with tuberculosis. When the news of Baba's acquittal came, I went to Patna to see my Baba.

Our central office in Patna was in a rented building at Kalpana market. One morning, I accompanied Acarya Ramananda Avadhuta to the jail gate. I did not go inside the jail, feeling that since Baba was physically weak, if I went there, He might contract this disease from me. Ramanandji used to go to the jail every morning. Baba asked Dada whether there was any person outside the jail. Ramanandji told Baba about my health and the reason I was not willing to come inside and see Him. Baba asked Dada to convey the following message to me:

  1. Ask Vandanananda to take liquids during fasting

BEING BLESSED WITH TB

  1. Tell him to stop the medicine. Baba is coming out of jail soon and will then give medicine.

I immediately threw out all the medicine that the doctor had given me for the next one year.

When Baba came out of jail on August 2, 1978 I was blessed to be present there. A sea of humanity had gathered on the road to see Baba. The feelings of all of us who remembered those dark days—when we thought Baba would never come out; when so many disciples abandoned Him; when sometimes doubts would arise about Baba and the future of His mission; the feelings of margis jailed during Emergency while their families struggled to survive—all of this exploded into shouts of "Param Pita Baba ki Jai." For our Father was victorious and can there be a greater joy for His children than this? The entire roadway from the jail up to Baba's quarters in Pataliputra was packed with people—one could not move. Countless non-margis had assembled to have a glimpse of this mysterious personality who had defied the government and won. I thank Baba again and again for blessing me with the hardship that caused me to get TB so that I was graced with the chance to see this glorious Liila (Divine Drama) of our Baba finally becoming free.

Baba's co-accused—Acarya Satyanandaji, Acarya Sarveshvaranandaji, Barun Kumar and Pavitra Kumar—were acquitted by the Patna High Court and He was honourably released along with them. This was a day of joy that cannot be forgotten ever. It was unbelievable to think that just two years earlier Baba had been sentenced to life in prison and was so weak due to more than three years of liquid fast on just two cups a day. Many had thought He would die in jail, spending His last days there. We disciples had our own doubts as well. When Baba came out, all the doubt, grief, and agony of waiting exploded into a celebration of the triumph of Dharma.

THE WEAK ARE FEARFUL

I stayed in a place near Kalpana Market in the Pataliputra area of Baba's city of Patna that He blessed with His liila (jail austerities for the sake of humanity). Baba was to come and address the gathering of devotees near His quarters. Everything at that time was so spiritually high. And why should it not be? After all, Baba had come out after a long ordeal in jail.

That same evening, when I was face to face with Baba while He was entering into His room in quarters, He questioned me. "Vandananda, you are in Vellore?"

"Yes, Baba," I replied.

Thereafter I left for Vellore. When Baba came out of jail, programs were scheduled in Delhi, Kolkata, Chennai and Mumbai — the four metropolitan cities. We all were very happy. He was going to stay for an extended period of time in each city.

THE WEAK ARE FEARFUL

I also went to Chennai to meet Baba. Baba called a meeting of all wholetime workers (Jiivan Dani). Baba's reasoning was that "meeting them first lightens My heart." Baba was full of longing to shower love on all His workers many of whom He had not seen for many years. However, He knew many of us were worried because of the mistakes we had made during this period when we had been outside the organisational flow. So Baba called for RDS so that after some scolding, after He consoled us and made us laugh - then we would be ready for Him to shower His love upon us.

In this regard, Baba called a meeting in Chennai. Everyone had questions in their minds. RDS was being conducted after a long gap of seven years. It was indeed a blessing. It was the review of our own actions and samskaras. There were plenty of mistakes to be uncovered. With some fear and happiness everyone was sitting there.

All-knowing Baba's eyes were looking at everyone. The environment was a little silent. Suddenly one Brahmachari Dada (Acarya Prabhakar) who was posted in Andhra Pradesh asked Baba a question.

"Baba, people are afraid of Ananda Marga. There is a lot of problem in doing pracar (ideological propagation) work."

THE WEAK ARE FEARFUL

Baba did not normally receive such questions at RDS. I had not seen such things before. What happened? Baba in return asked us to answer this question.

He said, "Could someone from among you give the answer to this?"

No one said anything. I raised my hand. Baba said, "Good! You can answer."

I said, "Baba those who are weak, they are afraid of Ananda Marga. They know that Ananda Marga is a good institution while they are weak, so they are afraid to join it. Hence they oppose it."

THE WEAK ARE FEARFUL

Baba replied, "This is the answer."

He asked Acharya Prabahkar, "Have you understood?" Then the RDS started.

While in jail, Baba was asked a similar question. As published in the August 1977 Crimson Dawn, Baba said:

There are many persons who dislike Ananda Marga. It is like the story of the owl. Do you know the owl? The owl is a bird that lives in darkness. When the sun comes out it gets very angry, it ruffles its feathers and can't be found. And when the night time comes, it becomes more content. In the same way, persons working against Ananda Marga get afraid when the sun comes out and do everything they can to keep it from coming out. But they cannot do it. These persons only want to live in darkness and they will rebel against the sun. Do you see what the work of Ananda Marga is, my boy?"

TEARS WORK WONDERS

How happy I was to again get more time to walk with my Baba. As mentioned before, Baba came to Chennai on November 27, 1978 and stayed at Shrii Shiva Bhagwan Goenka's house on Venus Road. At this point, I must admit that I rented a room and started staying there. I had vacated the building in the Vellore suburbs where I used to run the school. So I was very happy and also very fearful when I came to know that Baba was coming to Chennai, thinking that He would certainly take me to task for this failure. I had many times violated the rules meant for workers and I knew it fully well. Anyway, I along with Shrii Pattabhiraman, whom I had initiated, went to Chennai to see our Baba. As was His usual practice, He called a workers meeting. Baba kept silent about the wrong deeds that we had been doing for the last several years. Then the acarya interrupted Baba as told in the previous story.

However, there was more to come in this meeting. Baba asked if there was any worker present who did not have any school. I stood up. There was some consternation among the workers, as they all knew that I had no margis, no dharmacakra, no jagriti, etc. Slowly Baba surveyed me and scanned me up and down. Then He asked,

"You are the seniormost among all here?" He then kept silent for some time.

Then He asked about the children's home, the jagriti, the bhukti pradhan. The answer was always the same — zero. Then looking at me, Baba said, "This is not good. You are the senior among all present here and your performance is so poor. Ramananda, he should go back to the place of his posting and come back to see Me only when he has started the school. I don't want to see him anymore."

TEARS WORK WONDERS

Now my mind was in turmoil. I had not expected that I would not be allowed to stay in Chennai and see Baba during the week that Baba was there. I pleaded with Baba that I would start after this week but it was all to no avail. Baba was firm. The first reporting session was over.

This became the topic of discussion in everyone's mouth. Goenkaji, his family and all the other margis agreed that Baba would be told that they would take the responsibility to start a school in Vellore but that Dada Vandanananda should be allowed to stay for DMC. This was because I was very close to them and also after so long Baba had come down to South India and they felt no one should be deprived of His darshan (blessed contact with the Lord).

This plea was given through Baba's PA, Ramanandji, but to no avail. The margis gave me some money and with a heavy heart and tears in my eyes, I left the place. The place of my posting was just a three-hour bus ride away. I reached there around two in the afternoon. I went straight to my room, dropped off my luggage and set forth mindlessly to the local bus stand. I got on the first bus that came. All the while I was thinking about Baba, feverishly absorbed in the feeling that whatever good or bad things Baba had said, my mind and eyes were throbbing with deep spiritual feelings. After a few minutes, I alighted from the bus and followed the street for a short while. I stopped at the door of a house. Hanging in front was the nameplate with the words "Subramaniam, Superintendent Post Office." I knocked at the door. One teenage girl came out. I asked to meet Mr Subramaniam. She replied that this was not possible as he was working in a place that was three hundred miles away.

I was trying to compose myself when from behind me a young lady walked up and entered the house. She summoned me to come inside. She then told the girl that she should have first requested the swami (me) to take a comfortable chair in the room and offered some fruit. She then asked the girl to bring fruit.

TEARS WORK WONDERS

Then she enquired about me, why I was there in this street and in this town. I replied, "Ma, I am looking for a building where I can start a school." It turned out that she was the head teacher in a government school. She was very happy to know that I was there to start an English school in her area. She immediately led me to 25 Sainathpuram where there was a building that had been vacant for eighteen years and was under renovation. The woman thought the owner would be happy to rent it out for a school.

I surveyed the building. I liked it. It had a boundary wall and some coconut trees. The woman was very helpful and I approved the building. Then arose the question of how to meet the owner. When I met the agent of the owner I was told that the owner stayed around twelve km away. To my surprise, this generous lady asked her daughter to take me to the owner's residence.

We boarded the bus and I was lost in wonder at how this lady to whom I was a complete stranger had asked her daughter to take me to a place completely unknown to both of us. I was in a deep and exalted state of bliss and could barely comprehend what was floating by in front of me. When we reached the house, the deal was settled after a few minutes discussion for the rent of Rs. 100 per month. I paid in advance, took the key and came back to prepare the school. When we reached the kind lady's house, she was very happy that the arrangements had been made for such a low price and also in her street.

My mind and heart were jumping for sheer joy feeling the bliss of how Baba was helping me. Later in the evening, the mother asked if there was anything else that I needed. I thought that now that I had a building, I must admit some children. The idea had just come into my mind and out of my mouth when the mother said that she had a third child and that child would be the first student of my school. She then went to three more houses and brought Rs. 60 from each house as admission fees. That same evening four students were admitted.

TEARS WORK WONDERS

I thought of a signboard. I went to the market and bought some paint and the elder daughter prepared the signboard within a few hours. The next morning with the full faith of that family I left for Chennai. The school had been opened by Baba's outright miracle and I was lost in wonder and bliss.

I reached Chennai around noon the next day where I witnessed a strange drama. At the entrance of the building where Baba was staying, many workers met me and asked about my progress. They were very surprised to see me the next day. I directly went to the room of Baba's PA. Ramanandji said that most of the time while

I was gone, Baba was repeatedly saying, “I am not feeling good. Normally, on such an occasion, everyone should be present. But I had to send Vandanananda to Vellore. What to do? He is a senior worker. He had not started a school. He is capable and he will do it.”

When Baba heard my voice, He immediately asked Ramanandji, “Who? Vandanananda has come? Why has he come?”

TEARS WORK WONDERS

Ramanandji replied, “Baba, Vandanananda says that he has started a school.”

Baba asked, “Has he rented the building?”

“Yes, Baba,” I replied.

“Children’s admission?”

TEARS WORK WONDERS

“Yes, Baba. Four students and one more to be admitted.”

“What about the receipt?”

“Yes, Baba.”

“What about the signboard?”

TEARS WORK WONDERS

“Yes, Baba, it is done.”

Baba then said, “Ramananda! By Vandanananda’s grace, the school has been opened.”

I said, “Baba, by Your Grace it was done. I was simply moving and you were making me move.”

I did sastaunga pranama (full prostration) to my Baba.

TEARS WORK WONDERS

Oh, I was so very happy. How can I express it to you readers?

I must also not fail to record a most important event during this DMC. All five Goenka brothers—Shiv Bhagwan Goenka, Omprakash Goenka, Bharat Goenka, Sita Ram Goenka and Sushil Goenka—had their marriages performed as per Ananda Marga system in their home, where Baba was staying.

They had already been married before joining Ananda Marga and now they were taking the Ananda Marga marriage vows during their middle age. So many margis, dadas and even their own children witnessed their wedding and witnessed Baba blessing them. After the marriages, there was a procession. It passed through the street leading to the front of Goenka’s house. Baba came out of His room to see the procession. Baba was lovingly seated on a chair and the procession changed its course and went through the passage that passed besides the house. The distance was hardly fifteen feet between the procession and Baba’s chair. Beloved Baba was witnessing the whole procession. Whomsoever happened to come near to Baba’s chair offered their namaskar and a few did sastaunga pranama (full prostration) also.

On a number of occasions I requested Ramanandji to ask Baba about the TB medicine that Baba had mentioned in jail. The chance however did not arise. Once or twice, Dada tried to say something but Baba did not pay any attention and changed the subject of the conversation. I thought that the time had not come for Baba giving medicine for my TB.

TEARS WORK WONDERS

I must mention that Shrii Pattabhiraman from Vellore was also there at that DMC. When he came out of Baba’s room after getting Personal Contact (PC) with Baba, he was weeping like a small child though he was sixty years old. After some time, when he calmed down, he took me to an empty room and told me what had happened in his PC.

He had lost his father as a small child. He did not remember his father’s face or his affection. That was a great loss for him and all his life he was internally hankering for that affection. When he did sastaunga pranama to Baba, the very first words Baba said were, “My little son, get up.” When he got up, Baba asked him to sit on His lap. Baba said, “I am your father, My son. You embrace Me and love Me as much as you like.” He began to embrace Baba shyly, but then, holding on to Baba with all his heart, he began weeping. This old man was sitting in the lap of his Father who was younger than him and both were one and are still one today. Mr Pattabhira-

man at this time was actively involved in work for Ananda Marga and later helped to buy a printing press. He died as an ideal son of Baba indeed.

GHOSTS BRING BABA'S BLESSING

While working in Vellore to develop the school, I moved around the area to enroll children in the school. I did not experience much difficulty. The kind lady who had helped me in renting the school building also accompanied me and in a single session eighteen students were enrolled.

The building had no water connection from the municipal authority, since it had been unoccupied for so long.

Our Baba will never let me sit idle as He Himself was busy all the time saving humanity. Guru is He who sets an example by action. The principle that He demonstrated was, "Empower yourself with sadhana and inspire others with your actions." He showed that it is struggle that make a man strong. Without struggle nothing can be achieved. "Break the hurdles with a strong blow from your feet." This is what He told us, and He Himself never compromised the spirit of struggle.

When I started the school at Sainathpuram, I didn't realize that the area had a lot of Christians or that there was already an existing school that was Christian. Through social service work and through the high quality of the Christian Medical College, many people had become Christian.

GHOSTS BRING BABA'S BLESSING

The day I started the school, the opposition also started. The most interesting fact was that the building I was renting belonged to a Christian and it had a cross in the front of the building. It was hidden under my school signboard.

One winter night, opponents removed my signboards that were displayed in various places in the town. One evening, RS Acarya Sujyananda and I were confronted by a goonda who was feared by the entire city. We were standing at a local bus stand near our school. The goonda came and started pushing me around. He grabbed my beard with his left hand. Forcefully, I grabbed his hand

and with a jerk removed it from my beard. He raised his right hand and Sujyanandaji caught it. And then in the presence of the public, we both kicked him many times. The local police came and on my complaint arrested him. That gave a strong signal to the people—Christian and non-Christian—that we are there and will remain strong.

What was the result? We worked harder and eighty-nine students were admitted shortly afterwards. Many students from the Christian school joined our school. Many important personalities like the MLA and Minister G. Vishvanathan and Mr. G Krishnamurthy and intellectual and spiritual people helped me very much. I am very thankful to those who not only financially but also socially and politically supported me later during Baba's visit.

GHOSTS BRING BABA'S BLESSING

But the clash was not only outside but also inside the house itself as well. In the past, if by chance any family tried to rent the building, they would vacate it after a few days. At the back of the main building, there used to be a servants quarter, which had been demolished just one month earlier. It was filled with stones and dirt. When I was alone in the night, I used to hear the sounds of stamping feet or screaming in another room. Sometimes I used to go to that room and would find nothing. Still, the idea that there might be a ghost or spirit did not enter my mind. I liked the building and enjoyed the tranquility of the area.

Nevertheless, many ideas started haunting my mind as to why these sounds were being heard. Why had the building not been occupied for eighteen years? Why at night did a big black cat come and start circling around me, only to run away when chased? The following days became more challenging, intriguing and enjoyable for me. Upon enquiring from the neighbour, I came to know that two persons had committed suicide there—one by drowning herself in the well and the other by hanging herself from the ceiling of the servants' quarters. To remove all traces of this tragedy, the well had been filled in and the quarters had been demolished. The rent for that main building with a boundary wall and two coconut trees should have been not less than Rs. 500. However I was being charged only Rs. 100. This fact only added to my curiosity.

Once Acarya Keshavananda, who was then the ES (ERAWS Secretary), came there to stay for a night. We were doing meditation in the late evening in a room after having prepared sweets, paratha (fried, flat bread), rice and vegetables. We had kept the food in the kitchen.

I had told the stories about the night events to Dada but he did not believe in these kinds of stories. We had just sat down for sadhana when all of a sudden a banging sound was heard in the kitchen. Leaving meditation in fear for our food, we rushed there together. To our surprise we found that half of the food we had prepared was missing. Dada left the next morning after warning me.

GHOSTS BRING BABA'S BLESSING

For some nights I slept dressed in full kapalika dress, with two skulls above my head and below my legs. I enjoyed the struggle with the forces in that house. After some time these mysterious night events disappeared and I became fully engaged in increasing the number of students in my school.

When I reached the school late one evening, at around 11 p.m., I saw that the light in the building was on, which surprised me. How come the light was on? When I knocked on the door, a Didi answered. I asked her how she had been able to enter the building and why she had come without informing me about her arrival, so that I could have made alternative arrangements for her stay. She replied that the centre had asked her to visit and start a girls' school.

I replied that she still should have informed me about her arrival. Thereafter I collected my apparatus for kapalika sadhana and a blanket and left for the cremation ground. It was a winter night and I did sadhana there the whole night. Later I found out that this Didi behaved inappropriately and ended up leaving renunciate life and married. I then realized what a grace from Baba I had received.

On May 18, 1982, Ananda Purnima (Baba's Birthday) DMC was held in Varanasi. Baba was staying in the Ajay Hotel. At that time, I was fully absorbed in spiritual vibrations. The late, great Parashivanandaji, Acintanandaji and Prasannanandaji and myself were staying together in a hall meant for workers.

GHOSTS BRING BABA'S BLESSING

On the evening of the DMC, we were sitting together, unmindful of any routine or announcement, just absorbed with our yearning hearts, waiting for our Baba. Baba came and graced the sacred stage. Then the DMC program began.

When the time for prize distribution arrived, the winning RS, DS,

Bhukti Pradhan, etc, were requested to be near the stage so that they could reach Baba in the shortest amount of time to receive their prizes and thus avoid any embarrassing delays.

When the name of the best DS (diocese secretary) was announced, Baba immediately expressed His displeasure and sternly told the ES that the selection was wrong. The entire gathering of devotees was silent, observing the situation with awestruck eyes. Everyone's attention was especially focused on Baba.

GHOSTS BRING BABA'S BLESSING

Baba sought an explanation from the person in charge. No one could say anything.

Then Baba said that He knew that Vandanananda had done good work as DS and he should be given the prize.

Parashivanandaji pushed me forward. I ran fast and in the exaltation of joy, I received the prize from Baba's holy hands.

What my venerable Baba whispered to me so that I alone could hear was a special blessing for me. I still feel that I was, and that I am, His chosen one.

GHOSTS BRING BABA'S BLESSING

This is how Baba loves, by finding the unique aspects of each person, each samaja, and inspiring, arousing them to fight and serve, blessing and intoxicating them all with His mamata. Mamata is His possessive love that says that every one of us is His alone, which gives each of us the ironclad confidence and determination that "He is mine alone and no one else's." Baba loves it so much when we, like small children, feel that Baba is ours alone. Baba does not like it when we then behave with a superiority complex with our brothers and sisters. But Baba has said also He does not like the goody-goody jinani (intellectual) idea that "He belongs to everyone." So how to love Him as our private dearest Darling and at the same time not mistreat others? Baba says that a real devotee knows at the most only two people exist — myself and my Beloved. A great devotee experiences Him only. This is the path Baba has laid before us.

THE DHARMA OF HUMAN BEINGS

Baba came to Ernakulam in July 15, 1979. He stayed with Mr. Gopalakrishnan. Shrii Gopalkrishnan was the district judge of Ernakulam (Kerala). He and his wife were great devotees. Whenever Baba came to Ernakulam, Baba stayed with them. This time also Baba had decided to place His Holy Feet in their residence.

I was attending the RDS. The moment He reached Ernakulam, Baba asked to hold RDS with the workers. His explanation for why RDS should be done first before DMC was that He wanted that all the tension (of worrying about scolding in RDS) should be dissipated at the time of DMC.

All-knowing Baba used to punish every mistake we made. If anyone had committed any serious violation, the memory of that mistake would keep throbbing in his mind. So to heal people from this kind of psychological illness, Baba used to call the RDS at the beginning of the DMC.

I was the seniormost among all present. It was my second year as DS and principal of a school in Vellore. I had been able to collect eighteen children. Baba asked which worker's school was "sick." Sick meant that a school had less than fifty students. I stood up, since I was first in the row on Baba's right side. Baba said He would deal with me last and asked me to come to the left side.

THE DHARMA OF HUMAN BEINGS

Baba scanned everyone present in the RDS one by one. He caned no one. Then He summoned me to stand in front of Him. He asked me how many students were in my school. When I answered eighteen, Baba asked why the school was not healthy. I told Baba that I had done my best. Baba replied that I was the seniormost worker present. Then Baba asked me to raise both my hands. He caned me thrice on my left side of my back, thrice on my right side. Then Baba asked me to stand straight in front of him. He asked me to

come closer and rubbed my chest with His right hand two or three times.

Then Baba said, "This is the TB medicine I have for you."

This medicine He had assured He would give to cure me of the TB that I had contracted while in Vellore. Even now the scar on my lung is still there. Doctors I met during that time were surprised that I had TB and was not taking any medicine. Once a doctor in Pune prescribed a TB medicine but my body reacted so strongly to it that I had to stop taking it. What a miracle of Baba!

THE DHARMA OF HUMAN BEINGS

But at that time, none of these considerations could touch my mind. The blessings of Baba were pouring down — all over me and in every part of me. I became lost and Baba went back into His room.

On the morning of the DMC, when Baba was coming back from morning walk, He suddenly stopped at the door of His room. He turned back as he entered the door, I stood behind him. Acharya Vijayananda Dada and Shri Gopalkrishnan were also in the queue behind Baba.

As Baba was just about to enter the room, he looked at his watch, turned back with a smile and asked a question, "What is the Dharma of human beings?"

His attractive style of walking, talking and His smiling face locked our eyes upon Him. Yet no one had the answer to this mysterious question. Then he indicated that Vijayananda Dada should answer the question. But Dada kept quiet. Who will answer?

THE DHARMA OF HUMAN BEINGS

I think Baba has many times enjoyed these pleasant moments. Baba often creates such a complex situation by asking very simple but mysterious questions. Then after heightening our curiosity at the mystery, He would explain so many complex issues with extreme simplicity and profundity. We came to know the answer to such small marvels by the grace of He who was the greatest mystery. When Vijayananda Dada was quiet, Baba motioned for me to answer it. I also kept quiet. Then he asked Gopalkrishnanji who also kept quiet. He simply kept on gazing at Baba with devotion.

Then Baba spoke in English. "To please the Parama Purusa is the Dharma of the human being." And gesturing with His hands, He asked if we understood.

Then Baba turned and went back to His room.

The characteristics, what is Dharma — I have read so many articles on it. But it was the first time I had listened to such a simple, comprehensive, practical explanation. This left an everlasting imprint in my mind. Pleasing Parama Purusa is the greatest mystery and the greatest beauty and the real Dharma of human life.

TAKING CARE OF BABA

It was November 8, 1979 that Baba came to Coimbatore, Tamil Nadu to bless the land with DMC. I was posted as DS Vellore. Baba's PA, Acarya Ramananda, was very stressed. Perhaps this was because he felt mental pressure and scolding from Baba for matters that he believed were not his concern. In frustration he told me to "take care of Baba's needs!" Sadly, many say they want to be close to Baba but when the time comes to take full responsibility for completely caring for all Baba's needs and work, they run away or they get clash and become reacted. This is natural for human beings but it is unnatural and a shameful stain on the honour of a devotee. Once in Jail (July 1978 issue of Crimson Dawn), a margi asked Baba how to bring Him out of jail? Baba replied:

I am your Baba —
I am your Guardian;
You are My mini-Babas,
You are My Guardians.

So when PA Dada told me to "take care of Baba's needs," I accepted with joy the duty of attending to Baba's needs. When it was nearing time for Baba to sleep that night, He asked me to handle the problem of where to place the cooler. The cooler is a machine in India into which one puts water and then a big fan blows cool air into the room. There was a cooler placed adjoining the windows of the room where Baba was staying in the house of Shrii Rajgopalanji. A small table was kept in front of the cooler.

The problem was Baba wanted to keep His personal belongings that were needed in the night on the table. Baba put His glasses, pen, small notebook and napkin and arranged them as to priority of need. Since the cooler's fan was so strong, the items on the

TAKING CARE OF BABA

table were getting disturbed. Baba asked me to solve this problem. I put Baba's glasses, notebook and small napkin on top of one another and asked Baba if this was all right.

Baba's reaction was immediate. He asked me why was I rearranging His articles, since He had arranged them as per His choice and need. As a rule, Baba arranged his personal belongings meticulously in a specific place in the room and suitcase so that He could find them even in the dark. He wanted to finish His personal routine quickly so that He could spend twenty hours solely on organizing service to humanity. Baba sternly said that I should not disturb this. Then Baba asked if the speed of the cooler could be adjusted or not. I was able to solve the problem by turning the cooler a little to the side.

Many ask, why does Baba behave in this way? The fact is that until there is absolute adherence to Ista (Ideational Desideratum; Beloved), then He cannot bestow the full force of His grace. Complete surrender of one's personal will is the beginning of what is called in Sanskrit prapatti. This culminates in absolute surrender—body, mind and soul. Only such a sadhaka can be blessed with the intense power of His grace and His love. Although Baba wants to love us with all His infinite love, He is not able to fully love most people until they surrender, and so He creates such dramatic situations over small things so as to goad His disciples onto the path of prapatti. As Baba says:

In prapatti the idea that “everything is being done by His wish, that it is by His grace that He is using me as a tool, that He may or may not use me as a tool, but ‘His will be done,’” predominates in one’s mind. To get the full joy of prapatti, one has to surrender completely unto Him. (“The Culmination of Devotion”, Ánanda Vacanámrtam Part 1)

TAKING CARE OF BABA

Later in his room, my Baba enquired about my health, particularly my stomach. He asked me to take papaya and said it was good for me.

Actually, the day before I left Vellore, I had taken papaya with milk late in the evening and my stomach was disturbed, so I immediately replied, "Baba, papaya does not suit me."

"You must have eaten it after sunset."

"Yes, Baba," I said.

TAKING CARE OF BABA

"Papaya is good for stomach if taken in the morning. Its good effect on the body increases as the sun rises in the sky. Its positive effect gradually declines as the sun sets in the sky. After sunset, it is poisonous for you. Don't take it then." Since then I have never been taking papaya after sunset.

At the end of the DMC, Baba was to depart for Hyderabad after having lunch. Baba was inevitably the cynosure of all hearts during DMC. At that time of departure, the entire family of Rajgopalanji, especially the wife and daughters, were gathered around Baba, hoping and seeking for any opportunity to be close to Him. The sisters thought it the best time to come close to Baba when He was served food. The entire atmosphere was not organizational but like a family celebration.

Baba was sitting on His cot, ready to have lunch. He was indeed in a joyous mood. All Baba's daughters had cleverly planned that each item of the lunch was to be brought to Baba by a separate person so that all could enjoy being with their Baba.

Acarya Ramananda was in a disturbed and distracted mood and did not monitor what was happening in Baba's room. He told me that I should take care of this. When I entered Baba's room, so many people were there, crowding around Baba. I knew Baba was uneasy because so many people were surrounding Him, possibly expecting so many things from Him.

TAKING CARE OF BABA

I first did sastaunga pranama (full prostration), since it was time for Baba to leave that day. When I stood up and approached Baba, He shouted at me, "Is this the time to do sastaunga?"

His voice was so loud, they all left Baba's room. Then Baba's mood changed dramatically. He said that this was good and then finished His meal in peace. On many other occasions, Baba would scold and punish me for the mistakes of new workers under my supervision. When I would be beaten, the junior worker who was actually guilty would feel very bad and ask Baba that they should be beaten, not me, because they were not able to fulfill the target. In this way, Baba would rectify junior workers without directly punishing them.

Similarly in this instance, I was scolded to make everyone leave. Actually Baba did not like people around Him while He would take his meal, because they might have a conscious or unconscious desire to enjoy the same food, and as Parama Purusa He would know this and could not eat in peace. Also many times people would serve Baba and stand around hoping to have their worldly or other desires fulfilled. In this instance, some of the relatives of Rajgopalan were also there and perhaps might have had some expectation of getting his blessings. This is my feeling.

This is another aspect of surrender. Many devotees want to be with their Baba but when they are blessed with being close to Him, they behave as if He were an ordinary member of their family. But Baba is not this; Baba is the heart of our hearts. A devotee should see who He is in reality and have the devotional ardour and yearning for Him. Then alone can He fully bless them. Otherwise, by behaving with Baba in a worldly way, we are not honouring Him in our heart, nor are we filled with the urgency that arises from the consciousness of being blessed to spend time with the Lord of Lords. It all boils down to not taking Baba (our dearest One) for granted, as has been done since the very day Baba came onto this earth.

TAKING CARE OF BABA

When Baba was leaving the house of Rajgopalanji, we were there waiting and watching. Baba said to Rajgopalanji as he left his room, "I have consecrated this room spiritually. You must maintain it." Later Rajgopalanji used to always meditate in this room and preserved its sanctity.

VELLORE DMC – EPIC LIILA

Blissful Telegram

I lived then in the inimitable world of Tamil culture with, as Baba says, the oldest language (over three thousand years old), the birthplace of the bhakti movement, the centre of the ancient Dravidian civilization that Baba said was the highest culture in ancient India, the centre of devotion to Lord Shiva and a centre of the movement against casteism and Hindi imperialism in modern times. Baba had said that Tamils are natural devotees and this is what history shows, as there have been countless great devotional songwriters in this land. North Indians generally know only such Tamil saints as Ramana Maharishi who lived 150 kilometres from Vellore, but there are countless towns and villages where innumerable yogis like Thirumular and devotees like Nammalvar have lived.

I worked with great zeal, trying to be a devotee, and met a good number of people including visitors from other states. I met academics, religious authorities, social thinkers and politicians. Although the language was a big hurdle for me, I enjoyed Tamil food, culture and the great hospitality of the people.

I started one more school at Abdullahpuram, a village on the outskirts of Vellore. My school became healthy (i.e. had more than fifty students) as per Baba's criteria. Many more villages were willing to provide me accommodation to start a school, which I politely declined due to lack of staff.

In the year 1980, Baba told me to visit Pondicherry and start a centre there. It is a five to six hour journey from Vellore. I thought many times to go there but somehow could not find the time. One day, I made up my mind early in the morning to go. It was five in the morning. The first bus out of town that went directly to Pondicherry would depart at six, so I was getting ready. I was putting

Blissful Telegram

on my turban when someone knocked at the door. I opened the gate. It was a telegram from our central dharma pracar secretary, Acarya Tapeshvarananda, sent from Delhi where Baba was.

The telegram read, "DMC will be held in Vellore. Baba is reaching Chennai on the eighteenth. Send Rs. 14,000 for air tickets." I was overjoyed but surprised also. I thought it might be a hoax but whatever the case, O how I loved the message of that telegram! I took off my turban, sat on the bed and started thinking about this possible visit of Baba to Vellore. I knew the preconditions required for a place to be declared as capable of hosting a DMC: a healthy school, healthy children's home, printing press, newspaper in the local language, own jagriti (meditation centre). My school was healthy with seventy-nine students. With a joyful heart, I went to the post office on a bicycle and sent a telegram.

"I welcome Baba to Vellore for DMC. Trying to arrange the money for air ticket."

I moved fast, contacting Shrii Shiva Bhagwan Goenka of Chennai on the phone from the post office. He told me, "Yes, Dada, the DMC will be held in Vellore but the Rs 14,000 for the ticket requested by the centre should be arranged."

Blissful Telegram

That was indeed a thunderbolt that struck my head—having to arrange such a large sum. Nevertheless the money had to be arranged for our DMC to be held. I went back to my place and again I planned to go to Pondicherry the next morning. Accordingly, I got up early in the morning and started for the bus station. I received another telegram.

"DMC in Vellore is canceled if you do not immediately send Rs. 14,000."

I, as any ordinary person, became a bit thick-skulled. I purchased my ticket and boarded the bus. After the bus pulled out, I fell asleep. Baba appeared in my dream.

In the dream, Baba said, "I am coming to Vellore and you are leaving Vellore. Will you not welcome me?" When I woke up, the bus had halted about thirty kilometers from Vellore. I collected my luggage and alighted from the bus. I caught another bus for Vellore and went back to my school.

Blissful Telegram

I again contacted S. B. Goenka to know of any further developments in his thinking. He told me that he had arranged for the money to be delivered to Delhi and that our Baba was coming to Chennai on the eighteenth. Baba was to go to Vellore by road on the nineteenth and the DMC was to be held on the twentieth. Baba would then be back in Chennai on the twenty-first.

I went to Chennai and informed the margis of South India that Baba was coming to Vellore. Shrii Rajgopalan of Coimbatore came to Vellore with an air cooler and stayed in a hotel.

I met the editors of two daily newspapers published in Vellore. I had met them many times earlier and they were my good friends. The news that Baba was coming and would be received with the tandava dance was published on the front page.

The news alerted the Tamil Nadu and Vellore governments. They immediately came to my school where I was staying. I was summoned by the senior superintendent of police and the magistrate of Vellore. They wanted to know the authenticity of the news published in the daily newspaper.

Blissful Telegram

I replied that the news was correct.

"So you will perform tandava?" they asked.

"Yes," I said. Then I explained to them why we perform tandava and the science behind it.

"Look, Mr. Acarya, you cannot do that."

Blissful Telegram

I asked why. They said that the central government had instructed them that performing tandava in public was prohibited. The argument became intense and I left their office saying that no power on earth could stop me from doing tandava.

The next morning when I came out of the school building, a paper from the district magistrate was pasted on our gate. It read: "144 has been promulgated in the city and if anyone violates it, they will be arrested."

One forty-four referred to Section 144 of the Indian Penal Code that banned the assembly of five or more persons with a dangerous weapon. In tandava, one uses a knife or trishula to represent the

courageous force of goodness. Along with Acarya Sarvatmananda, I went to the district magistrate and questioned him about the order. We entered into a heated argument in which we told them straightaway "How dare you try to stop us!"

Blissful Telegram

In the meantime, many dadas and didis had already arrived. The two places I had booked for them now refused to admit them. The district authorities had threatened the owners. By this time, many margis had also come to Vellore and they booked private hotels. The dadas and didis came to live with them.

Baba reached Chennai by air from Delhi. Goenkaji then briefed Him about the government order and promulgation of Article 144 in the city.

Working with some margis, we booked Hotel Ranga, the best hotel in the city. The hotel faced the largest field in the city, with the capacity to hold around five hundred thousand people. From the hotel, I called Baba's PA, Ramanandaji, and told him about the situation. I could hear Baba's venerable voice in the background in Chennai.

I heard Baba asking Dada to ask me whether the arrangement for His stay could be made in our school or not. I replied that this was not a good idea since Baba would face a lot of inconvenience there. Baba then asked where His stay had been arranged. I replied that it was in a hotel. Baba said this was all right because it was private; the government would not be able to readily interfere on private premises. But Baba insisted that alternative arrangements should be made in the school also.

Vellore Namaskar Blessing

Everything was moving smoothly. By mid-afternoon of the nineteenth (a Saturday) more than eight thousand people, mostly from the city, had gathered to catch a glimpse of Baba's grandeur. They wanted to see who had caused such commotion in their city. There were also five hundred police with rifles all around the grounds and the main road to stop the tandava dance if performed. The magistrate and SSP (Senior Superintendent of Police) were present. I was being shadowed by police.

Suddenly, news that Baba's car could be seen coming, created a big commotion. I rushed towards Baba's car and escorted it to the gate of the hotel. Baba came out and asked me,

"Is everything alright?"

"Yes, Baba," I replied. All assembled margis and dadas and didis joyfully shouted slogans about the glory of Baba. I guided Baba up to the first floor room. The staircase leading to the first floor was outside the building and everyone outside was blessed with the bliss of seeing their Most Adored One climb the stairs.

Vellore Namaskar Blessing

To heighten the drama and the thrill of the devotees, Baba stopped halfway up the staircase and with folded hands greeted the gathering. With a smile of such sweet joy, Baba said,

"I don't think that My arrival to your city has brought you a lot of trouble?" Then Baba gave one of His beautiful namaskars that melts the mind of everyone. Baba's namaskar was so charming that even some policemen put their rifles down on the ground and did namaskar to Baba.

When Baba entered the room, He asked Ramanandaji to leave. Then He asked me to close the door. Baba sat on His cot and I on the ground, gazing up at Him with wide, swelling eyes.

Baba said, "Vandanananda you were calling me to come while you were in the burial ground for so many nights. Now I am here. If the government of Tamil Nadu doesn't allow Me to hold DMC, I will do it anyway. If not allowed, I will do it in this room itself. And if nobody is here, you and I will be in the DMC."

Vellore Namaskar Blessing

When I came out, the magistrate, Mr Thyagarajan, and the SSP, Mrs. Thilkhawathy, were there. They asked me whether everything is all right. I said, "Why not?"

The magistrate was so much impressed with the splendour of Baba's namaskar that he said, "I don't know why the government of India is afraid of this great person who is a Divine Personality, shining and glowing."

In the General Darshan that night, Baba asked the crucial ques-

tions. What can you do? What should you do? Baba explained that devotees are to pray Parama Purusa, "If You want, please utilize me for the perfection of Your Mission in this universal drama. Please utilize me fully. If You so desire, please bestow me with the strength to achieve this."

Food for Our Heart of Hearts

Margis had occupied almost all the rooms of that big hotel. They were dreaming with excitement of secret plans of catching Baba alone and being blessed with one of those soul-slaying smiles and losing themselves in those eyes streaming with intense tenderness such that it filled their eyes with streams of tears.

The General Darshan in the morning of the twentieth was very special. Baba explained that in the human world movement depends on self-inspiration and self-governance. When this realm of the human mind called bháva is blessed with the touch of the Supreme, then the psychic and spiritual vibrations have no way in which they can be expressed because this Supreme Consciousness is beyond bhava. Hence one's actions will no longer create any more reactions or samskaras. For those devotees who take this tangential point as their final shelter or base of life they alone realize the ultimate truth of the cosmos and the culmination of the entire universal drama.

We may not have understood all this, but we all understood how by the touch of Baba upon our being we had found the Supreme Shelter and the Supreme Foundation, the base upon which we could dedicate ourselves to serving His mission.

One room with a big verandah was used for Baba's kitchen. I had agreed that Shrii Pattabhiraman would provide the milk required from his home. His recently initiated daughter was blessed with the opportunity to cook the special dishes of Vellore for Baba. Shrii Rajgopalan and Shrii Goenka were busy supervising arrangements for His morning and evening walks. What sheer joy! Can you imagine it! The Lord of the Universe took human form and allowed me to care for His every small physical need like He was my very own Father. Just thinking of all this sent shivers down my spine.

Food for Our Heart of Hearts

I was in charge of the workers, margis' accommodation, food arrangements and so much more. At noon on the twentieth, the day of DMC, when Shri Rajgopalan served lunch to dearest Baba, Baba refused to take the lunch; rather He became very angry.

He said, "I won't take this food. Take it away from the table."

All present sensed that something was wrong with the food. They could not think what it was, so a hectic search was made for me. I came running and entered into Baba's room.

Baba's face was red. He said He would not take that food. I could feel that something was wrong with the preparation of the food.

Food for Our Heart of Hearts

I went to the kitchen and wanted to know who had cooked what items. I knew Didi Sudha, the wife of Goenkaji, a good and devoted woman who had served Baba many times in the past. She alone knew the women who were in the kitchen. When I questioned the daughter of Shrii Pattabhiraman about what had been prepared, her voice was garbled and uncomfortable. I demanded to know what was in her mouth. She said that it was chewing tobacco. I politely requested her to leave and not to enter Baba's kitchen anymore. She obliged.

I said, "You are cooking food for Baba with chewing tobacco in your mouth!" Then I prepared a plate from the items that had been cooked by Didi Sudha and took it to Baba's room. Baba was happy and started eating, saying that it was sentient now.

Blessing Ending

The DMC was over and Baba had given His varabhaya mudra, that most explosive blessing with the gesture of protection only He could provide and the gesture of giving the ultimate gift that He alone could give — His love.

Baba left the hall and started for His room. The hotel was on the opposite side of the street and I was accompanying Him.

Inside the hall, the margis began to start dharmacakra. When Baba

heard them singing the invocation for meditation Samgacchadhvam, He asked me to go and stop the Dharmacakra. I went and did accordingly.

Blessing Ending

Reflecting on this, I felt that Baba had already consecrated the gathering by having sung Samgacchadhvam in His own special melody. So for a mere human being to sing afterwards was not just unnecessary but a sign of not properly appreciating His blessing. In fact, we know that it is only after many lifetimes of performing dhyana that one is blessed with darshan or contact with the physical form of the Lord. Hence after being graced with seeing that divine form, further meditation is meaningless. It is far better to simply absorb one's mind in the vibrations of His darshan that one just received.

The morning of the twenty-first Baba gave the last darshan. It was so short and so wonderful. Baba reminded us that the arts — song, dance, literature, and painting — bring such beauty to the lives of the hardworking and suffering people. And mysticism — the longing for that Supreme Love, Supreme Beloved, Supreme Beauty behind all the beauty on this sweet planet — must also come into our lives. Both of these must give meaning to their lives; this is our most sacred duty. In the past in India all the music, architecture, and sculpture were based on devotional love and the bliss of meditation. Baba asked us, in this land of Tamil Nadu where the bhakti movement began, to once again create a new bhakti revolution. It is an order, a plea from our Baba, that we have not yet obeyed but by His Grace we will start to honour it in every samaja of this sweet planet blessed by the dust of the feet of our Baba.

AESTHETICS AND MYSTICISM

Dharma should be understood properly by all. And that's why I said that there should be an immediate Tamil rendering (of the Author's previous discourse) — there should be renderings in all people's languages. Let people understand it properly and do accordingly. Last night I said that above the — "beyond the", not "above" — beyond the periphery of material mobility, there is the world of aesthetics; and above it, beyond it, there is the world of mystics. The world of mystics and the world of aesthetics are for human beings. And each and every human being should get a proper chance, should get adequate chance, to move into those worlds.

So you boys, you girls, you intellectuals, this should be your duty — so that common people's worldly burdens are minimized, so that they may get ample chance to move into higher worlds and utilize their energy for higher and subtler pursuits. The entire aesthetics is the only charming entity in human life. Had there been no aesthetics, human life would have been just like a desert. A slight touch of aesthetics in this anxiety-(ridden) life of human beings is just like an oasis in a desert. Art, architecture, literature, music — everything — had its origin, had its starting point — where? Just at the common point of aesthetics and mystics. That's why in ancient India, both in Áryavarta (North Indian) music and Dákśinatya music (commonly known as Karnataka music), music was devotional. Music used to get its inspiration from mysticism.

You should revive all these subtler expressions of humanity. You should remember that human life is not like a flower, it is like a collection of so many flowers, and that collection of so many flowers is the charm of human life. Human life is not like a flower; it

is like a beautiful garden of so many flowers. Let all those flowers develop, and let the final movement of all those expressions of charm and fascination be goaded unto Parama Puruśa. Whatever I do, whatever we do, whatever we will be doing, is just to please the Supreme Charmer, just to please the Supreme Source, just to please the Supreme Progenitor — there cannot be any other aim of human life.

AESTHETICS AND MYSTICISM

21 July 1980, Morning, Vellore

TANDAVA IN THRISSUR

There are so many forces that want to destroy you, so many forces that are inimical to you by nature. Say a snake — a snake is a born enemy. There are many such born enemies. As Tantra represents life, táńdava says that one's spirit should be based on vitality, based on the principle of survival.
This human skull represents death. You are surrounded by death, but you must not be defeated, you must not develop the psychology of fear or defeatism. So the knife is with you to fight against death. Yours is a fight for survival. In the daytime one may also use a snake to represent death — not an ordinary snake, but a venomous snake. In India our boys dance with a snake. But at nighttime neither a skull nor a snake will be visible. Here there is light, they will be visible, but in a burial ground, in a cremation ground, where there is darkness, a snake or skull will not be visible. There you may use fire to represent death.
This is the spirit of Tantra. To fight, to fight for survival. It is normal life moving towards the supreme state of abnormality. ("Dance, Mudrá and Tantra," Ánanda Vacanámrtam Part 12)

From Baba's quotation above we can realize that tandava is not only a yogic exercise that strengthens the nerves and the brain but it also has social importance if it is performed collectively by a group of devotees in public. When the collective social consciousness is lost, collective tandava ignites the revolutionary spirit in the people to fight against injustice and the immoral forces. Even those who are very timid, by witnessing tandava their conscience and desire to take action will be awakened. It fills the onlookers with

courage and vitality. Lord Shiva asked His disciples to do it in public together. Today all over the world we see so many people living in despair because they are suffering but they feel helpless and fear to do anything. Tandava will give these people the spirit of confidence so that they can fight against injustice. Baba emphasized that in order to arouse the fighting spirit against injustice, tandava should be performed in public everywhere, in every country.

TANDAVA IN THRISSUR

Baba asked us to perform tandava publicly with skull and knife so that people would understand its importance and spiritual significance. Under His instruction, Acarya Sarvatmananda, Acarya Shankarananda and another acarya were asked to perform collective tandava everywhere, starting with every town of India. At that time I was the RS of Bangalore region and went with them for this programme in Thrissur — the cultural capital of Kerala and a great centre of Shiva worship.

It was the day when the maximum number would gather at the marketplace. I stood on a stool and started addressing the crowd in English. People began to approach. I told them in English about the tandava we were going to perform and how it was beneficial for personal health and for arousing courage in the collective psychology. Then we began to perform tandava together and created one strong flow. The impact was so big that the road was blocked due to so many people having gathered around us, watching the tandava. We dispersed before the police could arrive.

Later Acarya Shankarananda demonstrated tandava in the Press Club of Ernakulam. This generated a lot of publicity and was widely talked about in the Kerala newspapers.

ALL ARE BABA’S CHILDREN

A difficult situation had developed in Bangalore to the point where personal sentiment rather than the ideology became paramount in how the organization functioned in this region. A certain acarya, who was a deputy commander (DC) of VSS was an old worker in this area and never let anyone supervise him; rather he would ensure that he supervised others. When Acarya Girijananda Avadhuta was posted as RS Bangalore, he was not welcomed as the supervising worker. The RS is the head of the region and the DC should have received him as RS as per the normal structural functioning of the organization. This time however, a strong group of margis was created around the DC who did not respect the authority of the RS. Shrii Puttuswamy developed a cordial relationship with Girijanandaji and was thus branded as being part of the group of margis who supported the RS. The unit was shamelessly manipulated in such a groupist way that Shrii Puttuswamy, who was financing the RS, was thrown out of the unit and was declared a non-margi. To make matters worse, Puttuswamyji had an accident and his leg was in a cast. He was bedridden and confined to the house.

Baba was to visit Bangalore on August 18, 1979. There was much pressure on all the RSs of Delhi Sector (India) to have their own offices and LFTs (Local Full Time Worker). Girijanandaji was under a lot of pressure to establish his office. He was new to the region and the margis had been instructed not to cooperate with him or help him. This was a great worry because it was normal procedure for Baba to inspect any office of the RS. Nevertheless Girijanandaji pressed onward in the search, with Puttuswamyji cooperating as much as he could.

Baba came to Bangalore and told the RS that He would like to visit His office. The RS contacted Puttuswamy and explained the situa-

tion. Puttuswamy told RS Dada that a portion of his house could be used as the office.

ALL ARE BABA’S CHILDREN

Baba went there and was so happy to see the office. Puttuswamyji was brought on a wheelchair by his wife to see Baba.

Baba blessed him and said, "You come and attend DMC."

Lo! Puttuswamyji was brought to the DMC to the shock of all those who had expelled him. Baba was in a very special mood.

Actually Acarya Parashivananda Avadhuta had initiated Mr Puttuswamy, who hailed from a so-called lower caste. He had an easy-going, cheerful nature. After initiation, he remarried because his first wife could not have children. This second marriage was a revolutionary marriage with a margi sister from a Brahmin (priest caste) background. Later, when I became RS of Bangalore, I sent him for Dharma Samiiksa and it became his PC and Dharma Samiiksa combined in one. He had a problem in his abdomen and Baba pointed this out in Dharma Samiiksa and gave him a good thrashing for his mistakes. Thereafter, he was cured of his ailment

ALL ARE BABA’S CHILDREN

It was he who singlehandedly started the Ananda Marga Polytechnic in Malur, the only technical institute currently run by the organization. It is he who started the school and childrens' home in Bangalore. He was admired highly for his work. He received the award for best bhukti pradhan from Baba Himself. He bought nineteen acres of land in Kolar District for a Master Unit.

But just see the drama of Baba's love. The RS dada had been wrongfully persecuted by a jealous worker. Margis allowed themselves to be used to ostracise an innocent worker and victimize an innocent margi. Even the RS did not stand up for this margi (by bringing him to DMC) who was giving him so much money, support and love. But Baba with one simple declaration restored the honour of His organizational discipline and system and restored the honour of His devotee. Those who attack other margis based on sentiment for a particular margi or dada, those dadas who manipulate margis to get power and control — they forget what Baba has clearly stated:

If an Ananda Margi engages in harming another Ananda Margi he or she remains under the condemnation of Shrii Shrii Ánandamúrtijii till his/her conduct is reformed.
("Society", Caryácarya Part 2)

HAVE A HEART WHEN YOU EAT

Like many, I read in Caryacarya Part 2 where Baba says: Bear in mind that you have a duty towards — indeed, you owe a debt to — every creature of this universe, but towards you, no one has any duty; from others, nothing is due.

To read this is one thing, but to put it into practice is another. The following incident happened in 1981. Baba was residing in Patliputra Colony, Patna.

Baba caught me during Delhi Sector reporting. He ordered Acarya Keshavananda (education secretary) to beat me with five hundred strokes.

After being beaten, Baba asked me, "Do you know why you were beaten?"

HAVE A HEART WHEN YOU EAT

I replied, "Baba, I do not know."

Baba said, "You see, Keshavananda, Vandanananda was VSS in 1969 when one margi came to Ranchi from a remote place to meet Me. When that man reached My place, he (Baba motioned in my direction) was eating. He did not ask that man to share his food. He just kept eating, keeping his head down. That man was also hungry. Vandanananda should have asked him to eat. But he kept eating in such an uncourteous manner. As a result that man started hating the monks of Ananda Marga. He thought that this is how the sannyasis of Ananda Marga are. Thinking this, he returned back without meeting Me. Even after all this time, I have love for that person. He still wants to meet me. And he plans to come here. But when he remembers this negative incident, his heart fills with hatred for our sannyasis. So Vandanananda was beaten until the feeling of hatred was removed from that man's heart."

At last I said, "Baba from now onwards I will not repeat this mistake. Please forgive me."

Baba said in a stern voice, "Why did you do that?"

HAVE A HEART WHEN YOU EAT

I again replied, "Baba, now I will not do that."

Suddenly Baba said, "Leave him. Now the feeling of hate is removed from that man's heart. He will come to meet me soon." After this I tried to inculcate the habit of caring for every person I meet. Baba has said:

You should always be vigilant that not a single individual of our collective body is in the least neglected or ignored. Let not a single boy or girl feel within them that no one is concerned about them — let not a single person be allowed to think, "No one cares whether I have taken my food or not." You must pay special heed to this.
Particularly those of you who are in responsible positions of leadership should remember that you should never think, "No one is bothering to enquire whether I have eaten or not." Rather, you should always be concerned with others' necessities, not your own. You should always think more about your duties and responsibilities than about your rights. ("Genius and Technician", A Few Problems Solved Part 3)

In mid-1980, Baba introduced Sadavrata. He gave clear instructions that between nine and eleven in the morning and five to seven in the evening, if any needy person comes to a jagriti of Ananda Marga or any other building of Ananda Marga, then they should be given food. If any more well-off person comes they should be requested to help with the provision of food supplies.

DHARMA SAMIIKSA: A MYSTERY FOR ETERNITY

This story took place at a time when margis from faraway places were coming to Kolkata for Dharma Samiiksa. This great liila (divine drama) continued for around two years. Even today I do not understand the real meaning of this Dharma Samiiksa. We do not understand so many of the words and liilas of Baba or their relevance at that particular point in time, but later on we gain some understanding. Nevertheless many things remain a mystery; they remain part of the great mystery that is Lord Anandamurti. His activities were mysterious because He himself was a mystery and I believe that this visible world that we call the universe is in fact part of the invisible world of His mind. This is the reason I get baffled when I try to ponder the magic of His liila. But no matter how much we become lost in amazement, our minds keep on exploring His irresistible liilas and will continue to do so until we merge in Him.

So let us try to see what Baba says in our scripture. The word iikśańa means "to scrutinize, to survey." The prefix sam means "completely, exhaustively, all-round, inside-and-out." So samiikśa means a "careful, painstaking all-round examination or observation." Dharma comes from the verb dhr, which means "to hold, to uphold, to support." So Dharma means the entity that supports us by holding us. It provides support to our inner qualities and traits. It maintains our existence and provides support from within us because dharma is our very essence or true nature. So when we think about it, who is that supporting entity that supports us and enables our inner being to blossom? Is it not our Baba, our Dharma Guru? Dharma has four parts, expanding the mind into the infinity of Baba's consciousness (vistara), merging in the flow of Baba's love (rasa), serving Him internally and externally (seva), until we merge in Him (tadstithi). So Dharma Samiiksa then becomes Baba's careful, complete survey of every aspect of our being, to see how we are moving towards Him in this Dharmic flow

DHARMA SAMIIKSA: A MYSTERY FOR ETERNITY

and empowering us to do this. This is the literal meaning. The reality behind Baba's meeting with thousands of people is beyond the capacity of our mind to comprehend, let alone to delve into its inner depths. Those few among the billions of people on this planet who got Dharma Samiiksa are crucial for human history and in some cases the stories of those who did not get Dharma Samiiksa is also crucial.

So Dharma Samiiksa was a mysterious journey.

I was posted as RS during that period. I came to Kolkatta from Chennai. When I reached, Sarveshvarananda, who was the general secretary, told me that Baba was calling me. I had no idea that Baba was conducting Dharma Samiiksa at that time. Later on I came to know that he was conducting Dharma Samiiksa for all the central workers. My turn came in the second group. I entered Baba's room on the first floor of the Jodhpur Park office. Baba was sitting on a cot and many central workers were in the room. After performing sastaunga pranama (full prostration), I was trying to comprehend the situation. Before I could understand anything, Baba asked me to adopt the Bhairava dress that we use in Kapalika sadhana. I followed His order. Baba reviewed me up and down with His penetrating eyes. Then he asked, "Have you done asanas today?"

I replied that since I had been on a train I was not able to do asanas. Baba said, "You have to do asanas in the train also. Good work can be done anywhere. Understood!"

DHARMA SAMIIKSA: A MYSTERY FOR ETERNITY

"Yes, Baba," I replied.

Baba then asked, "Tell me what kind of asanas (yogic exercises) you perform."

I answered, "Sarvaungasana, matysamudra, gomukhasana, utkatpaschimotasana."

I was about to add two more but Baba stopped me and said, "Sarvaungasana is compulsory for you. The rest you can either do or not do!"

DHARMA SAMIIKSA: A MYSTERY FOR ETERNITY

Again He asked, "Why are you scratching your head?"

I said, "Baba, I had a leech that I got from someone in VSS camp."

"Do not blame others." Baba said sternly.

I told Baba, "From now on, it will not happen." Then Baba admonished me to maintain cleanliness.

DHARMA SAMIIKSA: A MYSTERY FOR ETERNITY

I replied, "Yes, Baba."

Baba said to GS, "This is all right."

Then He faced me and said, "You can go now."

Even today I still do not understand this Dharma Samiiksa. Was it a cosmic survey? Or did He vow to examine every sadhaka Himself. I say this because in every RDS He Himself reviewed every worker. Then for what reason did He give Dharma Samiiksa? The answer does not come in my small mind, except the thought that this programme was for all margis and workers.

DHARMA SAMIIKSA: A MYSTERY FOR ETERNITY

At the time of this liila, Baba mentioned that Lord Shiva once thought to do this but never did. In His discourse on Dharma Samiiksa, Baba said:

Now I have already said that one must undergo the unsatisfied sámskáras (unrequited reactions of past actions), the unquenched samśkáras. In order to attain salvation, one must burn up one's past samśkáras. A seed when soaked in water germinates, but if it is burnt up it can never germinate. Every human being must be a dagdhabiija (burnt seed), that is, one whose samśkáras are totally burnt up. For this, one absolutely needs the grace of the Guru, the grace of Parama Puruśa. The scriptures say, "Just an iota of the Cosmic Grace is enough to ensure salvation for unit beings." The main purpose of Dharma Samiikśa is to make the jiivas (unit beings) dagdhabiijas. During the entire period of fifteen thousand years of human history, there has been no Dharma Samiikśá such as this...
Those who have assembled here are surely good people. Some of you have come all the way from five thousand or (ten thousand) miles away. Some have come from the vicinity. There is certainly some subtle reason behind your assembling here. You are all good people — certainly better-than-average people. That is why you have come. Though there are many, many others who live nearby, they do not come. In my opinion, Parama Puruśa should do something for these good people. How long can unit beings bear the heavy load of unburnt saḿskáras? So if Parama Puruśa should take away some of the sins from the unit beings, they will feel relieved of the heavy load and feel lightened. These people, free from the bondages of pápa and saḿskáras, can do many big things. By dint of their collective efforts they will bring heaven down onto this terrestrial earth; they will perform many noble and heroic deeds with courage and valour. Hence Dharma Samiikśá is the most epoch-making event of the last fifteen thousand years. (Ánanda Vacanámrtam Part 23)

So firstly, Baba is saying that this Dharma Samiiksa is the most important event in the history of human civilization. Can we stop to ponder this for a moment? We read, watch and hear so many things about history but about this liila — the most important event in the history of humanity on this planet — we know next to nothing.

DHARMA SAMIIKSA: A MYSTERY FOR ETERNITY

Baba says that the goal of this great liila is to burn up the seeds of our remaining samskaras. So the purpose of our coming before Baba was not simply to review our progress, expose our mistakes and bless us like in RDS; it meant that Baba burned the seeds of our samskaras by taking this suffering upon Himself. And what is the purpose of being a dagdhabiija or burnt seed? In a discourse on this topic, Baba explained:

While doing sádhaná (meditation), a sádhaka (meditator) reaches a stage where púrńa bhakti (cent per cent devotion) is aroused for Paramátmá (Supreme Self/Spirit). Then one remains unassailed even if a cyclone of misery attacks him or her. One feels maximum torture in the mind, but he or she little cares for it, taking it to be the benediction of Paramátmá Himself. Torture is there but he or she derives bliss from it. The sádhaka of that stage is called dagdhabiija (burnt seed). A dagdhabiija comes within the range of psychic directly.
Biija means "seed". Wherever a seed is sown, it sprouts into a plant; but where the seed is burnt, a plant is not produced. A sádhaka becomes dagdhabiija when he or she has no more pain or pleasure of his or her own. One who has surrendered in toto and has not kept in hand even a single paisa of it, is alone dagdhabiija. All carry their own burdens, but if a dagdhabiija sádhaka so desires, he or she can carry the burdens of others also. And those who want to carry the burden of others do lessen the burden of Paramátmá indirectly. You should remember this.
Do not remain worried about your individual problems at all. Be prepared to carry your own burden and be prepared also to carry the burdens of others. Then alone are you brave. Be dagdhabiija. Everyone has his or her own individual problems. Do not try to pass them on to others. On the contrary, bear the burdens of others. No one is your enemy. Be ready to bear the burdens of others. (Ánanda Vacanámrtam Part 30)

Every worker and margi who had came to this small Jodhpur Park office had Dharma Samiiksa from Baba. Some poor margis who sold their land and came to Kolkata and lived on the streets waiting to get Dharma Samiiksa were blessed indeed.

DHARMA SAMIIKSA: A MYSTERY FOR ETERNITY

So Dharma Samiiksa prepares us to become this kind of cosmic revolutionary who is capable of facing extreme persecution. Instead of becoming terrified or weary due to persecution, they go into deep bliss because they feel that His blessing is behind that suffering. This is why Baba told us, "Let suffering be your asset." For our wealth, our treasure, is the tapasya (austerities) we have done for suffering humanity. Baba began this programme a few years after coming out of jail to give us the opportunity, the capacity, to tread the path of tapasya, thus revealing Himself as Dharma Guru.

It is the call of the Supreme for all to adopt the course of tapasya. Despite knowing this, I still wonder about my Dharma Samiiksa and why Baba made it so brief? What did Baba do in the spiritual realm? Had Baba already purified me by RDS and hence the session was very short? These questions are part of my path of being His disciple, and like all pariprashna (true questions) it is part of my love story with Him.

THIS IS ANANDA MARGA

Once during Dharma Samiiksa, the disciples were gathered outside Baba's room in which He conducted the examination and removal of the samskaras of the disciples. Baba came out of His room. There was a sister from overseas immersed in meditation against the wall outside Baba's room. In her lap was a small baby who was nursing and playing happily, although she remained lost in sadhana. Thus she was unknowingly caring for her baby while meditating with the baby in her lap. She was enjoying spiritual bliss and her baby was also enjoying. This is the way Baba said we should live in the world. We should do all our duties to our family and society but at the same time remain steeped in the spiritual realm.

Even though Baba passed in front of her while leaving Jodhpur Park, she remained lost in deep meditation. Some people tried to arouse her but Baba signalled with His finger to let her be. Then Baba said,

"This is what Ananda Marga is."

HE ALONE TAKES CARE

Shrii C. D. Raghunath of a so-called lower caste married Uma Shettigar of a so-called higher caste and their marriage was blessed by Baba. He was employed in a bank and was posted in Bellary (Karnataka). They were expecting their first child at the time that Baba was conducting Dharma Samiiksa.

I convinced them that they should go for Dharma Samiiksa. It was not possible for Uma because the date of her delivery was approaching. However Raghu agreed to go after Uma gave permission. He knew that she needed him to be with her.
