When the time for the train came, Raghu was reluctant to go, but Uma pressured him forcefully because if he missed that train he would have to wait three days for another train.

So, remembering Baba, Raghu left, leaving his wife alone at home. Hardly had he reached the station when Uma's labour pains started. Alone, she left the house and took a rickshaw to the hospital. The nurse asked her, "Are you alone?" Uma replied that her Baba was with her. She delivered a hale and healthy baby girl.

In the train, Raghu was cursing himself for leaving his wife alone. The steam engine puffed and puffed and took twenty-four hours to reach Vijaywada. Finally on the third day in the morning, he reached Kolkata.

HE ALONE TAKES CARE

When he came to Jodhpur Park he saw a big line of margis from all over the world. However, he could only feel remorse that he had left his wife alone and uncared for.

Suddenly one dada came from Baba's room and asked the margis if there was any person from South India who had just arrived. Raghu looked around to see if there was anyone else from South India present. Finally a dada asked Raghu if he was from South India. Then the dada said to him, "You are the only one who came just now. Please rush to see Baba."

Raghu said that he had not taken bath. But the dada told him that there was no need, he should go as he was. He was called before Baba and Baba gave him Dharma Samiiksa.

Then Baba said, "Go back home now by the next available train." Raghu rushed to the station and reached Bellary after three days. He went home but found no one there. Upon enquiring, he heard that his wife was in the hospital and he rushed there.

HE ALONE TAKES CARE

He found Uma sitting on the cot. Uma told him that the entire time, Baba had been with her with His loving smile, caring for her. She was so happy enjoying Baba's presence all this time.

Hearing this, first Raghu and then both of them started crying.

BABA DID NOT LET ME GO

After Baba's release from jail, the number of dadas increased manyfold and Ananda Marga became a global organization. Baba selected many workers from different parts of India and sent them overseas. Every six months there was a competition for the RS who had done the best work. Bangalore was competing against Mumbai. Twice I won this prize as RS Bangalore. Thereafter my name was suggested many times to work overseas and I had heard they were trying to have me sent to GT Sector (South America). I was told that Acarya Haratmananda, the sectorial secretary, had already arranged for my ticket and visa. Some time after this, I came to Kolkata for reporting. I was told not to attend RDS because Baba had been told that I had already gone to GT sector.

The interesting part is that the worker who was to replace me in Bangalore had just left the organization. Hence there was no one to take my place in Bangalore. Ac Pranavananda, who was publications secretary, sent me back to Bangalore to resolve a problem there. In the meantime I was in touch with the centre, waiting to know who was coming to Bangalore to take over the charge of RS. No one came.

When I came to Kolkata for the next RDS, I gave the plants that I had brought to Didi Ananda Karuna, and she told Baba about them early next morning. She said, "This plant was brought by Ac. Vandananda," and was moving onto the next plant when

Baba interrupted her, saying, "Wait. When did he come from GT Sector and with whose permission?"

BABA DID NOT LET ME GO

Since Didi did not know about my being posted abroad, she was perplexed and remained silent.

Baba pressed His bell to summon the GS and central workers. Baba asked how had I come and why. GS Dada pretended not to know anything.

Then Baba said, "Since Vandananda has come, let him stay in India; post him as RS Bangalore again."

It was just like a child playing with a yo-yo. The child sends the yo-yo down but in the end the yo-yo comes right back into the child's hand. In this case, I went away only in theory, not in reality. Baba kept me in His hands. He did not let me go far from Him where I would not be able to see Him so often. For this blessing, I cannot stop thanking Him again and again.

APARIGRAHA

I was the RS Bangalore. As part of my normal tour programme I would regularly catch the Chennai-Howrah Mail leaving at seven p.m. On this occasion I collected plants for Baba and went to the railway station. That day the train was unexpectedly overcrowded. I went looking for the Chief Ticket Collector to plead for a seat. When I found him, he said, "Sorry. I found one Brahmacari there; he managed a seat for himself in Second Class AC by paying more than Rs. 700." Sleeper class only cost Rs. 110 in those days. As a result, I travelled in the unreserved compartment, which cost even less money.

During the RDS, Baba caught hold of that Brahmacari, who was the press manager and was posted in Port Blair. Baba asked, "By which class did you travel from Chennai?"

"Baba, there was no seat available in any class so I travelled in Second AC."

"How much did you pay?"

APARIGRAHA

"Seven hundred rupees."

"The Marga is poor and you paid such a huge amount." Then Baba asked, "How much do you have with you now?"

The dada replied that he had no money at all.

Baba said, "Bring his bag and search it." In the bag they found more than seven thousand rupees.

APARIGRAHA

Earlier Asiimanandaji, who was then general finance secretary, had

asked him to pay his dues to the central administration. However he had refused, claiming to have no money.

Actually this dada had planned to go home and give the money to his worldly family.

After being exposed, this brahmacarii was not seen again. This was a sad event. I was happy that I had traveled in the general class. I had been sad on the train thinking that this dada had paid such a huge sum just for a train ticket. I was even sadder when I realized he was stealing the money of our organization and later when I heard that he had left us. But I was grateful to Baba that He saved me from making such a mistake myself. For we are capable of any mistake. It is only by His grace that we do not commit mistakes by wasting His money meant for the suffering humanity.

DAWN OF TILJALA: DAYS OF LABOUR, LOSS AND LOVE

Though I was RS Bangalore, I was much aware of the centre. As the RS, I was also much concerned with my region. The work load and mounting pressure hardly gave a worker time to think of problems outside his posting. This was a boon in a way, as workers did not have time for idle gossip or complaints about matters outside their area. It was an established principle that a worker should think only of the assigned work and thus have no scope to express himself in petty or mean-minded ways. True missionary commitment with sincerity, honesty and dedication paves the way to devotion to Baba — a state where one feels this is one's mission in life, a sadhana to please Parama Purusa.

During one RDS, I came to know that we had purchased a piece of land in VIP Nagar in Kolkata. I learned that Dearest Baba had been putting pressure on the central workers to vacate the Jodhpur Park building and shift to our newly purchased land. The Jodhpur office was rented with one room for Baba and the central office in another. Dharma Samiiksa — that epoch event in the spiritual history of the world — took place in Jodhpur Park, which was used before Baba's Lake Gardens quarters were purchased.

When we came for RDS in the month of June-July 1981, it was raining cats and dogs. Numerous times, Baba had got promises from the central workers that they would vacate Jodhpur Park and shift to Tiljala in VIP Nagar. As usual, the workers failed to keep their promise, since there was no money for construction, although a boundary wall and two rooms for security personnel had been constructed.

Baba had already shifted his residence to the newly purchased quarters in Lake Gardens called Madhu Malainca. It was half a kilometer from Jodhpur Park. As part of His regular routine, Baba used to go for a walk each morning and evening. I had hardly arrived at Jodhpur Park when I noticed Baba's car stopped on the road in

DAWN OF TILJALA: DAYS OF LABOUR, LOSS AND LOVE

front of the Jodhpur building. It was raining and thundering ominously.

We rushed to see what had happened to the car. What happened next was beyond my comprehension. As we were all gathered around the car — some with umbrella, some without — Baba suddenly opened the back door and stepped out. A number of non-margis were looking from their windows or from the balconies of nearby buildings.

Baba, addressing the general public, said, "Don't trust these sadhus. They promised many times to vacate this building but they didn't keep their promise. Don't believe them."

The local public could hardly have understood the implication and ramifications of this, but the workers understood it for sure. Those who accepted Him and His commands as the sole dictum never argued, either in individual life or in organizational life. We all took this act of Baba as a signal to vacate Jodhpur Park. As the Supreme Commander of our lives He never gave us scope for argument. We hired a truck to transport our office records, files and publications and went to Tiljala — a place where there was not even a roof to shelter our heads, what to say of an organization. The rain poured down even more strongly, as if enjoying our predicament. But we all were in high spirits, with full confidence and dedication to Baba.

DAWN OF TILJALA: DAYS OF LABOUR, LOSS AND LOVE

Ignorance was bliss indeed. We, with no idea of crises lurking ahead, accepted the challenge and passed the night in two thatched huts with a metal sheet. Why bother when the Lord's command was ruling our heads and hearts?

Tiljala Construction

The night passed and day dawned with new vigour and enthusiasm. We moved out looking for places to go to toilet. While doing this, we disturbed the nearby people who were Bangladeshi refugees on the payroll of the communists. Initially, they came forward boasting that they were the best workers and so we engaged them as labourers. Later, after a few days of work, their real colour and culture started surfacing. They demanded a raise in their daily wage and we had no option but to agree. Then again they demanded a raise that we simply could not afford. Also they declared they would not let any other labourers come to work on the site. Most likely they were guided by the CPI(M) who were furious that Ananda Marga was building its headquarters in one of the biggest centres of their party in the whole of Kolkata. It was typical of Baba to pitch our tent in the heart of the enemy camp.

As a result, work ground to a halt. Our dear Baba was being briefed on the progress of construction. Baba declared an internal emergency and said all workers would work as labourers until the construction was completed. Baba said that since it was our spiritual centre, we would not hire any outsiders as labourers. The workers and margis will donate their labour.

Nearly 150 dadas and 75 didis had come for All-India (sectorial) RDS. We dadas worked as labourers in the main building and didis worked on the didi's building. We called some expert masons and iron-fixers from Bihar and surrounding states. They agreed to say that they were margis who were donating their labour. We did not allow a single local person to work on the construction.

The flames of anger became a fire after being ignited by the CPI(M) leaders of that area. We worked day and night. When the mission absorbs the mind, it crosses the boundaries of time and sleep. Since we knew that Baba was enjoying the drama, we also were enjoying it. Baba used to get reports of the progress and asked GS Sarveshvarananda Dada to provide us the best food.

Tiljala Construction

The distance from the main gate to the road was about 250 metres. The access road did not have the bearing strength to allow loaded trucks to directly come to the gate. The rain and crumbling mud road made our plight even more miserable. Sometimes under the rain, the cement bags and other construction materials were unloaded on the main road to the delight of nearby looters. When we complained to the police they claimed to be helpless and then blamed us for not hiring local labour. It truly was an ordeal not to be forgotten.

On the other hand, Baba used to take stock of our progress: what we were having for breakfast, lunch and dinner; how the buildings were increasing; who was doing what.

Whenever we happened to go out for purchasing materials for our

daily needs, we used to feel the hatred and anger among the local people. The situation became very tense.

Tiljala Construction

The main gate of the didis' centre was hardly forty feet away from the main road. There was a drain that was causing problems in bringing the supply trucks inside the boundary. Hence we made a small temporary bridge that proved to be very helpful.

The local people and the police did not like that we were transporting the construction materials directly inside where they could not be looted. So the police broke the bridge we had built on the grounds that the land belonged to the municipality and we had no legal right to construct a temporary bridge.

When this news was brought to the notice of Baba, He instructed us to file a case in Kolkata High Court against the police for trying to destroy an existing bridge. The police argued that there was no bridge. The court ordered that there be an onsite inspection to ascertain the truth. This was a setback as the bridge had already been destroyed and would not be found.

Hearing this, Baba gave the following points:

Tiljala Construction

  1. The English word "police" in Hindi is in feminine gender. So Baba said that our people should pass by the police post at regular intervals (they were guarding the construction site to make sure we did not reconstruct the bridge) and pass remarks to each other loudly so the police could hear, such as saying, "Oh, how is it that these police (feminine gender) are standing with such a big mustache?" This psychological drumming was to bring down their morale and make them angry.
  2. In the night, empty cement bags should be filled with sand inside the boundary of didis' building. At that time the police were camped outside to make sure we did not rebuild the bridge. So the idea was that the police should not know what is happening inside our boundary.
  3. The police guarding the site became vulnerable at two times. First in the morning when they went to the toilet outside on the open ground, since no bathroom was nearby, and second when there was a shift of duty. If both happened at the same time, then their vulnerability was even more heightened—especially in the morning between 5:00 and 6:00. At that time they thought that we were asleep or were going to toilet. So it was during this time when we should rebuild the bridge.
  4. Baba said that at this time the dadas should shift the bags filled with sand by the didis and use them to build a bridge of sand bags.

Thirty-five or forty of us dadas started transporting the filled bags from the main gate of didis' quarters to the culvert some twenty feet away. Surprisingly within fifteen minutes we completed the job. The police later stared with wide eyes in astonishment, wondering how we had done it. At that time, we were on guard to make sure they did not dismantle the bridge again.

The police came in large numbers and charged with sirens and rifles in hand. Many dadas retreated back to the building but Ac. Nigamananda and I stood there firmly. I shouted at them with double their volume.

We had cameras and video camera and gave them a stern warning that they could not break the bridge. Thus when the court inspectors came they found the bridge and we won the case.

Tiljala Construction

This angered the police greatly as well as the CPI(M) cadres. I remember that Baba once said that the area of VIP Nagar is the stronghold of the CPI(M). I discovered the sources of their income.

  1. The word VIP means Very Important Persons. This signified the Hindus who came as refugees from Bangladesh in 1971. They were given free land in that area so they were VIP for the CPI(M)
  2. The local leaders got the status of special patrons, for they associated themselves with the trusted local leaders of the CPI(M). They were given money to carry out party orders.
  3. Their families received rations from government shops for free or at very low prices. Hence it paid to support the CPI(M).
  4. The young girls were used as consorts for high-ranking officers and leaders of the CPI(M), either in hotels or guesthouses.

Despite all this, the building was raised with our flag on top, a vic-

tory over the grand boasts of the CPI(M) that they would finish us off. They could not swallow the presence of Ananda Marga in the centre of their stronghold.

Tiljala Construction

Later, after the Bijan Setu massacre of our dadas, didis and margis by CPI(M) goondas, someone suggested to Baba that He should leave Kolkata and shift to somewhere in Bihar where it was safer and better for His health.

Baba retorted, "I am grinding dal on the chest of communism and you suggest to Me to go? I will let communism die here." Later, when communism died in the Soviet Union, Baba said, "Look here, it is the emergence of PROUT that has knelled the death of communism."

I should mention here an important point. Baba was staying in Lake Gardens. He used to come on weekends to Tiljala. On the other days, we workers went to Lake Gardens for reporting. Normally we would leave Tiljala for Lake Gardens 8 a.m. We used to go either by bus No. 39 to Park Street and then get another bus to go to the Jodhpur police station stop and then walk or catch another bus. It took not less than two hours to reach Lake Gardens. While returning from His morning walk, Baba used to come to the jagriti hall for reporting and we were supposed to be there by 10 a.m. sharp. Evening reporting was very uncertain. Hence sometimes we had to stay at Lake Gardens until midnight. The central workers wanted to be assured that in case Baba got angry about any development in the field, the concerning local worker would be taken to task and not them. When they were sure that Baba was not going to ask anything and had begun His two hours sleep, then only were we asked to go back to Tiljala. Often it was so late and so there were no buses. As a result, many nights we had to walk the long distance to Tiljala from Lake Gardens.

Those roads, which today are vibrant with people and full of colour, were in those days haunted with loneliness and darkness. We used to avoid the main roads as they were not considered safe. We used to do our kapalika sadhana where today is the Ruby Hospital and other corporate buildings. Some dadas used to stay in Lake Gardens and in Jodhpur Park but this happened rarely.

Tiljala Construction

The night before 30 April, 1982, we were told that everyone should go to Tiljala and not stay behind in this area of Lake Gardens or Jodhpur Park. Some workers who were very tired or who did not take Baba's words seriously remained at Jodhpur Park or Lake Gardens. In the morning of the thirtieth those workers were ambushed along the twin roads to Tiljala by CPI(M)-trained murderers who systematically murdered our brothers and sisters and set them on fire in broad daylight at five a.m.

"Give a dog a bad name and hang it" is the practice of all politicians. For many days the CPI(M) had spread rumours in the area of Tiljala that Ananda Marga monks were child kidnappers and should be punished for this. This was their planned strategy.

Baba did not take food on that fateful day.

Acarya Nigamananda and myself contacted the police officials and others to take the corpses of our brothers and sisters from them. We took them to the cremation ground where they were cremated. From there all of us — three to four hundred margis, dadas and didis — walked in a procession to Baba's quarters in Lake Gardens. The situation was so painful. Our brothers and sisters had been killed and there would be no justice under the regime of the communist murderers.

BEING THROWN OUT BRINGS SALVATION

This incident happened when I was transferred from Bangalore to Dharwar as DS. I had to go to Bangalore to participate in our IRSS (Inspection, Review and Structural Solidarity). Baba almost always sent me to difficult places to work. I was the DS of Dharwad in Karnataka for three years. When I was the RS of Bangalore, the DS of Dharwad left the hard life of a monk and went back home. There was no one to take care of Dharwad jagriti. Prior to him, Acarya Mananananda had been there for nearly twelve years. With great difficulty he had constructed the jagriti in an area not easily accessible to non-locals. It was situated in a place where no one would like to be outside after the sun went down. There was no water supply and no electricity. It was just an isolated building like a haunted house. One new Brahmacarii had committed suicide in that same building.

At reporting, Baba said, "You are the RS and there is no school for a long twelve years. Go and start the school." So I became the DS and after great struggle I started a school and made it healthy. At DMC I received the prize as the best principal for the second time. It should be mentioned that by His grace we got a water connection, electricity and completed the construction of the building. Coconut trees were planted beside the house.

Now I come to the main point. As I said earlier, I had to go to Bangalore to participate in IRSS. The Bangalore circle organiser, Acharya Jaganamayananada, accompanied me. We went to Dharwar bus stand late in the evening to catch the overnight bus for Bangalore. Other passengers were also waiting for the bus. We were last in line. All the buses that stopped there took on board no more than two or three passengers. We remained standing at the end of the line, wondering when our turn would come.

Then a bus arrived and stopped in front of us. The conductor shout-

BEING THROWN OUT BRINGS SALVATION

ed that only two passengers would be allowed to board the bus. We boarded and the bus left. After traveling only two or three hundred metres, the conductor told us to get down from the bus.

We started arguing. Why we should get out of the bus? We had to go to Bangalore. Whatever the fare may be, we were willing to pay it. The conductor did not listen to us. We asked him what the problem was? Why had he suddenly stopped the bus? An argument ensued.

Seeing this, the other passengers started shouting at us. "When conductor is not willing to take you then why are you arguing with him?" We both were in a furious mood. Muttering angrily, we got out of the bus. We started walking back to bus stand. As we reached there, a completely empty bus stopped. Both of us, along with all the other passengers boarded the bus and we started towards Bangalore. After some thirty-five minutes we reached Hubli town (part of Greater Dharwar). Hardly had we gone another twenty or twenty-five kilometres when we saw that the previous bus had rammed into a tree. The bus was severely damaged and eight or nine passengers had been killed and many others had been injured.

We both remembered Baba and thanked him. We realized it was He who made the conductor force us off the bus. We told each other, "One who is in lap of almighty Baba is always safe."

BEING THROWN OUT BRINGS SALVATION

When I met Baba during RDS, He said, "Was it necessary for you to argue with the bus conductor?"

NEVER AGAIN ASK ABOUT MY HEALTH

This story happened in Baba's Lake Garden residence. I was RS Bangalore. As on earlier visits, I came to Kolkata from Chennai and took a city bus to Jodhpur Park. From the bus stop I started walking to the Ananda Marga office. As I turned left, I saw Baba's car pass by and stopped. Baba was going for morning walk. Baba's bodyguard, Vishnu Dev, came up to me and said that Baba was calling me. I followed him and got in Baba's car. I had not yet taken bath and had a small shoulder bag with me.

Baba asked, "How are you?"

I replied, "Baba, by Your grace I am well."

Then I asked Baba, "How is your health?"

NEVER AGAIN ASK ABOUT MY HEALTH

Baba did not answer.

(Readers should know that whenever I met Baba alone or when there was no reporting or organisational meeting, I always asked Baba about His health and He always answered that He was well.) When I did not get any answer, I asked Baba again, "How is your health?"

Again Baba did not answer and again I repeated my question.

Stressing my words, I asked, "Baba, I am asking about your health. Please tell me how you are."

NEVER AGAIN ASK ABOUT MY HEALTH

This was my obstinacy, my excessive concern, foolishness or closeness, whatever you chose to think.

After few moments, Baba said, "Vandanananda, from today henceforth, you will never again ask me about My health. Why? You

know, whenever you ask me about My health, a few of My moments are spent thinking about My health. I want to devote this time for you people and the welfare of the world."

I started silently thinking about what He had said.

NEVER AGAIN ASK ABOUT MY HEALTH

Although I had many opportunities, I never again asked Baba this question. I could not gather the courage. Despite thinking about this for a long time, I could not reach anywhere. I could not understand the grandeur of Baba's utter selflessness.

FIRST TILJALA DMC

In spite of the fact that the construction of our Tiljala headquarters was incomplete, DMC was held there. Revered Baba told us why the DMC should not be conducted on our own premises rather than renting another place.

Baba was sitting on the dais and the devotees were sitting on the ground. It had rained heavily the day before. Water was flowing under our feet, so how could we sit? Even when one or two bricks were kept on the ground, still it was not possible to sit properly in the water. Just like village farmers sitting anywhere they can, so we somehow managed to sit on piles of bricks. We could not do kiirtan because of the flowing water. In spite of the fact that we had many problems providing basic needs for DMC, we never even noticed this. We were all so happy being together, just like children in a new house under construction, sharing their dreams.

Baba completed the DMC and blessed all with His mudra. Then as usual, he distributed the prizes.

At that time there was a small shed with an asbestos roof to the right side of the entrance of the main building. Baba came there and began to give tantric abhishek. Abhishek means "consecration" or "empowerment." This was a most special blessing in which Baba would touch each of the cakras of the worker. This was never given before or later by Baba in the history of the organization. In the middle of this sacred event, the electricity went out and then Baba stopped. But blessed indeed were those who received this tantric empowerment from Baba – the Supreme Guru.

ASSASSINATION

Each department of Ananda Marga has to be present for RDS (review, defects and solution) on a certain day assigned to them for reporting. At the time, I happened to be posted as DS for Dharwad in Karnataka. I happened to be in Kolkata at that time for reporting and was present during one examination conducted by Baba. After the completion of training in a basic training centre, the trainees were sent to Kolkata for further coaching and grooming for acaryaship. After getting PC, the trainees were getting still further training under the central CTS and finally Baba used to conduct the examination Himself. Baba used to take the final examination of trainees in five items: spirituality, languages, asanas, general knowledge and socio-economic philosophy (PROUT).

Baba asked, "Well, my little boy, what is the spelling of assassination?"

The trainee was not able to spell this word. Baba asked each candidate.

Baba then said, "What is the spelling of assassination? A, S, S. Then double it — A, S, S, A, S, S. Then I. Then nation. The nation (India) is headed by "I" (i.e. then Prime Minister Indira Gandhi) and the "I" is headed by two asses. What a pity!"

ASSASSINATION

Baba's eyes blinked and seemed empty and far away.

Then Baba said, "You now understand the spelling of assassination?"

"Yes Baba," everyone answered.

The next morning, the news of the assassination of Prime Minister Indira Gandhi on October 31, 1984 reached us. She had been the prime mover behind the persecution of Baba and Ananda Marga.

DELHI HOME FOR BABA

When I was the Dharwar DS, there was a rule that along with the area-level worker, a lower-level worker from each of the three trades (departments of Ananda Marga) were also required to attend the RDS. The RDS was held wherever Baba was.

Baba made a tour of India by road in 1984 and the DMCs were fixed in different places. Before the DMC in Delhi there was a DMC in Agra so I offered to be the below-level representative from the ERAWS department in the RDS.

I was intending to attend both the Agra and Delhi DMCs. Acarya Acintananda was the DS of Bhopal. He also joined me in the train. When we reached Agra, it was late and very cold. We did not have the address of the DMC site. What to do?

We went directly to the police station and explained our problem. The DSP of Agra was there. He began contacting the different police stations in Agra and in no time, we learned the address of the DMC. Not only this, the DSP also dropped us there in his jeep.

DELHI HOME FOR BABA

In Agra, I was blessed with a special experience. In the evening, it so happened that I was standing near the window and I saw Baba meditating. For more than ten minutes I watched Him meditate and then I saw Him getting up. These secret moments, watching Baba in silence, were so special.

After that we went to Delhi. Baba was staying in a guesthouse at Maharani Bagh that had been arranged by Shrii Mahaviir Jain, who was a very good margi.

However, Baba was upset because there were no quarters for Him in Delhi. He expressed his annoyance at the failure of the margis and workers. Due to the intense pressure given by Baba, Shrii Jain and other margis started searching for a place.

Somehow Shrii Jain came to know about Gadaipur from an old newspaper. It was bought for Rs. 900,000. Shrii Jain paid some

DELHI HOME FOR BABA

amount in advance and as per the agreement the rest of the money was to be paid in two installments. The total amount was paid while Baba was still in the guesthouse. The land was registered in the name of P. R. Sarkar Educational Trust. When the news was conveyed to Baba in the late evening, Baba called the workers and told us that we must shift there at once. We engaged taxis and went with our baggage. We spent the night cleaning the rooms and Baba arrived in the early morning.

When Baba arrived, hundreds of devotees were waiting for Him. Baba surveyed the premises after alighting from the car. Then Baba called Acarya Arteshananda and made him the caretaker of the Gadaipur quarters. We erected a pandal and DMC was held there on the 26 of February as well as RDS.

In the RDS I was in trouble. I was required to have opened a school in each of the districts in my area and the schools were supposed to have trained LFTs as teachers. I was running only one school. At this time Baba was exerting so much pressure that when I broke the news of my failure to the education secretary (ES) he angrily told me that not only would I be beaten severely, but he would also be beaten. He insisted that I lie and tell Baba that I had schools in all the districts. I refused to lie to Baba, as I had never done this throughout my life as His worker.

The ES put more and more pressure on me. Then I said that in order to say I had a school, I had to give the name of the LFTs teaching there and all LFTs were given PC by Baba, so whose name could I give? The wily ES replied that he would take the names of some LFTs who had recently got PC from Baba and that I was to say that they were the teachers. The ES carefully constructed the fraudulent report that I was to give to Baba. I was very sad about lying to my Baba but I was not able to withstand the pressure being put upon me. When I came before Baba, He said, "Who is there? Vandananda. Let him give his report to the GS, not to Me." In this way, Baba saved me from the evil fate of having to lie to Him, the ultimate truth of my very being.

DELHI HOME FOR BABA

Later in reporting, another dada was caught lying to Baba. Baba told him to get down on the ground on his hands and knees like a crawling baby. Baba then told him to stick out his tongue and pant heavily like a dog. Baba said that this was his punishment for lying to the Guru.

While leaving for Jammu, Baba called Shrii Mahaviiraji and gave him the name "Hasmukh," meaning "smiling face," as a blessing for all his noble work.

LOOK AT BABA'S FEET

Everyone knows how to speak to one's elders and how to be respectful. But a devotee should know how to speak to the Supreme Beloved in human form (Mahasambhuti). The following incident is related to this vital topic.

We were sitting in a small hall on the upper floor of Baba's Lake Gardens residence. Sometimes Baba would sit here to take reporting or review the organizational work. That day we were fewer in number and we were sitting outside Baba's room. Dada Mantreshvarananda and Acarya Samanvayananda Avadhuta were there. Baba was sitting with His index and middle fingers on His upper cheek and the ring finger on the lower cheek, a mudra (gesture that bestows delight) that even my meager wisdom knew was very special. Baba's left foot was hanging down from the wooden cot, touching the ground. Baba's right foot was folded on the cot. Baba's left hand was on His lap. How His face was shining! In what a divine, regal bháva (existential ambience) He sat! It is not possible to understand it by normal vision. One needs to be in a state of rapt concentration to begin to understand it.

Acarya Samanvayananda approached me and said, "Vandananda, this is Baba's Dakshineshvara mudra."

My acarya, Samanvayananda, and I gazed for a long time at Baba's revelation of this blissful mudra. Baba's face had a uniquely sublime expression and His eyes were radiating special bhávas. Acarya Mantreshvarananda was discussing and explaining some details related to a discourse Baba had given on Bengali grammar, later published as Shadba Cayanika. Baba was repeatedly looking at Dada but then would become lost in His blissful mood.

LOOK AT BABA'S FEET

But Mantreshvarananda was eager to get approval from Baba about his views. Without realizing the spiritual state Baba was in, he began to argue and refused to accept what Baba said. He was

disturbing Baba and when Baba was in such a state, it was like he was desecrating Baba.

I was also feeling some uneasiness at dada's behaviour as I tried to understand Baba's special form.

Suddenly Baba pointed at Mantreshvarananda and said in a stern voice: "Mantreshvarananda,

LOOK AT BABA'S FEET

Gurur mukhe dekhe kathá bolte hobe ná.
Gurur carań dekhe kathá bolo (while seeing the Guru's face one must not argue; look at the Guru's feet, and then talk!)."

After some time Baba withdrew this special manifestation and went back to His room.

Dakshineshvara is one of the five faces or five primary expressions that Shiva's disciples were able to notice after meditating upon His liila (divine play). As Baba explains:

Dákśińeshvara, the extreme right face of Shiva, is so called because He is showering dákśińá (compassion) upon the created beings. (Discourse 1, Namah Shiváya Shántáya)

LOOK AT BABA'S FEET

But truly we judge and name as per our limited knowledge. We say Baba is sweet based on our idea of sweetness from honey and our dear relatives. But can this really describe how Baba makes us cry with His sweetness. Similarly, every time, Taraka Brahma (Saviour Consciousness) manifests on this earth it is with a more powerful, more sublime, more beautiful form. So we try to describe Him using names and expressions of Shiva and Krsna but ultimately these expressions are inadequate to describe Baba. The science of Mahasambhuti Anandamurti's rapturous beauty has not till this day even begun to be explored, let alone used to give Him true love.

SAVIOUR

In 1982, Baba was back in the city of Patna. Countless devotees thronged to see Him. Many had not seen Him since before He went to jail. The devotees sang devotional songs of the ordeal they had faced and how Baba's love had enabled them to survive. Baba would come out of His beautiful Pataliputra residence for field walk. Once, when Baba was going for morning field walk, His car stopped right in front of a young margi sister with her two children. She leaned inside the window and touched Baba's feet. Baba said something to her and she just started weeping and continued weeping even after Baba's car had left. Her children were also weeping, because she was weeping. Her husband came but was unable to find out what had happened.

Later she told me the story. When she was a young wife with a young child, her husband found a job in the city of Jabalpur. He had to leave her behind but he told her that he would come back to bring her. However, once her husband was in Jabalpur, he told her that she would have to come by train because he could not get leave to bring her. She boarded the train with her young child. She was wearing the traditional gold ornaments (mangala sutra). She arrived safely in Jabalpur, but the train was late and her husband was nowhere to be found. Since it was late evening, she decided to take an auto rickshaw to her husband's office. She knew the name of the company where he worked but did not know where he was renting a room. She thought she would go to the office and try to find out where he was staying.

The rickshaw driver took her into a deserted area near the outskirts of the city. When she asked him why, he said he was taking a shorter route. Suddenly he stopped the rickshaw, whipped out a knife and demanded that she hand over her gold ornaments and money. She complied out of fear.

The thieving driver realised that she was educated and had seen his face and rickshaw license number, so he dragged her and her child to a nearby well and pushed them in. It was a dry well and he wanted to make sure they were dead so he stooped to pick up a large stone that was lying nearby, thinking to hurl it on top of her. Suddenly, a cobra emerged from underneath the stone and encircled both his hands, poised to strike.

SAVIOUR

The robber was stunned and couldn't move. Meanwhile, this sister had been crying for help. Some labourers walking home from the city after work heard her cries and ran there. They found the robber frozen in fear. When they approached, the snake disappeared beneath the stone. The rickshaw driver was arrested and she was reunited with her husband.

Sometime later both she and her husband learned meditation and became devotees of Baba. When Baba's car stopped in front of her and she touched His feet, Baba said "You my little child who was that snake? It was I" pointing towards Himself.

Baba is not just the saviour of individuals but of entire societies. At this time in Patna there were samaja workers from different parts of India. A samaja is a socio-economic and ecological-cultural region. Baba created movements for the economic and cultural liberation of these regions in India, Maharlika (Philippines) and the rest of the world.

One of the poorest samajas in India is Angadesh, the samaja in which Baba took birth. Baba created the first grammar for its suppressed language, its first newspaper and a movement for an economically and culturally independent Angadesh. Still today in many districts more than 40% of the children are malnourished and the entire economy is under the control of outside exploiters and mafias. One day in Patna, while He was sitting with some devotees from this samaja, Baba began to sing a slow, short and moving song in Sanskrit that began as follows:

SAVIOUR

Kathang krandosi Kathang mukosi, Kathang dalitosi, Mátar Anga bhúmi, Mátar Anga bhúmi, Mátar Anga bhúmi [How long will you cry? How long will you be struck dumb? How long will you be suppressed? O my motherland of Anga]

By Baba's grace, one day beloved Anga Desh will awaken and achieve the greatness it is destined for. Wherever there is any person alone and helpless, wherever there is any community suppressed and victimized, Baba is here for you. By His grace this supreme truth will reach the suffering humanity everywhere.

BRICK BRAIN

Fortunately I used to get beaten by Baba regularly. Initially, during 1966 to 1971, I reacted at being beaten in front of everyone, but after that in every RDS I was beaten by Baba. Afterward I would feel happy to have gotten His special attention because spiritual bliss always came to those who were beaten. But later on, Baba stopped beating not only me but everyone. But I was always eager to get beaten by Him.

This incident is from the period when I was RS Jaipur. The Delhi sector RDS would start on the twenty-first of every month and finish on the twenty-third. But it so happened that we would usually have to spend half of every month in Kolkata. As per rule, it was necessary for every DS (if not the DS then DitS, district secretary) to attend RDS. Representatives from each diocese had to be present. Not only this, one LFT (workers who were not monks) from every diocese had to go. He had to be trained and have had PC. Generally a person who had been graced with PC was considered a trained LFT. If any DS or LFT was not present, then the RS had to stay in Kolkata until the absent person arrived. And the criteria for acceptable progress were made for superhumans! Baba would shower us with questions. How many WTs have you made? How many schools have you opened? How many schools and ashrams are self-sustainable? The end result was that due to our guaranteed failure to meet Baba's standards we had to spend fifteen to twenty days of the month in Kolkata. Baba would always find a way to find flaws in our work so as to keep us near Him, while pretending to be furious with our failure to achieve our targets.

If these things were fulfilled then we were happy but if they were not fulfilled then we still did not feel bad because we were used to these conditions. Out of the essential requirements mentioned above, I had not opened a school in my region. Since every DS had

BRICK BRAIN

to come to Kolkata every month it was a problem from a financial point of view. Furthermore, if our school was healthy (having fifty students) then Baba would ask who was the LFT teacher? If the LFT was not trained then we would be beaten. If he was trained but had not come, then too would we be beaten. Thus if the school was healthy you would still likely end up being beaten and if it was unhealthy then of course you would be beaten. If by His grace the school was healthy and it had a trained LFT and if he was also present in Kolkata, then questions used to be raised about whether all other districts were covered or not? Our Baba would always find some kind of reason to beat us, to console us, to thrill us with His blessings, to perform the play of love. Whatever we might think, we would end up spending almost fifteen days a month in Kolkata. Baba always kept the GS and PA ready to create an atmosphere of severity and seriousness in the RDS. Where He would start, on which item of work He would focus, He alone knew. I realized this and was always prepared for the drama. Normally we like to think and plan. But Baba's mysterious demands and utter genius in generating reasons for scolding us forced us to be in a state of surrender and at the same time to be filled with joyous wonder at what His latest drama would be. This tension of waiting for Him to catch us in some mistake was also a special kind of thrill. Now that this tension is gone, how we long for it! There was such sweet stress waiting to be scolded for most mysterious reasons. We were carefree but now we are unhappy thinking that those days are no more. The great devotees were those who are experiencing His divine play all the time but we mischievous devotees do not have this capacity. So Baba has to come onto this earth just to scold, console and make us cry, feeling His tenderness. In fact Baba says this was why the universe was created.

BRICK BRAIN

Before the universe was created Parama Puruśa (Supreme Entity) was completely alone. Just think of a vast universe where there are neither stars, nor comets, nor satellites, but only one Entity – Parama Puruśa. If you remained so completely alone, how would you feel? I am sure you would go mad. Well, Parama Puruśa was in a similar situation. He had the power to speak, but there was none to hear. He had the power to laugh, but there was no occasion for laughter. He could not even cry, or scold anyone. Just think what a terrible situation He was in. Hence to perpetuate His eternal divine game – to speak, to laugh, to teach, to scold, to instruct – He manifested Himself in numerous ways. ("To Know Him Is to Be Free from All Fetters", Subháśita Saḿgraha Part 9)

After returning from morning walk, Baba used to sit take reporting. In every reporting session we experienced perfect coordination between Baba and GS Dada. In this synchronization, Acarya Sarvatmananda was very adept indeed. He used to understand the flow of Baba's organizational talks and he adeptly fulfilled his role among the workers.

When Baba arrived for reporting, GS Dada was always self-controlled. Baba would motion to him and

GS would say, "Yes, Baba!"

BRICK BRAIN

Baba would ask, "What is today?"

GS Dada would reply, for example, "To.... to... to.. today, RDS should start from Kolkata." If Baba did not say anything then reporting would start from there.

Otherwise, if Baba specifically caught someone in His special divine drama (liila) then it was a different matter altogether. For if Baba said, "Catch him, catch him," then with full vigour the GS would definitely start grilling Baba's badmash (mischievously intelligent). Generally workers were also prepared. The environment was always serious. Our hearts would be heavy due to tension. Those who had broken the code of conduct were even more worried. Some workers were there who used to get beaten regularly. I was in this category. An unforgettable incident is related to this.

That day as Baba sat upon His seat after morning walk he ordered in such a hard tone of voice, "Let Vandananda stand in front of me." I did sastaunga pranama (full prostration) and keeping my hands raised stood in front of Him.

BRICK BRAIN

Baba asked, "How many students are in your Himmatnagar school."

"Baba there are forty students."

"Why has your school not been healthy until now?" Baba said sternly.

"Baba we can run only nursery level classes there." I explained that after KG 2 (there are two stages of kindergarten in India) students used to move to other schools. And we could not add higher classes because we were not recognized. Our students would go to another school.

BRICK BRAIN

Baba immediately seized upon this point. It all went downhill from there. I said that the students moved to other schools and that was why the school was not healthy.

Baba became silent and serious. Seeking confirmation, Baba said in a serious tone, "Tell me, GS, what is in his head?"

Looking towards me the GS said, "Tell what is in your head — brick or stone?"

I quickly said, "Baba, brick!"

BRICK BRAIN

In a tone of outrage, Baba said, "What you told ...brick not stone?"

Immediately I answered, "Stone."

Again Baba asked in the same mood, "What you told ... stone?"

Immediately I replied, "No, Baba! Both brick and stone."

BRICK BRAIN

Baba laughed loudly.

Then Baba said to GS, "Vandanananda has finished my seriousness. Today I cannot take reporting. Yes, do kiirtana today." Immediately we lost all fear of being beaten and started continuous avarta kiirtana. As we sang and turned in all directions, Baba sat with His eyes closed and His head tilted irresistibly in front of us as we danced. It was just like a rose pouring perfume amid thorns. From anxiety we moved to such ecstasy that it cannot be written. This was Baba's tantric love of extreme scolding and extreme loving — we never knew when we would move from one stage to the next. In this way we would be totally absorbed in Him.

WHEN TROUBLES COME, FLY TO BABA

Baba became everything for the dadas, didis and margis. Gradually, with the passage of time, He became the Beloved at the centre of everyone's life. Right from early morning till late night, He was the centre of attention and adoration of everyone. Whenever He used to go for His walk in the morning and evening, the workers and margis used to gather well in advance near the main gate of Baba's quarters, be it in Lake Gardens or Tiljala. They used to wait in the rain or heat just to catch a glimpse of Him. Sometimes there was hardly any place for them to stand and gaze at Baba. On Saturday and Sunday, the crowd was great indeed. Every Sunday, Sadavrata (free food for people) was organized in Lake Gardens. Once, Shrii Marimurthu, from Coimbatore, came by airplane to Kolkata to see Baba. Because of some incident he was clashed out and was maintaining a low profile in Ananda Marga. No person who met Baba even once — no matter what the circumstances might have been — can ever forget Baba. His divine fatherly care, His overwhelming affection, His entrancing smile and spiritual radiance — this could not be found anywhere in this world.

At that time, Baba was composing Prabhata Samgiita, His exquisitely tender and blissful songs. He was already collecting rare plants from all over the world. Persons from all over the globe were chosen to come and join Baba in exploring the wonders of His garden. Large numbers of young men and women had already joined sannyasi life and more were waiting to join. Baba's philological revelations about the Bengali language astounded scholars and laymen alike from Bengal. In addition, His inimitable grasp of so many other languages, especially Sanskrit and the Prakrta languages, had never been seen before by these scholars. All of this together made Him deserving of unparalleled recognition. However, He was careful to leave the earth before that recognition came.

Who can forget Him? No one, no way, neither friend nor foe. Not

even those who opposed Him and His ideology and organization could remain unmoved by His radiant personality. Whether friends or foes, He left an indelible imprint in the minds of all. During His discourses, everyone planned and plotted how to get close to Him just to get one dazzling smile or one radiant namaskar. The VSS guards had a difficult time controlling the crowd.

WHEN TROUBLES COME, FLY TO BABA

Who can describe this intensity and sublimity? We cannot express how just one namaskar would cause an entire crowd of people to gasp and go into bliss. We cannot express how with one slow, resplendent smile, He could give more love and beauty than people would see in their whole lives.

Mr. Marimuthu from Coimbatore could not desist. Though he was clashed because of one incident — his family members objected to his remaining in Ananda Marga — he could not resist the attraction of Baba thousands of miles away. One morning, without letting his wife and other relations know, he went to the airport and boarded a plane for Chennai and then another for Kolkata. He came directly to Lake Gardens.

Baba used to go for His first walk at 1:30 p.m. It was perhaps a Sunday and Mr. Marimuthu was surprised to see the great number of margis pouring in and the non-margis onlookers gathered near the gate. He felt sorry because he had not been in touch with Ananda Marga and Baba for many years. He was surprised by the dynamism and progress of Ananda Marga.

He stayed near the iron gate, trying to find a place where he could peep through and see Baba behind the many people standing in front of him. Baba came and the commotion increased. Everyone was singing Prabhata Samgiita songs and then Baba Nama Kevalam kiirtana so that their love for Him could be expressed to the maximum.

WHEN TROUBLES COME, FLY TO BABA

No ordinary person could have seen a person standing far behind the crowd, but Baba knew who was where. Just calling the name "Marimuthu" and looking in his direction was enough. Baba's son pushed his way through and went to his Baba. He sat on the ground, put his head on Baba's feet and began crying. Everyone was surprised to see this unknown margi who had grabbed Baba's feet, crying. Baba's guards dared not take him away, as it was Baba, Himself who had called him.

"Marimuthu, how long will you be here?" Baba asked.

"Baba, just this evening I am going back by flight."

"You came after such a long time and are leaving so early?"

WHEN TROUBLES COME, FLY TO BABA

Marimuthu did not, could not reply. He just kept on crying and crying.

OUR FAMILY MATTERS

While we used to take our morning bath in Tiljala, during the day nearly 150 workers had to use the ground-floor bathroom of Baba's Lake Gardens house. We were supposed to take a bath at least twice a day and we had to do half-bath four times a day. Hence the water in the tank used to run out.

Once when Baba went to His bathroom, there was no water in the tap. Baba said, "How come there is no water in My bathroom? It means some sannyasis have been using the downstairs bathroom." Baba said that this was a family quarters and asked why sannyasis were using the bathroom. Henceforth, no sannyasis were allowed enter His house without His permission. Baba then said, "How has this worker (pointing to one) entered into My house without taking second lesson?" We were always laughing and enjoying when Baba expressing His annoyance at us, because we knew the love that was behind it. Like a small boy loves it when his mother becomes annoyed and scolds him, so also we loved the many small dramas that Baba created.

Lake Gardens is an upscale locality. We were not allowed to enter His quarters, and during the monsoon rains we used to take shelter under the overhanging roofs of other buildings. The people in the buildings did not allow us to enter but they could not oppose us, since we were standing on the road under the part of their roof that projected onto the street.

The guards were instructed not to allow us inside the quarters but we used to go inside under some pretext when we could. And we were very particular about taking second lesson.

OUR FAMILY MATTERS

Once, the police came and stood at the gate. The reason was something unthinkable. Baba used to take reports at midnight and if the canning was severe, the worker used to moan and say, "Mar gayá, mar gayá." (Oh, oh, I am dying, I am dying). Sometimes

mischievous workers who had not been severely beaten used to say this loudly in hopes of their beating ending soon. Sometimes they would say this even when they were not being beaten. Those moments were among the most enjoyable moments in life, but they were sometimes very irritating. This was especially the case for workers who had to wait up all night to face a beating. The neighbours lodged complaints with the police. They couldn't sleep properly and many times they heard weeping or shrieking as if someone was being hurt.

The police came to inquire into the matter. When it was reported to Baba, He declared that He would beat no more. "Let the workers go from here," He said. "I don't need them." Look here at the predicament. He didn't want the workers to be there anymore. Most of us were of the view that Baba should not call us to Lake Gardens; rather He should come to our headquarters in Tiljala during reporting time. This would be good for us and good for Him as well. Baba declared that He would not take reports anymore, nor would He go to Tiljala. Just see the predicament, a situation that He had created. No reporting, no beating and finally (therein lies the rub) no meeting with our Baba.

So to the police we all declared in one voice that this was a family matter. Who beats whom is our internal matter. No one has the right to say anything. We told the police that no such thing was happening at any hour.

OUR FAMILY MATTERS

The ones who benefitted most were the shop owners in the Lake Gardens area. They usually ran their businesses at a loss when there was no reporting. They were happy to get our business, but with the neighbours it was a different story. Their comments ran along the lines of: "What kind of a householder is He that He has hypnotized these youths to become sannyasis." They dared not oppose us openly since they knew Baba had been in jail and that even the thuggish CPI(M) was afraid of Him. They realised He was indeed a powerful man.

When Baba shifted to Tiljala, the most affected were the neighbours. The area became unsafe. Their girls were harassed. Cases of robbery and looting became routine affairs. Later they realized that when we sannyasis were present, they were secure even at the late hours of the night. Many shops closed down also.

I received blessings almost every month in Lake Gardens in the form of beatings. The way Baba was in His residence, which was filled with flowers (both flora and human), can never be forgotten. In Tiljala, Baba's residence was cut off from us. In Lake Gardens, we were His unruly, naughty but loving children driving Him to distraction. We were the most blessed people in the universe when after a scolding His tenderness would make us cry far, far more than any beating could. This was indeed a special arena of Baba's liila.

BEATING CURES BLINDNESS

Baba almost always sent me to difficult places to work. I was the DS of Dharwad in Karnataka for three years. The very day I felt that now I was comfortable. Acarya Bodhaprajinananda came to replace me as DS and I was reposted as RS Jaipur.

It should be mentioned here, that Jaipur Region was considered to be a very difficult field. We only had a single Ananda Marga monk from that region, which covers Gujarat and Rajasthan. The pressure to create workers and LFTs was such that three RSs had left the field in the past eight months. Baba said, "Send Vandananda. He will do the work." I should say that by Baba's grace I was there for more than three years. Three persons became monks. I had many beautiful experiences while working there.

Once while traveling in a Jaipur city bus after returning from RDS in Kolkata, I noticed that the vision of my left eye had become hazy. I could not read the signboards along the road. I checked this repeatedly while on the bus.

Upon reaching the jagriti, I contacted Shrii Mangal Bihariji, a leading margi in Jaipur, and told him about my eye problem. He took me to the hospital. The doctor told me that soon I would lose vision in that eye. He said the efferent nerve was drying up and if not controlled (which is normally not possible) the eye becomes sightless.

BEATING CURES BLINDNESS

I was shocked and saddened. I had never had any pain in my eye. The doctor gave me eyedrops. I went to Kolkata and requested that, Baba be consulted about it, if possible. On many occasions in the past Baba had cured my stomach ailments by touching the area in RDS. He had cured my TB also. I was in Kolkata for a few days but I felt Baba was not giving me the chance to speak to Him about my problem, so I decided that I should go back to my posting and concentrate on my duty to Him.

I reached Ahmedabad and went straightaway to an eye hospital. The doctor diagnosed it as central serous retinopathy and I became a rare specimen for the students to study.

The next time, I did not go for reporting. Baba asked the GS why I had not come. The GS told Baba about my eye problem.

Baba said, "I have already cured his eye problem when he was given forty cannings on his head during the previous RDS. Tell Vandanananda that he should not be worried."

BEATING CURES BLINDNESS

I was staying in the brothers training centre in Ahmedabad. When I received a telephone call revealing Baba's blessing, tears rolled down my eyes.

The canning on my head was one of the strangest ways of curing a disease and giving a blessing. Two RDSs back, Baba had called for me after sitting on His sacred dais. I remembered how many times in RDS or elsewhere, Baba would say:

"I am with you all the time."

"You cannot hide from Me."

BEATING CURES BLINDNESS

"I love you the most."

In these ways, Baba used to tighten the strings of loving closeness whenever He found that there was any slackness or lack of eager ardour in my heart in this relationship of pure oneness.

"Where is Vandanananda?" Baba had asked. He was stern and harsh. I was chatting with Acarya Viireshvarananda at the back of the meditation room in Lake Gardens.

The RDS is one way of screening of a bhairava (one who is initiated into tantric meditation by Lord Shiva). I remember once Baba said in Ranchi when He introduced the system of RDS that Lord Shiva used to treat His disciples in the same way. He would ask the sadhaka to raise both hands and then the Lord would screen them, as if putting them through an MRI, body, mind and soul; past, present and future. Then He would do what needed to be done.

BEATING CURES BLINDNESS

I did sastaunga pranama to Baba and stood up with both my hands up.

At that time, Baba had stopped beating people Himself. Instead he told the GS to carry out the beatings. Baba told the GS, "Hit his head with the cane twenty times. And there should be the sound of 'thud.'"

"Do you know why you are getting this beating?" Baba asked.

"No," I said.

BEATING CURES BLINDNESS

"You have been cursing Me for the sweet you ate this month in that margi's house."

I remembered how a margi sister, the wife of R. C. Chaturvedi in Rajasthan, had served me laddoos prepared with ghee and dal. It is a Rajasthani dish famed for its delicacy and sweetness.

She knew full well that for the last three years I was not eating any sweets. She was very happy because I had helped arrange the marriage of her daughter, and especially because the marriage had been blessed by Baba.

When I was having dinner, she put a laddoo on my plate. I said, "Ma, you know I don't take sweets." She replied with commanding love and respect that I must take it.

BEATING CURES BLINDNESS

I started eating it but every time I opened my mouth to swallow I felt a strong aversion. I cursed Baba each time silently in my mind. Now in RDS I began cursing myself for blaming Baba for the situation that had cropped up at the dinner table. Then I said, "Baba! I had not been taking any sweets for three years but that evening this margi sister forced me to take that sweet."

"What!" Baba said. "GS cane him twenty more times. Why has he not been taking sweets?"

And so I got another twenty canings. All together it was forty canings on my head.

Then He gave me many instructions about what I should and should not eat. He gave me some personal and general do's and don'ts regarding diet. He said that raw rice is much healthier than pre-boiled rice. Raw rice needs more time to be digested. When taken with ghee it does not cause constipation.

BEATING CURES BLINDNESS

Spinach, Bengal gram leaves, cauliflower, and cabbage are good

for a person with a weak stomach. He said that overripe bananas with cow's milk are both food and medicine for people with weak stomachs. Similarly, soup made from ripe tomatoes is also good for health.

Then Baba explained how to prepare the soup. "Boil the tomatoes in a pot. Put just enough water so that the tomatoes are only half covered by it. When the tomato skin is cracked, take it out and pulverize it in the same water. Add salt and drink it. This is cheaper and healthier." Baba said it was better for me not to take food after 8:00 p.m.

Baba said that He had not included in the 16 Points that sweets should not be taken. Only that they should not be taken regularly. So I got a beating for both reasons: when I ate sweets and for not having eaten sweets. Is this not a strange way of creating a situation where the devotee is trapped with nowhere to go? That was my state — what to do?

BEATING CURES BLINDNESS

A devotee is absorbed in giving love to their Beloved by dhyana, by relief work, by fighting against exploitation, and in so many other ways. Such devotees react only to the liilas — the divine play and drama — of their Beloved Baba. Just a small gesture of His can make them go mad with joy or cry their hearts out. They are partially or totally blind to the world of physical and psychic pleasures. When they do enjoy any physical or psychic pleasure they are sharing that enjoyment with Him. When eating a sweet they will relish that sweet and offering that enjoyment to Baba so that they feel Him enjoying it as they do. They alone are able to go beyond indulgence and repugnance, attachment and aversion.

But let us come to the mystic aspect of how Baba saved me. When GS dada said, "Baba, Vandanananda is admitted in the eye hospital and the doctor says one eye will become sightless," Baba commanded GS to tell Vandanananda that he has already been cured of the problem by being caned forty times on the head.

Just think, before our suffering comes Baba takes that suffering upon Himself, and to teach us He gives us small suffering. We grumble, complain or throw our hands in the air but we don't realize that this minor suffering is the medicine for far more serious problems in future, just like the pain of the injection prevents the terrible suffering of a deadly disease. So like small children, we cry, complain, or glumly resign ourselves or we get fed up with our Father. Not only do we not realize how much love there is behind that little suffering He gives, we also shamefully fail to realize how much pain He is undergoing by taking on our suffering.

Most importantly, we never seriously ponder and wonder about the boundless depths of His love. The love He has for us is such that He will again and again happily suffer for us without any hesitation (even when we exploit His kindness) and without ever revealing this suffering to us, let alone asking even for a word of thanks. So without letting me know, Baba cured my eye problem by taking my samskaras. That is why He is Hari, the Divine Thief.

LOVE BRINGS BABA ALWAYS

There was a young girl named Rajeshrii from Paraguay. She was weak in both legs. She was walking with the help of crutches. Although she wanted to be Baba's avadhutika or even LFT (local full-time worker), she could not because she herself needed help. She came to Kolkata to see Baba. Baba was mostly staying at Lake Gardens at that time. The official name given by Baba to Lake Gardens was Madhu Malaiñca, which meant "sweet flower garden." He used to come to Tiljala only when there was a workers RDS on the weekend. Otherwise, He mostly remained at Lake Gardens. While coming back from field walk, He sometimes used to walk through His garden. He was the Baba of the plants too and would give them time and attention and would even write small poems for them. Sometimes He used to give a plant His special smile, just like He did with us human beings.

This sister was very sad because she could not either become an LFT or a didi and therefore, as per the rules at that time, she would not be graced with the chance to talk to Baba. She also was not allowed to be a bhukti pradhana (margi in charge of service activities of AMPS in a particular area) and hence was not eligible to meet Baba in bhukti pradhana and upbhukti pradhana reporting sessions.

She was able to see and hear about all Baba's wonderful liilas in Madhu Malaiñca with others but unable to experience it herself. Baba expressed this pain in one of His songs about Madhu Malaiñca:

LOVE BRINGS BABA ALWAYS

4328 (06/04/1988)
KENO BHULE GELE,
KII CHILO ÁMÁR TRUT́I VICYUTI,
MOR MADHU MALAINCE ÁJO ÁNKÁ
RAHE GECHE HE PRIYA TOMÁR SMRITI
KII CHILO ÁMÁR TRUT́I VICYUTI,
ALAKÁR JE KUSUM ALAKE GÁNTHÁ CHILO
AMARÁR SUDHÁ TÁRE AKSÁR KARE CHILO
CYUTA MUKULER MADHU MANE MANE KAHE CHILO
BHULILEO SE BOJHE PRIITI;
KII CHILO ÁMÁR TRUT́I VICYUTI
CHILO NÁ VIBHAVA MOR PRÁPTIR PRATULATÁ
CHILO SHUDHU EŚAŃÁ, ÁRTI O ÁKULATÁ
CARAŃE ÁCARAŃE DIINATÁR MAONATÁ
DÚRE THEKE JÁNIÁCHI SHARAŃÁGATI
KII CHILO ÁMÁR TRUT́I VICYUTI,
KENO BHULE GELE,
KII CHILO ÁMÁR TRUT́I VICYUTI
O why do You remain oblivious to me?
What were my defects and failures?
In Madhu Malaiñca
Even today, I mark this day
And keep remembering You,
O my Beloved.
O, what were my defects and failures?
Your divine flower that was
Woven within my hair,
Its immortal nectar
Is unsullied and undecaying.
The honey of that fallen flower
Speaks forth today
In my mind, O in my mind.
O, what were my defects and failures?
I never wanted opulence
Profits or prosperity.
I was only longing for,
Searching for that
Intensity of longing and
To live in poverty, in silence.
At Your Feet.
This was my character.
I knew this from way back
And that is why
I am moving, rushing
Towards Your Shelter.
O, what were my defects and failures?

This sister then decided to garland Baba. But how was the question. She engaged a taxi from the didi's quarters in Tiljala and went to the flower market where she bought the best garland available. She came to Madhu Malaiñca well in advance, walking slowly with her crutches. She had carefully planned exactly where to stand so that when the rear door of Baba's car would be opened for Baba by His bodyguard, Baba would come out right in front of her. And if the chance came, she would be ready with her garland for Baba. I saw this sister go through this routine every day and every day it would end in failure. One week, two weeks, three weeks passed. But Baba did not look at her. He just went for field walk and came back.

LOVE BRINGS BABA ALWAYS

Finally, it was the last day in India. Her visa was expiring the next day and she had her return flight to Paraguay. She had booked a taxi for the airport from the Tiljala didi's quarters, via Madhu Malaiñca, and had loaded it with her baggage. She had gone to the flower shop and bought the best garland and positioned herself perfectly as always.

Everyone present was singing Prabhata Samgiita and then Baba Nama Kevalam. The moment the devotees got the signal that Baba was coming downstairs, they started singing His name and jockeyed for position so that they could see Him clearly and get as close as possible to Him. Baba sat in the car and left for field walk. This sister became very sad. She consoled herself that Baba would come

back and accept her garland. She remained standing on her steel crutches, which were attached to her forearms. She closed her eyes and concentrated on Baba.

Then Baba's car arrived back and came inside. The guard opened the rear car door and Baba walked ahead to the stairs that led to the first floor. Baba had walked eight or nine feet. What had happened to her intense desire for Baba, her loving, divine Father? She was silently weeping.

LOVE BRINGS BABA ALWAYS

Lo! Baba walked back eight or nine steps and stood in front of her. With a loving, wonderful smile, He said, "O my little child, do you want to garland Me? Do it." Then Baba bowed His head low so that she could garland her Baba without any trouble.

Thus after taking her to the height of longing and the height of her devotion, Baba fulfilled her desire.

FLOOD IN TILJALA

Tiljala is situated in a low-lying area that would often flood during the monsoon season. Once in 1986 during the rainy season there was torrential rain and the ground floor and the meditation hall were flooded. Baba was in His quarters, Madhu Koraka, in Tiljala when the water reached chest high all around.

Many margis and RDS workers were trapped in the building. It was hard for us all to get food and drinking water. Of course there was no electricity. However we were all cozy like a family in a storm because Baba was there and we did not feel any hardship.

In order to provide more comfort for Baba, we felt that we should shift Him to Lake Gardens. So a boat was arranged and Baba and His PA Keshavananda stepped into the boat. I and other workers swam alongside the boat as it slowly took our Baba away from Tiljala.

Some more workers like Liilabodhananda and Laliteshvarananda joined with great enthusiasm the groups who were accompanying the boat. Seeing Baba slowly move on this boat while we moved with Him was a most precious liila. Baba was sitting on the small boat of His mission that is ever floating in the universe and we small boys were swimming beside Him trying to care for Him and to catch a closer glimpse of our Beloved Father.

FLOOD IN TILJALA

Someone commented later, "Look, the Lord who takes mortal human beings across the ocean of this creation to the land of immortality is Himself being taken upon a journey by mortal people." We are very grateful that Acarya Pranavatmakananda was there to videograph this precious journey of Baba.

Thereafter we did a lot of relief work in that area. The whole area of Tiljala was marooned and we workers and margis threw ourselves into the task of serving the local people, providing them with food and other supplies. Baba said that when we start to do service we

should thank the Lord that He has blessed us with the chance to love Him by serving Him in the form of His creation. When we are blessed to do this kind of AMURT work we feel such joy that we cannot stop thanking Baba for gracing us with this opportunity.

GREENHOUSE GAMES

Baba used to create so many dramas. One precious drama was to erect a greenhouse. He wanted to keep plants from high altitudes in His garden and so He needed a greenhouse. The responsibility was given to Dada Pranavananda, who was widely respected for his intellect, hard work and great efficiency. Baba most of the time kept him in central posts.

To erect a greenhouse requires special technology, expertise and money. Baba called Pranavananda Dada and said, "You are to take care of this responsibility." Dada readily agreed. Then Baba said, "Look here. Some plants have already come. They won't survive. Do you want them to die?"

The pressure to build a greenhouse began to mount, as Dada was only given one week's time to complete the construction. This was not humanly possible. So Dada was punished and beaten left and right. Another three to four days were given by Baba, but still the work was not yet done. Again came another fine beating. Then the third and final date elapsed.

Baba declared that He did not want to see Dada's face anymore and that Dada should not come to His building. Baba said, "I do not want to see his face or hear his voice."

GREENHOUSE GAMES

By His grace, Dada completed the greenhouse. Before anyone could tell Baba the good news, Baba called His PA and said that He did not want the greenhouse anymore because that man (i.e. Dada) had lied to Him. Baba said despite whatever progress had been made, it was still made of glass. Baba said that with a strong kick, He would break that glass.

GS, PA and myself were all there enjoying the drama. The PA and GS Dada replied, "Yes, Baba, the greenhouse will be broken with one good kick of Yours."

Baba said, "Yes, you are right." He put on His shoes and took His

stick — in those days, He used a walking stick to support Himself, even when coming for reporting.

GREENHOUSE GAMES

With a big smile and a mischievous expression on His face, He got up from His cot so that He could go to the roof and break the greenhouse.

Suddenly Baba's PA said, "Baba! Pranavanandji is just sitting there on the stairs leading to the greenhouse."

"What! How come he is there. His entry has been banned."

Suddenly Pranavananda's voice could be heard saying, "You neither want to see me nor hear my voice. How then can You go up?" Dada had heard of Baba's plan to destroy the greenhouse and had taken this step to block Baba from going upstairs because Baba had claimed that He did not want to see Dada.

GREENHOUSE GAMES

Pretending to be angry, Baba turned to His PA and said, "Look here, Pranavananda has played a trick on Me."

Then Baba went back to His cot and removed His shoes, accepting the presence of the greenhouse.

The greenhouse was ready. Soon afterward, Dada was restored to Baba's favour and this drama came to an end.

The real delight of this drama is how adorable Baba would be when He became angry, claiming not to want to see His beloved child and trying to destroy the greenhouse. Every child knows how adorable their mother can be when angry externally but full of love internally. This is worldly joy. But the bliss of seeing Baba in great anger while inside being full of such tremendous love — this is the joy of the devotee.

NO COMPROMISE WITH EXPLOITERS

The Tata company is one of the oldest and most powerful capitalist firms in India. It made its first fortunes by participating in the British crime of selling opium in China after having defeated the Chinese army. This company still controls a large section of the Indian economy.

In Tatanagar, there is a park named Jubilee Park that is owned and constructed by the Tata company. It is very nicely maintained and everyone likes to visit there. Once during DMC in Tatanagar, Baba's car was taken to Jubilee Park.

When Baba's car arrived, He asked where He was. When Baba came to know that this was a park developed by the Tata company, He refused to step out of the car.

Baba said, "This park was developed and is being maintained with the blood of the people." Baba declared that he would not put His foot down in this park. Thus He demonstrated how capitalism is anti-human and a bloodsucking system.

NO COMPROMISE WITH EXPLOITERS

Baba lived up to His famous words uttered almost thirty years earlier when He said:

Regarding earning money and hoarding wealth, most capitalists today are pishácavats (ghouls). In Sanskrit pisháca means "one who breaks the neck of an animal and then sucks out all the blood, leaving only the flesh and bones". In India it is said that it is extremely difficult to understand the nature of these bloodsucking pisháca capitalists — because when they drink water, even if it is already pure, they filter it; but on other occasions they drink people's blood, which, even if it is not pure, they do not bother to filter! ("Various Occupations," Human Society Part 1)

A ROTTEN POTATO

Once during reporting in Kolkata the RSs were present. We all occupied one room in Tiljala and we had a nice group feeling, sharing the pressure and joy together as a team.

One afternoon, Baba called all of us to the upper side room of his Tiljala quarters. We were in a relaxed and leisurely mood. All our DSs had been asked to return to their field and our supervisory responsibilities were over for a short time.

Baba was sitting on His cot. The GS and a few other central workers were present. Baba questioned RS Kolkata as to how many workers his region had created in one month. Dada replied "eight". Baba became furious. Dada was punished with great severity and instantly transferred. Baba was in a very different mood. Then RS Ranchi replied five and he was flogged and transferred. Allahabad and Chandigarh RSs were severely punished and transferred also. When my turn came, my heart and breath stopped. I was sure the sky would fall on me because I had created zero.

Baba looked at me and said, "He is a rotten potato. Don't touch it. Throw it in the corner. It is smelling badly."

A ROTTEN POTATO
