Then the Mumbai, Bangalore and other RSs were dealt with and all were transferred. Baba then left the room.

At this time another unusual event was happening. GS Dada and Vijayanandji both had told me that in the past three months Baba said on several occasions, "Vandanananda speaks, reads and understands Bengali." Actually I do not pick up languages or learn them well. Also I was not so fond of Bengali at the time. But seeing Baba's desire for me to learn Bengali, I bought Ananda Marga books in Bengali and took them back to Jaipur to study.

After three months I was transferred like the other RSs. I was posted as DS Pune, which was another area where there was a great struggle to maintain a rented jagriti. When I reached there, I found that the jagriti building was locked and the DS had left the field. The rent had not been paid for four years and we owed Rs. 14,000. I began looking up my old contacts when I was RS Mumbai, one of whom, Subodh Sinha, was now a building contractor. He was very happy when he came to know that I was posted there. He took me to his house and got one floor registered in the name of Ananda Marga. Much later in reporting, after my transfer, I mentioned his name to Baba about him having donated a building to the organization and Baba said that he was a good man. Later he used to regularly attend DMC and have Baba's Darshan (contact). After 1990 I again visited Pune and got another building registered for our organization with the help of the same gentleman. It is being used as a jagriti.

After acquiring the Pune jagriti, when I came to Kolkata for reporting, I was told that I could not appear before Baba because I had been transferred as trainer of the Varanasi training centre and I was supposed to have already gone there.

A ROTTEN POTATO

This was indeed a remarkable turn of affairs. For many years, no land or building at Pune could be arranged and within one month I got the building registered and opened a school. In that one month I had also learnt a little Bengali and just when I thought that now I could comfortably stay in Pune, I was transferred to the training centre where speaking Bengali was compulsory. Baba had told me that I must converse with the trainees from Bengal in Bengali. They are very sentimental about their language. Baba said that this way they would feel closer to me. Though my knowledge of Bengali is limited, even nowadays I try to speak with them in Bengali. Another interesting fact is that whenever I attended reporting regarding the Varanasi training centre, I used to speak to Baba in Bengali, He always used to talk with me in English.

On one occasion, He asked me to answer a question related to PROUT in Chattisgarhi. He told me that Bengalis are more sentimental about their language whereas Oriya speakers are less sentimental. Baba said that the Chattisgarhi people have even less sentiment for their language. He said that this has to be awakened.

Everywhere we look, we see that people are most exploited where their language is undeveloped or unrecognized. Baba explained why:

Culture denotes all sorts of human expressions. The best possible means of communicating these human expressions is through one's mother tongue, as this is most natural. If people's natural expression through their mother tongue is hampered then inferiority complexes will grow in their minds. This will encourage a defeatist mentality, which ultimately leads to psycho-economic exploitation. An example is the imposition of the Hindi language by a section of Indian leaders as the national language of India. Hindi is not the natural language of the people in Uttar Pradesh, Bihar, etc. There are many local dialects in these regions, which need immediate encouragement. To utilize the sentimental legacy of the people, people must raise their socio-economic consciousness, know who their exploiters are, expose psycho-economic exploitation and become imbued with fighting spirit. ("Developmental Planning," Proutist Economics)

WE ARE ACCOUNTABLE TO HUMANITY

Baba founded Ananda Marga with the objective of átmamokśárthaḿ jagaddhitáya ca (self-realization and all-round service to humanity). He declared that morality (yama & niyama) was the base, sadhana (spiritual and social) was the means and Life Divine is the goal. Baba said that ensconcing oneself in the Cosmic Soul is the Life Divine. Ananda Marga keeps its doors open always for everyone. Many dadas and margis feel sometimes Ananda Marga belongs to them alone and that Baba is the Guru, the Supreme Father or Parama Purusa for them only.

In jail, however, when Baba was asked if He was for Ananda Margis alone, Baba immediately and forcefully replied (as reported in the June 1978 issue of Crimson Dawn),

"Yes, I am not only for Ananda Margis. I could not, should not and must not be. To the organization, one may say who is Ananda Margi and who is not, but I cannot tell who is margi and who is not. All are one in My eyes."

Baba founded Ananda Marga and propounded His philosophy, gave His Tantra sadhana, spread Neohumanism, laboured for PROUT and composed Prabhat Samgiit for everyone — not just for those who have joined Ananda Marga Pracaraka Samgha. In fact, AMPS is just one instrument to manifest His objectives for the welfare of all beings. This is why He said in Patna, on January 3, 1979:

WE ARE ACCOUNTABLE TO HUMANITY

Today, you should remember that Ananda Marga is not limited to a handful of honest and intellectual people. Rather, it is trying to rally thousands of people under the banner of one sublime ideology. I dedicate Ananda Marga in this DMC to all sections of society. Prior to this DMC, I would only discuss intellectual topics, but from this DMC onwards I have become a man of the people because I believe that Dharma

is the property of the masses, not the exclusive monopoly of a handful of so-called educated, middle-class elite. Dharma belongs to one and all. From this DMC, I recognize it as the property of the common people. (“Spiritual Truth Surpasses Everything,” Ánanda Vacanámrtam Part 7)

Each day, Baba would ask someone to read the newspaper to Him. Often there was only time to read the headlines. One day, when the news was being read out to Him, He heard that two people in China had died of starvation. When the reading of the news was over, Baba asked all the workers to come and see Him. I also happened to be there.

Baba asked the general secretary, Acarya Sarveshvarananda, “How come these two persons in China died of starvation? You are the general secretary of the entire humanity and the Food and Care Department is to provide food security to all and take care of the needs of everyone.” The GS was punished for failing to fulfill responsibilities that he never knew were his.

WE ARE ACCOUNTABLE TO HUMANITY

Thus Baba made it crystal clear that Ananda Marga Pracaraka Samgha and its office holders are accountable for everyone on this planet, not just the present-day followers of Ananda Marga Pracaraka Samgha. Ananda Marga Pracaraka Samgha is for one and all. Baba is the Father of one and all and this includes our plant and animal relatives. Ananda Margis thus have the bounden duty of serving all beings.

On April 5, 1989, Baba demanded that margis take full responsibility for the hungry and starving people in the world. In a special message that speaks to us today, as so many people in this global economic depression fall prey to malnutrition and starvation, Baba said:

We must do something concrete for these people, otherwise the hunger of these downtrodden and distressed people will destroy the peace and tranquility of the entire globe. You must not forget this fact. There should be an immediate influx of food from outside to these malnourished areas.
When you are taking responsibility for the entire globe, solving the problem of malnutrition is also your sacred duty. What is the cause of this problem? The cause is irrational distribution in the economies of the world. The long-term solution to this economic problem is PROUT, but the immediate food shortages themselves must be solved as an urgent necessity. What are you doing about this? You cannot shirk your responsibility. The entire globe is waiting for you. (“Multi-Purpose Development Schemes,” Proutist Economics)

However, it was not only for the great issues that we disciples of Baba have full responsibility. It is also for the minutest responsibilities as well that we may deem unimportant but which have a pervasive impact on society. Some time after this incident, there was a DMC held at Ananda Nagar. One afternoon Baba arrived and expressed a desire to drink some milk. This desire of Baba was totally unexpected since the cows in our dairy farm had already been milked.

WE ARE ACCOUNTABLE TO HUMANITY

Some leftover milk in a can that had not been sold was brought to Baba to drink. After being boiled, it was served to Baba. After one or two sips, Baba became angry. Water had been added to the milk. The GS, Acarya Sarveshvarananda, was summoned and questioned by Baba. “How did this milk become adulterated and that too in Ananda Nagar?”

The GS was in no way responsible for this corruption but still he was accountable as the head of the organization. He was accountable for whatever happened in the organization and in the society. Baba demanded that every disciple be held accountable for the corrupt activities of everyone in their workplace or their organizational posting. In this case, the poor people of Ananda Nagar were being cheated and the honour of Ananda Marga was being stained. Then Baba said, “Since this adulteration happens in Ananda Nagar, the headquarters of Ananda Marga, you being the GS are responsible for this.”

The GS dada was punished and then transferred to a lower post. In these ways, Baba forced His disciples to be accountable for the suffering in society.

NO EEH AND OOH

Baba once told us a very educative story. Near Mokama (Ambabhumi; the famed, fertile land of Magadha) the Ganges splits in two. There was an island at this point in the middle of this vast river. Upon this island lived a fakir (Sufi saint) and his disciples. Every day the disciples would go by boat to the shore and beg for some raw food that they would bring back to the island. They would cook a meal for everyone and then give the remains of the food to the fish, tortoises and crocodiles.

Though completely isolated from normal life, the fame of that fakir spread. Three Britishers heard of him and wanted to meet him. When they reached the shore of the mainland opposite the island, they encountered the small boat with the three disciples of the fakir who had come for begging. The mainland coast was marshy and covered with water, and the Britishers did not want to step into that muddy water to get into the boat. Having the ruler complex, they told those three humble disciples to line up on hands and knees so they could walk on their backs to the boat without getting their boots dirty.

The disciples humbly complied. The British walked over them onto the boat. They were wearing hiking shoes with spikes on the bottom and there was a gap between the third disciple and the boat. In order to reach the boat they had to take a big step, which required pushing down hard on the back of the last and youngest disciple causing him to bleed and suffer greatly. Unable to bear the spikes on his back, the young disciple cried out in pain. "Eeeh, oooh." Eee is a sound expressing pain and oooh is a severe expression of pain.

While the Britishers were sailing to the island, a sudden strong wind overturned their boat and they drowned.

NO EEH AND OOH

When the disciples returned to the island, that fakir (their guru)

took a burning piece of wood and started beating the youngest disciple until he became unconscious.

Baba's maternal uncle was present at that time. He asked the fakir why was his nephew was being beaten when he had already suffered greatly from the spiked boots of the Britishers.

Baba then said:

NO EEH AND OOH

"Listen to the answer the fakir gave: 'They should have not agreed to the proposal of the Englishmen. But if they had agreed and taken the samkalpa (vow) to do this, thinking that it was for their master, then they should not have expressed their pain. Because of the expression of pain by this disciple, Prakrti (Nature) could not tolerate it and punished the Englishmen. So this disciple is responsible for their death.'"

Baba explained that when a disciple has taken a samkalpa to do something for their master, no matter what happens they should not express whatever pain they may face due to the various obstacles that arise.

Later in Shabda Cayanika, Baba said that if a genuine sannyasi expresses pain, Nature cannot tolerate it and will punish the guilty party.

THE HIGHEST EXAMPLE OF HUMANITY

I was RS Jaipur Region for more than three years. Many memorable experiences happened during this period. This is one of them, the story of how I became a Brahmacarii once again for a few weeks. Baba made a new rule that turned out to be one of His special liilas. As a rule, if any worker under one's supervision becomes negative and leaves the organization, the immediate supervisory worker and the department head will lose their avadhutaship. In order to get back their avadhutaship, each one has to create two workers. Thus the organization will gain four workers against the loss of one worker. As time rolled on, the speed of the organization increased and more organizational rules were introduced. This was with the intention that the maximum organizational output should be achieved on the minimum amount of time. Under these rules, the workers were engaged in work alone. Though objectively the pressure of doing missionary work was tremendous, yet the workers were psychologically relaxed; they enjoyed the pressure. The interesting, rather I should say, the blissful part of all this was that if the target given by dearest Baba was not achieved, the workers concerned were made to undergo punishment.

Caning, beating, hopping while doing knee bends were common practice. In the beginning, when any rule or practice was introduced, the targets were impossible or at least required superhuman capacity. The workers enjoyed it. They took it as beloved Baba playing with His devotees. This was considered an essential ingredient of Tantra. Almost everyone accepted in his or her heart and mind that getting beaten, caned, or becoming the subject of mockery in the presence of others was nothing but a drama enacted by our revered Baba. This was the devotional spirit that pervaded our minds.

Even then, sometimes the question used to float in the mind as to how this would establish Baba's mission for suffering humanity. But

still, everyone including our Baba was immersed in the full flow of the One Love that bonded us and fused us together.

THE HIGHEST EXAMPLE OF HUMANITY

Those who couldn't keep up with this speed and tried to analyze it intellectually got stuck midway and left the blissful organizational flow. Now let me come to that incident which, I believe, is one of those linked with this flow.

I was the RS and there were twelve workers immediately under me — ten diocese secretaries and two district secretaries. Rajasthan and Gujarat were in Jaipur Region. As per the new rule, one representative from each diocese had to be present every month in the sectorial RDS in Kolkata. Arrival time was before midnight of the twentieth of every month. The reporting used to start by ten or eleven in the morning. The sectorial secretary, the general secretary and the department head used to count the number of representatives from the morning of the twentieth. They wanted to be sure that all had shown their heads. They were more particular about my region. They knew that this region was weak with regard to commitment to organizations like Ananda Marga. Marwaris and Gujaratis are rich in wealth but poor in Tantric lifestyle based on fighting inner and social vices. We were unable to create sannyasis from these two states, except for one from Kota (Hadoti Samaj, which Baba said was a most ancient civilization).

During my tenure, three avadhutas were created but due to the pressure of work they left. The creation of three workers added a feather to my cap and despite declining progress, revered Baba let me stay on as RS. The previous three RSs had been removed from this region within the previous seven or eight months. This was not because I had any capacity but because of His grace.

As mentioned, Baba had instituted a new rule stating that each diocese must have one LFT and one dada present every month. The LFTs were required to reach on the seventeenth. My only schools were in Ajmer and Kota. However I had no LFTs and the Himmatnagar school had only forty students. We could start nursery classes only in Gujarat. We were not able to fulfill the terms and conditions required by the government to run a school.

THE HIGHEST EXAMPLE OF HUMANITY

When we had opened one more primary school in Kota, I came to know about the new set of rules given by our Baba. I was quite sure I wouldn't be able to implement these rules. Along with these rules, one more condition was demanded. Each region must send candidates for Personal Contact with Baba to Kolkata. Personal Contact was the most special blessing in a person's life. Many were so dramatically transformed by it that they became lost in samadhi or became dynamic workers or left Ananda Marga due to their inability to assimilate the spiritual vibrations of Baba's love. Those who left will return to the path of spirituality in future lives and that is why Baba was so eager to bless people with Personal Contact — even those whom He knew would leave Him or betray Him. When I think of the depths of Baba's obsession with blessing us, I become choked with tears.

The conditions that a worker had to follow generated an avalanche of expectations. There were moral codes, conduct rules, the opening of schools, children's homes, jagritis, printing presses, publishing newspapers in local languages, inspiring and caring for junior workers and so on. As Baba said in one of His songs:

ÁMÁY CHOTTA EKTI MAN DIYECHO,
ANEK ÁSHÁ REKHE (Song 29)
You have given me a tiny mind,
But from it You expect O, so much.

All these demands were occupying the mind along with the thought that no one except a lunatic or a die-hard devotee with full faith in Baba could manage to cope with all of this, let alone move ahead dynamically. These endless, impossible demands forced absolute surrender and also forced us to keep thinking about Baba either out of surrender or out of fear of punishment. This pressure sometimes caused dramatic spiritual progress as our love for Him increased tremendously. Baba explained this in talking about how Krsna created spiritual warriors during the Mahabharata era: Krsna wanted to arouse a strong urge in people's minds to fight against evil forces. Although Krsna did not provide any socio-economic-political theory, He made people conscious in the social, economic and cultural spheres, and advanced society by providing inspiration in His own unique way. That is, Párthasárathi Krsna helped the people to advance by expanding their social consciousness. That is why those who

THE HIGHEST EXAMPLE OF HUMANITY

had received schooling from Párthasárathi jumped to the stage of sársthi (between the eyebrows) right from the lowermost stage of sálokya (along the spine opposite the male reproductive organ). They did not rise gradually to successively higher stages (of spiritual realization) through joy and sweet blissful experiences, but advanced to the highest stage all at once. But this sudden expansion involved labour, toil and struggle. It entailed great risk and demanded uncompromising fighting spirit. (From Discourse 3, Namámi Krsńasundaram)

Whenever I reached our headquarters in Tiljala or Baba's home in Lake Gardens, Kolkata, I found in these two places — pure LIVING. The absolute and true way of existing and being alive I found where Baba lived. I used to find myself in the flow of that LIVING. How I used to enjoy!

But back to the story. When the new rule was told to me, I smiled with acceptance and tried to convince myself with faith and dedication to my Guru, Lord Anandamurti. When all the ten RSs from Delhi Sector discussed this new rule, we put our hands over our heads and said, "How is this possible?" I said that we should move onward with the spirit of the time, for what else could we do.

I was a bit apprehensive about one DS of Udaipur who was a new brahmacarii. I was not sure whether he would join me in Kolkata RDS. The field was tough also when it came to raising funds for travel. Anyway, in order to be sure about him joining me, I telephoned him that I would be there at 5 a.m. and the same day we would start for Kolkata. When I reached there, I found him missing from the jagriti. When I enquired from the neighbours, I found out that at three or four that very same morning this young worker had left with all his belongings. I was not sure where he had gone — whether he had gone home or had gone to do relief work in a nearby district affected by floods. However, I had a sinking feeling that most likely he had left.

THE HIGHEST EXAMPLE OF HUMANITY

I was in a fix. I reached Kolkata to be sure that he had indeed left. On the morning of the twenty-first, as the RDS session began, the general secretary and Baba asked me if everything was all right — have all required workers come. The GS shrewdly passed the question onto us and started enquiring from the RSs one after another regarding the presence of their representatives. The workers of Kolkata region had already gone to the field with their LFTs since their IRSS (RDS) started on the eleventh and ended on the thirteenth of every month. Nevertheless RS Kolkata used to join us in reporting.

Everyone on this earth thinks that all should move in harmony all the time as per our wishes, but of course this never happens. The wish of Parama Purusa prevails everywhere. Why worry? When this thought came to me, I mischievously said, although it was unlikely, "Baba, one of my DSs has not reported. I think he has gone to do relief work in one of the worst affected areas of another district."

"Relief?" Baba asked.

"Yes," I replied.

THE HIGHEST EXAMPLE OF HUMANITY

Baba commented with a knowing smile, "If anyone does relief work to save humanity, that is good indeed." The GS started to question another RS. I thought I was saved.

All of a sudden, Baba turned to me and said, "Vandananda, you must find out where he is and report to Me." I thought that now I was trapped.

Nothing can be hidden from Him. I was not sure whether that worker had left the organization or not, but when my Baba, ignoring the GS's questions to the other RS, asked me about him, I was convinced that I should be sure that he had indeed left his fellow monks.

After this, the RDS went on as usual. The next day, when Baba sat on His cot for RDS, He asked the GS, "What about the worker of Jaipur Region who did not report?"

THE HIGHEST EXAMPLE OF HUMANITY

I replied, "Baba, I think he has become negative (i.e. left the organization)."

Baba asked GS what the rule was when a junior worker under one's supervision became negative. Baba asked the GS to make sure that we all knew. Everyone knew that as per this new rule, the RS and the ES lose their avadhutaship. Upon hearing this, dearest

Baba nodded His head as if He was enjoying the drama. It did not affect me also. I knew that on earlier occasions some avadhutas had been demoted to brahmacariis and Baba used to address them as brahamacari. So I thought that my Baba would address me as Brahmacari Vandana. I was really enjoying the whole drama. It is good to be detached from titles and posts but being His avadhuta is a tremendous blessing. Sadly I did not realize how blessed I was to have this title of avadhuta and I did not realize how seriously Baba would take the loss of my avadhutaship. The one who was most affected was the ES (Education, Relief and Welfare Secretary). I noticed him murmuring with disapproval.

The RDS ended and Baba went to His room upstairs in Lake Gardens. At the time, Baba used to come to Tiljala on Saturday and go back to Lake Gardens on Monday. Since it was a weekday, the RDS was being held at Lake Gardens. Baba was in His room and before relaxing He started talking to the PA, the GS and others who were following Him.

THE HIGHEST EXAMPLE OF HUMANITY

Baba started saying that Vandanananda is a very senior worker and he has lost his avadhutaship. What to do? The rule is the rule. It is the same for everyone. Since I was the topic of conversation, someone came running downstairs and asked me to follow him. I rushed there. Dear Baba, time and again, was asking, "Can his avadhutaship be saved?"

GS said, "Baba as a rule, if he gives two workers and sends them to the Varanasi training centre, then his avadhutaship can be saved." Baba had made this rule because it was the duty of the senior worker to compensate for the loss of service to humanity. The purpose of the rule was not just organizational—it concerned social service and upholding the dignity of humanity as well.

Baba looked at me and said, "Vandanananda! Divyarupananda has some candidates with him. He has kept them in observation. Go and touch his feet and beg for two. He will certainly give you. Thus your avadhutaship will be saved."

I came running downstairs and found this dada sitting in the meditation hall. I touched both his feet. He immediately withdrew his feet and asked what I was doing since I was the elder in age and senior in the organization. I then told him what Baba had said. He immediately gave me two of his candidates and joyfully I rushed back to Baba's room. When this was told to Baba, He was happy and so was everyone, except the ES dada.

THE HIGHEST EXAMPLE OF HUMANITY

We were preparing ourselves for noon sadhana and lunch when someone came and told me that ES dada was badly disturbed and was blaming me. I went to him. He had witnessed the entire episode. He accused me for his losing the avadhutaship. He said he would go to Tiljala and not come to Lake Gardens any more, I said, "Dada, why are you so upset. You please take these two candidates in your name and let your avadhutaship be saved."

He then said, "All right, come on and tell the GS."

We went to the GS and I said, "GS Dada, it is not the ES's fault nor mine that the worker became negative. As per Baba's rule, I feel that since I was his immediate supervisory worker, I am responsible. I sacrifice these two candidates for him."

GS Dada replied, "Vandananandaji, Baba Himself saved your avadhutaship. Please think over it."

THE HIGHEST EXAMPLE OF HUMANITY

"What is wrong if he gives these two in my name?" ES Dada asked.

So GS Dada agreed and we went to Baba's room. GS said, "Baba, Vandanananda wants to give his two candidates in the name of Ac Mantreshvarananda Avt., the ES."

Upon hearing this, Baba became very serious and quiet and said, "Let it be."

Baba then further said, "This is the highest example of humanity on this earth. Vandanananda is sacrificing his avadhutaship in order to save the avadhutaship of another person. This is the greatest sacrifice. Now it is the responsibility of the entire humanity to save the avadhutaship of Vandanananda."

THE HIGHEST EXAMPLE OF HUMANITY

Beloved Baba became silent. We all then came out of His room.

SANNYASIS MUST SHARE

I was the RS of Jaipur Region. Once while going to Kolkata for RDS, I purchased bananas on the train and ate them myself. As a rule I should have offered some to my co-passengers, which I did not do. Perhaps, one or two passengers might have taken this selfishness of mine in bad taste. A sannyasi is expected to willingly offer whatever food he has before taking himself.

When the RDS started in Kolkata and my turn came, Baba said in a loud voice,

"Who? Vandanananda? He eats banana by himself. He does not share with others!"

These rebuking words of Baba immediately reminded me of my lack of hospitality and greed on the train. This reminded of the incident where I was scolded for not being hospitable to a visitor who came to see Baba in Ranchi and returned back in reaction to my not offering him food.

SANNYASIS MUST SHARE

Baba had told us clearly in Caryacarya Part 3:

Before sitting down for a meal, invite whatever persons are present around you and share your food with them. If they do not want to share, enquire whether they have enough food with them or not... There is no harm, however, in eating together from the same plate if there is no contagious disease among the participants. Rather, this is a nice thing to see. (Procedure for Eating)

In 1987, on the day of Ananda Purnima DMC, there was a workers meeting in Baba's quarters and then an avadhuta meeting and finally the purodha meeting. I did not leave after the end of the avadhuta meeting as I was curious to see the purodha meeting.

Acarya Shraddhananda noticed that I was there and asked me why I was there when I was not a purodha and had not even learned vishesh yoga. Dada told me Baba would be angry so he gave me an application form to apply for vishesh yoga. I filled it out then and there. Later this application was submitted to Baba and thereafter I was given all the lessons of vishesh yoga.

UNDERSTANDING BABA

As RS I was to come to Kolkatta every month for reporting. We had so many impossible targets that we created myths about them being accomplished and that Baba was going to establish sadvipra samaja soon. We spent so much time travelling back and forth, spending one week for reporting and if all targets were not complete then we would have to wait in Kolkatta until we could give some good news from our posting. In such a situation, there was hardly any time left to do any substantial work in our area. Work like running the children's home, school, printing press was hampered due to lack of a worker to manage these institutions. Yet, Baba created this system and used it to justify scolding us. Why? Why all this drama?

Finally, we workers began to realize the truth. The truth was that Baba's main interest was not the work. His main interest was us. His main interest was me. He wanted to play with us and keep us around Him all the time. He knew that the real work would only begin after 1990 when He publicly departed from us.

Once I heard Baba saying that the war in the Mahabharata was won because of the devotional gopis of Vrindavan and not because of the Pandava warriors. What does this mean? By leaving Vrindavan, Krsna created great heartache (viraha) for the gopas and gopis. Baba said a gopa or gopi is someone who lives only to give anandam (bliss) to the Lord. To give bliss to a human being such that they go into the ecstatic trance of samadhi is hard enough. But to give bliss to the beloved Lord — this is beyond our imagination. So their separation pangs for him or viraha created a powerful force on the earth. Feeling this vibration, the Pandavas were able to develop devotion and surrender to Krsna so that Krsna's shakti could work through them and thereby win the war for Dharma. The glory of Dharma is only beheld by the devotees. One who is the

Lord Himself — the controller and preserver — alone knows how to establish Dharma. Only those devotees who behold Dharma, they alone can establish Dharma — none else.

SWINGING FOR HIS SMILE

After 1984, Baba began a new liila of playing with His workers. Baba was always a genius at coming up with new punishments for workers who failed to meet His infinitely expanding targets. Sometimes the punishment was so hilarious one would be laughing despite the pain and the scolding.

One especially joyous punishment was becoming Baba's swing. The guilty worker was to lie on his back in front of Baba. One or two dadas would hold his legs. Another one or two dadas would hold his arms. Then they would lift the offending worker high in the air and swing him back and forth.

Baba used to enjoy this very much. He used to sing a very funny song (sung by a mother rocking the baby in the cradle) in a joyful, cheery voice like a small child:

Dolá re dolá
Rájá re dolá
(Swing, swing
O my prince, swing)

SWINGING FOR HIS SMILE

Then all of a sudden, Baba would say, "Ahh... You are enjoying here. You have done no work. You think you are a prince here. Throw him over there."

Everyone used to enjoy this, and in fact almost everyone experienced this unique punishment. In such ways, He used to play with us like small boys and make us so happy. Only later we would realize what the joy of those days truly was.

Baba expressed this reality of how He makes the whole universe swing and sway to and fro with His joy. One song we all love is Song 2777:

VISHVA DOLÁY DOL DIYECHO,
LIILÁY BHUVAN NÁCE;
KÁCHE DÚRE NÁNÁN SURE
GIITIR DHÁRÁ MÁJHE
LIILÁY BHUVAN NÁCE;
ÁPAN PARER PRABHED BHOLÁY,
DVÁR VATÁYAN HOLO KHOLÁ
EKHAN SHUDHUI EGIYE CALÁ,
BHÚLE BHIITI LÁJE
LIILÁY BHUVAN NÁCE;
VISHVA TOMÁR LIILÁBHINAY,
NAY HOVE HOYE, HOY HOBE NAY
BRITHÁI KÁNDÁ BRITHÁ ANUNAYA
RASÁBHÁSER SÁJE
LIILÁY BHUVAN NÁCE;
VISHVA DOLÁY DOL DIYECHO,
LIILÁY BHUVAN NÁCE;

SWINGING FOR HIS SMILE

You have been swinging the entire universe.
By Your divine play the world is dancing.
Far and wide, Your countless melodies
Amidst this earth are filled with songs.
By Your divine play the world is dancing
The distinction and discrimination
Between mine and thine is all forgotten
The doors and windows are all open,
Now, only forward movement should prevail,
Fear, shame and shyness should be forgotten.
By Your divine play the world is dancing
The universe is nothing but
Your ever-new, divine play.
Impossible can be made possible
And possible made impossible.
Amidst the display of the cosmic flow,
no such thing as crying and pleading remains.
You have been swinging the entire universe.
By Your divine play the world is dancing.

Another punishment given by Baba was called 'Railway Passing Under Bridge.'

Some short dadas were asked to stand up with their legs apart. Then some tall senior workers who were found guilty of various offences were made to crawl beneath the legs of these short dadas. Everyone used to laugh and enjoy this, especially the junior workers under the supervision of those senior dadas.

TEACHING HOW TO LOVE PLANTS

"Who has brought these plants?" Baba asked Didi Ananda Karuna, who was in charge of collecting the plants to give to Dada Asiimanandaji.

"Dada Vandanananda, Baba, the RS of Jaipur," replied Didi.

"No, I do not accept those plants. Throw them out of My building," said Baba in a very stern voice.

Baba kept on repeating, "I do not want the plants that Vandanananda has brought," stressing this point. Baba didn't listen to further names of workers who had brought plants.

TEACHING HOW TO LOVE PLANTS

GS and PA were outside the room. Every morning Baba used to enquire about Ananda Nagar and about any sick dada, didi, or margi. Normally we used to offer only positive news to Baba. The GS could guess that something serious had taken place and that was why Baba was referring to the plants brought by me. GS sent someone to the main building in Tiljala where I was staying. Baba used to shift to Tiljala during junior RDS (Indian sectorial reporting) and senior RDS (global). He adopted this practice after His quarters in Tiljala were completed.

After giving the plants to Didi Ananda Karuna along with basic information about them, I went to the main building adjoining Baba's residence and started my morning duties.

I was in the bathroom taking a bath when all of a sudden I heard a voice shouting and banging on the bathroom door,

"Vandanananda Dada, you are required to come to Baba's quarters."

TEACHING HOW TO LOVE PLANTS

Taking no notice of what the person was saying, I said, "Please wait your turn. Let me finish my bath first."

Again the person called out, "Dada you are required at Baba's

quarters right now." Again he started banging on the door. Though I had not even started bathing, I opened the door.

That person said, "You must reach Baba's quarters immediately. Go as you are." "What happened?" I guessed that something was seriously wrong. He said, "Baba is furious. GS wants you there right now."

TEACHING HOW TO LOVE PLANTS

I put on my gown and rushed to Baba's quarters. The moment I reached, Baba's mood and tone of voice reached the peak of anger. The GS was standing outside Baba's room talking to Baba through the door.

"Has he come or not?"

"Yes, Baba, he is here," replied GS. Why Baba was so angry so early in the morning the GS did not know. But he was used to such incidents, as it was almost a daily drama — personally I feel that this was a combination of both Baba's spiritual and organizational responsibilities, goading us to work harder and exhausting our samskaras. The GS, as was the custom, began interrogating me.

"You have brought these plants?"

TEACHING HOW TO LOVE PLANTS

"Yes," I said.

"GS punish him," Baba said.

Then the GS started beating a register lying there thus creating a sound of beating for Baba to hear.

Baba said in Bengali "Jor kare maro." (Beat him hard) Through the door, Baba said, "Beat him severely."

TEACHING HOW TO LOVE PLANTS

"Yes Baba I am beating," replied GS and he started beating the register forcefully and sometimes the walls of the room.

Not knowing anything, I added to this performance by crying out as if I was in great pain due to being beaten.

"You brought these plants," GS asked again.

"Yes, I said," while groaning as if in great pain.

TEACHING HOW TO LOVE PLANTS

Hearing this, Baba said, "GS, ask him how many times he took food and water on the way from Gandhidham to Kolkata."

I thought and said, "Many times..."

It so happened when choosing a plant to give Baba, I enquired from the horticultural officer whether this plant — a rare variety from the desert of Kutch — can survive in Kolkata or not. The officer had replied that it could survive. I then asked what should I do while taking it to Kolkata and whether it can survive without water. The officer said it could survive without water for weeks. Thus assured, I boarded the train. It took three nights and two days to reach Kolkata from Gandhidham. I kept the plants under my seat and arrived in Kolkata.

When I replied to Baba that I had eaten and drunk water many times, Baba angrily asked, "How many times did you water My plants?"

TEACHING HOW TO LOVE PLANTS

I realized what Baba was trying to make me understand. I said, "No Baba, I didn't give water." Shamelessly, I tried to justify my irresponsible deed but Baba refused to listen.

Baba said, "The moment you took the determination to bring those plants to My garden, I maintained a link with them. Those plants did not like the treatment you gave them." Baba was silent and so was I. I returned back to the main building, feeling that the whole of RDS that day was for me alone.

BABA IS WITH YOU

Sandhya, the daughter of a good margi from Rajasthan, was married to Parameshvar Dayal from western Uttar Pradesh. He was an engineer who had a high-salaried position in a private company. He was so inspired by Baba's mission to create the garden city of Ananda Nagar in one of the poorest areas of India that he resigned from His job. He took his wife and went to work at AMIT. There was no water supply, no electricity and no proper housing. To make matters worse, violent communist thugs would attack buildings and loot supplies that were meant for service projects.

Sandhya was staying with her husband in the Ananda Marga hospital. She gave birth to her first child in this dangerous place with no electricity. Afterwards, she started complaining angrily about her husband resigning from a high-salaried job to come to this dangerous and primitive place. She declared that soon she would leave and go back to her parents' house.

Shortly afterwards, Baba came to conduct DMC in Ananda Nagar. One day Baba declared that on this day He only wanted to eat Rajasthani food. The sisters in charge of the kitchen started thinking which margi in Bokaro or Ranchi was from Rajasthan. However they failed to think of anyone.

Seeing their disappointed faces, Baba asked what was wrong. They told Baba that they could not think of a margi from Rajasthan. Baba said that they should look in Ananda Nagar itself.

BABA IS WITH YOU

So Sandhya was discovered and she cooked for Baba for the entire day. Baba said to her, "Oh, My little girl, I am with you. Why should you be afraid of living in Ananda Nagar?"

This sister continued to live in Ananda Nagar and is still there with her family.

BABA FULFILLS ALL PROMISES

Baba had many visitors who came to see Him in Tiljala. Perhaps the most famous visitors who came to see Baba were from Africa. At that time, ostriches were only rarely taken outside of Africa. As Baba's programme started to expand beyond collecting rare plants from around the world, some devotees thought to bring two ostriches from Africa. It was a great adventure for these visitors of Baba to be put in an airplane in a cold, dark place and to fly from Africa to Mumbai. It was another adventure for them to wait in Mumbai and then again to fly to Kolkata, where they were taken to Tiljala.

These two ostriches were put in a large, lightly fenced area in Tiljala, the site of the present-day memorial hall. At that time, Baba used to walk from His quarters past the ostrich compound. The ostriches would follow and walk along the fence as Baba was passing to and fro. After this short walk with Baba they would again remain mostly still.

Sadly, these two devotees from Africa died. When the death of the last ostrich was reported to Baba, He kept silent. Then Baba started telling the story of these two ostriches.

There was a great fakir in Kashmir who had many disciples that used to live with him as brothers and sisters. This was the rule of his ashram. One brother and sister became attached to each other. This was disturbing the collective spiritual flow.

BABA FULFILLS ALL PROMISES

The fakir called both of them and sternly warned them to break this attachment of theirs. Both the brother and sister promised to do this.

However they could not keep their promise. They became more and more close. Finally, one day the fakir called them and asked them to leave his community.

Tearfully, both the disciples said that they could not bear to be

separated from their master who they loved so much. They then said that at the same time they were also closely attached to each other.

BABA FULFILLS ALL PROMISES

The fakir kept silent. The two disciples said that they would go but they asked for a blessing: that they would die at the feet of their master.

The fakir said, "So be it."

Baba then said that he was keeping the promise made by the fakir to those two erring disciples who were reborn as ostriches.

SPECIALTY OF ANANDA MARGA PHILOSOPHY

When I was the RS of Jaipur, a new rule was introduced by our Baba that every worker up to Area level (north, west, east, south) should be present in every month's RDS at Kolkata. Furthermore, every diocese also had to be represented by one worker if there were more than one worker in that diocese. Since Baba was staying in Kolkata, Kolkata Region had its own reporting session. Thereafter for monks, LFTs, bhukti pradhans and UBPs (margis in charge of a block), a separate schedule was introduced.

Why did Baba create all these categories of margis and workers with their own schedule for reporting? By nature, Baba lives to give love. It is just like a mother who wants to give love to her sons, but her sons are worldly minded and want to enjoy themselves outside. So what does the mother do? She creates various chores for the naughty sons to do in the house. This way, she keeps them running and working around her and enjoys directing them, scolding them and loving them. While doing the chores she will get upset for even the smallest mistake so that the boys lose their confidence and are forced to ask her how to do the task. This way she can love them while instructing them for a long time how to do even the simplest task. This is how Baba, who said in Prabhata Samgiita that Parama Purusa is full of mamatá (possessive love where He says that we belong to Him only), created this organizational drama.

The IRSS (Inspection, Review & Structural Solidarity) program was to be done in a region where the central organizational authority was physically present. The IRSS of workers of Kolkata region used to be over by the eighteenth or nineteenth of every month and the rest of Delhi Sector's workers had to reach Kolkata before the night of the twentieth. Overseas workers were required to reach

for reporting by the night of the twenty-fifth of every month and the Global RDS used to start by the morning of the twenty-sixth. I would like to share with the reader one particular incident that took place during one RDS. Baba had floated a question during the Kolkata Region RDS, seeking a proper answer. When I reached there on the twentieth, I and other workers were given a chit of paper with the question.

SPECIALTY OF ANANDA MARGA PHILOSOPHY

The question was, "What is the fundamental difference among the philosophy of Ananda Marga and others (Eastern or Western)?" I said the answer was very simple. Many central dadas who had been there for Kolkata RDS asked me, "Are you sure you know the answer?" I replied that the answer was very simple. They asked me to tell them but I said, "Why should I tell you? But still I know the answer." No one had given the answer in the Kolkata RDS (IRSS) so the question had assumed great importance and aroused curiosity in the minds of the workers. The next day, as soon as the RDS started, Baba wanted to know the answer.

"Vijayananda, do they know the question?" Baba demanded.

"Yes, Baba," replied Dada.

"Yes! Who knows the answer?" No dadas came forward. I watched the situation for a while and summoned up my courage. I came forward and did sastaunga pranama to Baba.

SPECIALTY OF ANANDA MARGA PHILOSOPHY

"Who? Vandanananda?"

"Yes, Baba."

"You know the question?"

"Yes, Baba."

SPECIALTY OF ANANDA MARGA PHILOSOPHY

"What is the question?"

I replied, "What is the difference between the philosophy of Ananda Marga and other philosophies?"

Baba corrected me saying, "Not 'between' but 'among'." Baba explained that when the comparison is with two objects, then the word "between" should be used. And when the comparison is between many objects, then the word "among" should be used. So the question was "What is the fundamental difference among the philosophy of Ananda Marga and other philosophies?"

I said, "Baba, the answer is very simple. The concept of Taraka Brahma is not found in any philosophy of the world." Baba then asked me to explain in English. I said that Qualified Brahma (Consciousness) and Non-qualified Brahma is mentioned in other schools of philosophy but that the Brahma who has the qualities of both is the Taraka Brahma. This reality is revealed only in Ananda Marga philosophy. "Yes, this is the answer." Baba replied. Baba was very happy. He called me nearer to Him. I went forward and put my head in His lap. Baba blessed me by placing both His soft, divine hands upon my head. For the remainder of the RDS, Baba was again and again talking about me. With my head still feeling Baba's hands and my mind still drenched in that blissful blessing, I truly felt that this particular RDS was only for me.

BLISSFULLY BUSY IN VARANASI

I cannot express in writing how happy I was when I reached Varanasi, because I was not trained at this training centre. And now I was there as CTS taking care of a big establishment. Taking care of the whole establishment, organising the purchase of all the food, giving classes to the trainees were all part of this joy. As a rule, there was a school attached to the training centre with more than three hundred students that had to be taken care of. There were some cows under the care of an old margi and great devotee named Dadu. There was also a Master Unit surrounding the training centre. All of this kept me very busy. There were three dogs, a monkey and some birds as well. Many wild animals like foxes, jackals and snakes were also part of our family. There were also many large lizards that used to eat up my vegetables and fruits. During the rainy season, I used to plant water fruit (paniphal) around the pond.

In Varanasi, I had to look after the patients who came to stay there. Apart from being responsible for building construction, legal affairs, the primary school, training the trainees and taking care of the farm and Master Unit as well as the animals. My other task was to visit the other training centres, like Ananda Shiila, Ahmedabad and Bangalore to conduct examinations and I had to attend reporting in Lake Gardens or Tiljala every alternative Wednesday. Wednesday was the day CTS or GTS was supposed to be present there for reporting.

Ac Sadhaneshvaranandaji, who met with a fatal accident, was the CTS of Ananda Shiila. His cremation was done in the training centre and his ashes are still there. He was a very caring, loving, intelligent and hardworking dada, and he used to come to Varanasi to conduct exams. His death was a great loss to me. After his demise, someone from the centre used to come to conduct exams. Baba

had full knowledge not just about the centre and the trainees but also about the animals. Whenever anyone from the centre went to Tiljala, we used to send khoya for Baba made from the milk of our cows, which He liked very much. If not sent, Baba would ask what had happened.

BLISSFULLY BUSY IN VARANASI

Once I told one of my dogs to chase a big lizard that had been eating the roots of our vegetables and fruits. Sadly, two dogs attacked it and brought it to me and then the trainees killed it. Later, when I went for reporting, Baba said, “No living beings including snakes should be killed in the Prashiksana Math.” Later I came to know there were many poisonous snakes that had done no harm to the trainees all these years. That was enough for me to understand the sanctity of the centre and the importance of not taking the life of even an animal causing problems.

Another time in reporting, Baba told me, “You should not be careless about the food materials you get from the shops; you should take proper care of them.” That was enough for me to understand which incident Baba was referring to. A week earlier, Dadu had awakened early in the morning and saw Baba moving on the road before our ashram. Baba was standing next to our monthly food stock that was lying on the road. The previous day was a fasting day and a hot summer day and I was tired and dehydrated. The trainees dropped the food stock late at night and came and told me that the food stock was lying outside and should be brought inside. I said OK but then I just slept.

Dadu came rushing to me in my room when He saw Baba in full white dress and said to me, “Please get up. Baba has come.” Unmindfully I arose from sleep on his insistence and followed him. When I reached the place where he said that he had seen Baba, I was surprised to find the entire food stock lying there, safe from thieves.

Because of legal complications, we were under the apprehension that if we demolished the old crumbling building it might invite legal hurdles. Many central dadas warned me not to do this. But I went ahead and demolished the old building and constructed the ground floor. When the second floor was being constructed, hardly I had done the roofing (not walls) when the police came to stop it because the opposite party had obtained a stay order from the court. This was a great achievement to have a concrete building, even though still the case is going on today.

BLISSFULLY BUSY IN VARANASI

During that period there was great pressure to collect plants for Ananda Nagar. At one time, I had to provide plants for Baba’s garden also. Once I had to arrange for fifteen thousand Beri plants, special varieties that were available only in Varanasi. We sent them by truck to Ananda Nagar.

Those days were so blissful for me because I was busily engaged in Baba’s work.

MICROVITA MARVELS OF MAHAKAOLA

Background

I had gone to Lake Gardens for reporting. Like all the devotees who were blessed to go there at that time, I felt so fortunate because I was going to actually meet, see and hear my beloved Lord, my Baba. The duty allotted to me was to impart training to the wholetimer candidates at the Varanasi training centre as CTS (central training secretary). Varanasi and Ananda Shiila were only an overnight train journey from Kolkata. Hence the trainer of Ananda Shiila and myself used to be blessed with the chance to attend weekly reporting. Sometimes the GTS used to attend the RDS.

Every Wednesday was reporting for trainees. During an earlier reporting, Baba had played a trick on me of profound significance. My turn for reporting used to come every second week. He summoned the GS and myself into His room. Baba was sitting upon His cot. First Baba gave me a soul-touching inspirational talk.

"You know," he said, "The work which normally needs a specific amount of time should be completed with a faster speed in a lesser amount of time. The time, once gone, is gone forever; it is out of your hand. The wise man should move faster than the fastest and complete the work earlier than the time allotted."

The sonorous flow of Baba's voice, His deep seriousness, made my heart swell with feelings as I tried to grasp the meaning of what He was saying. There was a little pause. Baba's eyes were locked with mine. I found Baba so serious.

Background

Then He continued. "I am fully depending upon you to complete the work. We are to cover all the blocks and districts of India at the earliest."

I found myself unable to fathom the significance of Baba's words. In fact, I was no longer myself. My mind was in motion, merging

with Him. Folding my hands, I nodded my head in affirmation and said, "Yes, Baba we should."

"Well then," He said, "we are to cover all the blocks and districts with workers. We cannot wait for a longer period. Presently, I am in need of sixteen workers in four weeks time. The choice how many you give each week lies with you. You can give four each week or eight in the last week and the rest in the three weeks. The choice is yours."

Background

Baba then fell silent. My mind went back to the trainees in the three training centres — Varanasi, Ananda Shiila and Ahmedabad. I summoned my courage and, understanding the situation of the trainees quite well, I told Baba that we did not want to bring the candidates who were not fully trained. I tried to explain my position. Normally we used to bring one or two candidates per week, and that too, often only to avoid getting a zero mark during reporting — to save our own skin. I could not understand Baba's sudden emphasis on speed.

Baba then became a bit harsh with me. At one time, as regards the covering of villages, panchayats, blocks and districts, we had realised that the intellectual accomplishes much less work than the spiritual worker. This ethos of the spiritual worker occupied my mind. The condition of the training centres, the standard of the trainees and demands of Baba for more trainees — all of these were boiling in my mind. I felt I was going nowhere. I was far from attaining the level of speed that Baba desired in manifesting His mission. I was fully lost.

I had no choice but to accept the cosmic dictum. I raised my hands and prostrated before Baba. I was silently speaking in my heart, "O my Baba, it is Thy Work and Thou alone will do it. Ye, my Lord, Thou art not a human being. Thou art God. Thou can do any work, at any moment. Nothing is impossible for Thee."

When I raised myself from the ground, I looked towards Baba. He was smiling with such bliss, such sublime serenity.

Background

I retreated back to the other room. GS Dada followed. I asked GS Dada how it was possible to do what Baba wanted.

"You know the position well," GS retorted, "You should have resisted. You should not have succumbed to the pressure."

I could not understand the twin roles of the GS — the role he played inside Baba's room and the role he played outside His room. The reader may wish to know to what degree the pressure continued up and until the days before Baba's physical departure. The answer is that we had no other choice. The situation unfolded in such a way that we had to send the candidates for becoming a monk within a week of their arrival in the training centre. The pressure got so overwhelming that I even sent around 150 trainees within a six-month period.

Only later, to our great grief, could we realize how right Baba had been to force us to do this. Baba had already planned His departure and hence was declaring an emergency in the creation of monks so that He could personally bless as many monks as possible with the ecstasy of His physical darshan and Personal Contact.

Background

In September 1990 I was transferred to the PROUT Department. This was a big surprise since much earlier the PROUT department had requested Baba to have me posted as their worker.

This was not just my experience alone; rather it was the experience of all central workers at this time. Baba was creating tremendous reservoirs of bliss by His physical contact to enable us to survive the ordeal of the ensuing condition of 'internal emergency' after 1990.

Mystery Disease

The day was Wednesday, 4 October 1989, the day fixed for reporting for the training centres of India and abroad. Each day in the month was booked for a specific department or some special work considering its priority and urgency. The reporting day used to seem so tough because of the tension and the ordeal of coping with Baba's high expectations arising out of His ardent desire to do more and more for suffering humanity. On Wednesday, the transfers and postings had to be finalized before 5 a.m., which was a brain-wracking task. Then we had to face Baba with all the details. If there were any mistakes, we would be subject to much scolding. After that, the candidates had to prepared for four examinations by four different people and then their Personal Contact with Baba had to be arranged.

When I went to Kolkata the next month, news of the crisis in Ahmedabad had already reached there. Acarya Bhavaprakashananda Avadhuta had arrived the previous morning with eleven candidates (six for the current week and five for the next week). The news he brought shocked everyone, especially me, for I was directly responsible for the training centre.

As per schedule, I was supposed to be at Lake Gardens before five in the morning. To be safe, I had stayed overnight. We (myself, GS Dada and PA Dada) were in the first room sitting near the door of Baba's room.

The telephone rang. I picked up the receiver and the caller at the end of the line was the head trainer of the Ahmedabad training centre, Acarya Viitamohanananda. He recognized my voice and urgently asked me to bring up the crisis.

Mystery Disease

He said to me, "You know the environment around the training centre very well because you were here for three to four months to undergo treatment for your eye. How fresh the air and water are, how healthy the natural surroundings are, above all how strong the spiritual vibrations are."

I asked him what had happened. "Brother," he said, "suddenly I found change in every respect. Everything got worse and worse. Everyone living there, including myself, developed unusual symptoms — swelling that began in the toes and gradually moved up to the legs, hips, penis, chest and face. Then the body became pale and weak. Vomiting was the death signal. One of the trainees has already died and four are in the hospital in critical condition, fighting for their life. The doctors have failed to diagnose the disease. Acarya Dattatreya Brahmacarii left after a week's stay to get treatment in Mumbai. On the way he vomited, and after reaching Mumbai he died. Brother, please do me a favour. Bring this matter to the notice of Baba. He and He alone can protect us. His blessing is our only hope, our only solace, the only rescue."

Dada's voice became very feeble and I could sense his tears. I realized that his voice was aimed at the Master — our Saviour. My heart died inside me. In a very soft voice, I said, "Yes, brother, Baba should do something and He will do something."

I explained to the GS and the PA what I had heard and apprised them about the medical condition of the trainees. Within ten minutes the telephone rang again. This time it was Dr. A. K. Saha, an old and devoted margi of Ahmedabad. He had been taking care of the Ahmedabad trainees since the centre opened and he also was dealing with the crisis.

Mystery Disease

He said, "Dada, the trainees have peculiar symptoms that are unknown to the medical world. All of us attending doctors have failed to diagnose it. If something spiritual is not done, they all will die. Please seek the blessings of Baba. He alone can guide us." I assured him that we would surely apprise Baba of the situation.

Baba had not yet opened the morning dialogue in which He first used to gather news about the health of workers and margis or about sick persons whose health He was already monitoring.

That Wednesday was a fateful day in the history of Mother Earth. The acarya candidates who had come from Ahmedabad had swelling all over their bodies. Then came the third phone call. It was from the bhakti pradhana of Ahmedabad, Shrii Dwarika Bhai. He shared with me the same information about the crisis.

GS, PA and I were sitting next to Baba's room watching Baba's door. At anytime Baba could start the morning conversation. At exactly 5:13 a.m. we heard Baba's voice. The three of us bent our heads closer to the lowest panel of the door so that we could hear Baba's voice. GS dada mentioned to Baba that I was present. Baba then asked about the training centre. I indicated to the GS that now was the time to break the news of the crisis to Baba.

Mystery Disease

GS Dada said, "The Ahmedabad training centre is not well. All the trainees and the trainer are suffering from a strange infection. One died after a brief sickness. Four are in the hospital. The doctors are not able to reach any conclusions as to the cause of the disease."

Then GS Dada became silent, waiting for Baba's response. We all had our hearts in our mouths. After a while, Baba broke the silence. He said, "I know." Then He paused, the silence vibrating with the force of His concentration. Then Baba said, "A special kind of negative microvita has come from another planet. It wants to destroy the living organisms of this earth. It has no name. Its effects will be greater and more rapid upon those without a mustache or beard. Ladies will not be affected."

Baba was talking in such a way that we realized that He knew every detail about this unknown disease. Then Baba said with quiet but tremendous authority, "I will not allow the Earth to be destroyed." Baba then remained silent in His room for over three hours.

Baba had known that this disease was coming. Instead of allowing it to spread all over the planet, Baba had redirected it to come to our Prashiksana Math, that most holy place where the devotees are trained to become acaryas, ones who teach others spirituality, morality and dharmayuddha (righteous war) by their conduct in daily life. It was a place where young men renounced the pleasures, fame and comforts of worldly life and assembled to dedicate their lives to serving the Lord and the suffering humanity. Two of our noble boys had given their lives to this disease and had thereby saved humanity. The training centre was a place where sadhana and kiirtana was done four times a day, where the moral principles of yama & niyama, Sixteen Points and sannyasi rules were followed very strictly both in letter and in spirit. This spiritually consecrated place, which was radiating positive microvita, significantly weakened the force of that negative microvita. Had this disease been allowed to work elsewhere, you can imagine how disastrous it would have been. I feel future research on microvita will reveal the historical facts about the magnitude and gravity of the devastation that would have been caused if this negative microvita had spread all over the planet. Baba nipped this danger in the bud.

Mystery Disease

Later Baba said that the great influenza epidemic after World War I, where millions died in a matter of weeks, was caused by negative microvita attracted to the earth by the countless corpses of soldiers killed in the barbaric First World War of nationalist dogma.

I remembered the story of Krsna and Govardhana. Even as a small boy Krsna aroused the consciousness of the people against religious dogma. At that time the religion was the Vedic religion and the main god was Indra, the thunder god. Krsna convinced the people to stop worshipping this god out of fear. Later there was an unusual and tremendously powerful storm. This storm was also caused by negative microvita, I feel. Krsna saved the people by revealing His divine power. Countless thoughts like this were flowing through my mind as I was doing my morning sadhana. This flow was a flow full of Baba's light and I enjoyed that morning sadhana perhaps more than any other previous morning sadhana.

At 9:20 a.m. the bell of Baba's room rang again. We rushed to the door. Baba asked us to suggest an alternative place where the training centre could be shifted. I looked towards the GS and PA to see if they had any ideas. Cleverly the GS left the talking to me. I had been thinking that the trainees should be shifted to Varanasi, as now it was not possible for them to stay in Ahmedabad. This would help since I had been struggling to coordinate the three centres. I thought that two training centres were enough. Hence when Baba asked about an alternative place, my mind jumped with eagerness. My eyes widened and shined as with glee, I said, "Varanasi."

Immediately Baba replied, "No." Baba asked me to suggest some other place. He wanted to know if there was any place in the states bordering Gujarat — Maharashtra, Madhya Pradesh and Rajasthan. I had worked earlier as RS Jaipur and knew the area well. I could not think of any single place that could conveniently accommodate a training centre. Hence I suggested Gandhidham, which Baba approved.

Mystery Disease

Gandhidham was a small city in Kutch, which was a marshy seaside area. Baba had talked about it on a number of occasions, expressing the importance of Kutch. Baba said in Shabda Cayanika that the Kutch dialect of Gujarati was a sweet dialect blended with a little Sindhi from across the border in Pakistan. Interestingly, Baba said that the entire area was under the supervision of Maharsi Bhrgu and that the city of Bharuch in modern-day Kutch comes from the word Bhrgu Kaccha. Bhrgu's son, Vaedarbhi, is one of the rebel sages of the Atharva Veda, which incorporated Tantra into the Vedic tradition, which is why the school of Bhrgu is associated with Krsna Tantra. More significantly, in the Taiitiriya Upanishad there is a famous section by Maharsi Bhrgu that says that all the beings of the entire universe have come from ananda (bliss), are existing in ananda and will eventually merge in this ananda, which is Brahman (Pure Consciousness). From this shloka comes not just our spiritual ideology but also the idea of ananda parivara (the ideal that the entire universe is a joint family), as well as the idea of cosmic inheritance, that everything in the universe belongs to

all. Thus it was indeed fitting that the training centre be moved to Gandhidham from Ahmedabad.

After a little pause, Baba said to me, "Bring me some red-coloured flowers, the same quantity as the number of persons affected by the negative microvita."

Then Baba said, "I will not allow that negative microvita to destroy the Earth."

Mystery Disease

I have seen and heard Baba on many occasions when He had to command Nature in favour of His devotees. Now His voice was in that exact tone and vibration. He was truly in full command as Mahasambhuti (special manifestation of Liberating Consciousness or Taraka Brahma).

My mind went back 3500 years ago, thinking of how Lord Krsna had saved his people when negative microvita had assembled in the form of a devastating storm. Then also, Lord Krsna had commanded Nature with full authority. No force on earth can harm anybody if the Lord's favour is there. In this particular liila Baba proved Himself to be the Lord, the great Saviour.

Baba's Medicine

Then Baba prescribed some instructions for the affected persons and I noted them down in my diary. The prescriptions were as follows:

  1. a) Cumin seed should be heated in a black earthen stove and heated until the smell disappears. But it should not be burnt. Then grind it with a stone grinder and add rock salt as per taste. The rock salt should not be excessive.

b) Squash by hand fully ripe, red tomatoes and keep them in a glass. Mix one tablespoon or two teaspoons of this cumin powder in a glass of juice. Filter it through a thin cloth. Then warm the juice under the hot sun or fire until it becomes lukewarm. Then it can be taken. In no case should the juice be directly heated in the fire. It should be taken twice: once in the early morning when the stomach is empty and again five hours after one's meal. By then the food will be fully digested.

  1. Under no circumstances are they to consume white salt.
  2. Under no circumstances are they to drink cold liquid or eat cold food. The drink and the food should not be less than body temperature. It should always be taken lukewarm.
  3. They are to use whole wheat flour for chapattis, not white flour.
  4. Only raw rice is to be taken, not pre-boiled rice.
  5. They are not to use mustard oil. If they like, they can use ghee or groundnut oil; however, the quantity should be very, very small.
  6. They are not to have milk or yoghurt (curd). Buttermilk can be taken in small quantities. It should also be taken warm and not cold.
  7. They should eat boiled vegetables and especially green vegetables. Leafy vegetables like spinach and green papaya should be eaten regularly.
  8. Potatoes or roots should be taken very rarely. It is better if they avoid it.
  9. It is always better if they drink from well and stream water.

Baba's Medicine

After giving these instructions, Baba went back to His normal routine. At noon He left for His sublime residence in Tiljala, Madhu Koraka. Everyone, including myself, was relaxed and tension free now that Baba had personally prescribed a cure. The information was sent to the trainer, Viitamohanandaji.

The next morning I got up early, finished my morning routine, went to Baba's quarters to do Guru Puja and silently left to collect the flowers. My mind was fully occupied with the Ahmedabad trainees, wondering whether they had gotten some mental relief after hearing that Baba had prescribed a cure. I was trying to empathize with how they must be feeling.

It was around noon when Baba asked for the flowers, which were promptly given by GS on a round white plate provided by Acarya Kinshukji, the adopted son of Baba. We had both gone to the market for these flowers. Baba instructed that the flowers should be red and half-bloomed and thus we chose half-bloomed roses. The

plate was covered with a white cloth. Baba was sitting in sahaja asana (cross-legged) on His cot. He had a white shawl on. Baba took the plate of flowers in His Hands and signaled to the GS to leave.

Baba's Medicine

We all, especially I, were very curious. With my eyes lowered, I stood at the door with my right ear to it.

Baba was reciting some shlokas. Unfortunately, we could not make them out. It was perhaps a divine benediction that the Mahakaola (Great Tantric) was doing to save the kaolas (tantric disciples). We gazed at each other and wrung our hands, expressing our inability to comprehend what was happening.

Within two minutes the divine benediction was over. We only can say that positive microvita was infused in each and every flower, nay indeed all over the earth.

The bell rang and GS entered Baba's room. And what did I do? Mischievously, I nudged the door open with my leg and peeked inside. First, my eyes were drawn to Baba's face. I saw Baba sitting in the bháva (ecstatic stance) of kalyana sundaram (sublime beauty of absolute benevolence). Baba was holding the plate upon His lap with both hands. I felt as if Baba had armoured the entire earth. Baba Himself said in 1987:

Baba's Medicine

In the past there was no mass imposition of positive microvita. For the first time the collective application of the positive microvita is being done to elevate the spiritual standard of the general mass. (Questions and Answers on Microvita, Microvitum In a Nutshell)

The GS came out of the room and I came forward and fell prostrate before the Lord of the entire Brahmacakra, my Baba. Baba then told me, "You carry these flowers yourself. Remember that no flower should fall on the ground." Then Baba explained to me twice how to use the flowers to ward off the malign effects of the negative microvita. To be sure that I had thoroughly understood, He asked me twice to repeat the instructions to Him. Baba again emphasized that the youths who had no mustache and beard would be affected first and foremost and that ladies would not be affected.

The instructions were:

  1. First apply the flower on the ajina cakra (between the eyebrows), second on the anahata cakra (between the nipples), third on the manipura cakra (navel), fourth on both the hands (including wrists), starting with the right hand. Sixth at the tip of the penis, seventh on both testicles, eighth on the knees, starting with the right knee, ninth on both big toes and tenth on the sahasrara cakra (crown of the skull).

Baba's Medicine

  1. One flower will not be used twice and under no circumstances should it be used by two persons.
  1. So long as the sun is visible in the sky, the sick persons can take bath in lukewarm water but not at night.
  1. It is better if they take a sunbath with very few clothes on the body.
  1. Tie the leaves of kadamba plant (neolamarckia cadamba) around the legs and penis if there is swelling.

Baba's Medicine

  1. The shift from Ahmedabad to Gandhidham was only to be done during the daytime, not at night.
  1. If any trainee was too sick, they should be taken upon a stretcher.

Why should this process end at the sahasrara cakra? This has great significance. After finishing all the bondages of physicality connected with the five lowest cakras and the lower motor organs, and after finishing with all bondages of the mind connected with the ajina cakra, we come to the realm of pure spirituality, pure Consciousness. The process begins with the ajina cakra because it is the controlling point of all lower cakras. The process ends with the sahasrara because above the sahasrara is the blindingly brilliant radiance. This radioactive radiance is so powerful that seeing it is somewhat like seeing burning magnesium in the laboratory. And this blazing effulgence is at the same time so, so sweet because it is coming from the beauteous feet of Lord Anandamurtiji. This can

be seen by anyone who tries to love Baba. And it is the beatitude of those supremely tender feet of Baba that alone can heal the sorrows of His little boys and girls in every nook and corner of this tiny universe.

Baba's Medicine

Baba then asked what train I was leaving on. I replied that I was leaving on the Howrah-Ahmedabad Express. Baba nodded His head. I felt that He had given me His consent to go. I fell forward in sastaunga pranama.

Still today I remember His face, His sublime gestures, His voice and how utterly blissful and gracious He was.

Each and every flower was kept separately in a plastic box and then they all were wrapped in a big cloth. I kept them in my custody, feeling like a messenger from heaven bringing the gift of immortality.

After making the necessary arrangements for the use of the microvitized flowers upon the trainees from Ahmedabad who were in Kolkata, I left to do sastaunga pranama again inside Baba's quarters and then left for Ahmedabad.

Celebrating His Blessing

The long tedious thirty-nine hour journey passed in no time. I was in a different world of divine imagination in which my mind was sometimes in Kolkata, remembering the entire series of events, and sometimes in Ahmedabad with the trainees, imagining what they must be feeling now that they had heard the news of Baba's blessings and that the medicine was on its way. Nevertheless, I must confess that I was in special ecstasy because I, this small, mischievous boy of Baba, was carrying the positive microvita from the land of the Lord, our Saviour, unto the land of death.

The fact was that everyone at the Ahmedabad training centre had come to know about these flowers blessed with microvita by Baba. The light of grace and inspiration had entered everyone's heart. The moment I could be seen from the training centre, everyone started jumping with joy.

The atmosphere was charged with cries of Param Pita Baba Kii Jai! (Victory to Baba, the Supreme Father) and Amader Baba kii Jai! (Victory to our Baba!)

Try to imagine yourself in my place and think what would have been your state of mind. The fortress erected by this nameless negative microvita coming from another planet to destroy all living beings upon this Earth — this powerful fortress had to be broken. Thinking thus, I stopped at the main gate and with my mind immersed in Baba, I entered the Ahmedabad ashram and straightaway went to the trainer's room.

Celebrating His Blessing

I was well aware of my position, hence I kept the blessed, microvitized flowers in the safest place and instructed everyone not to touch them.

The evening and night passed narrating and explaining to everyone what had happened in Kolkata and how Baba knew everything in advance, how He had asked for the flowers, the grandeur of His benediction, and so forth. The preparation for shifting the headquarters to Gandhidham was also in full swing.

The next morning, as scheduled, the chartered bus arrived. Acarya Nigamananda, the GTS, had arrived by flight and joined us. After performing collective Guru Puja, we boarded the bus. Other than a few clothes the trainees and trainer had left everything behind to be burnt including blankets, cooking vessels, etc. Even the ashram's pet dog joined us on the bus. I especially felt sorry to leave behind Monia the peacock, and twenty-four pigeons because it was I who had nursed them from when they were baby chicks. They were part of our family.

The moment of departure from the land of death came, and when the driver started the engine, everyone's minds and hearts were charged with emotion. We all shouted, Param Pita Baba Kii Jai! (Victory to Baba, the Supreme Father) and Amader Baba kii Jai! (Victory to my Baba!).

Celebrating His Blessing

Obsessively I kept turning my head back to look at the ashram. It bore a deserted look and the peacock stood on the gate wall of the ashram and cried out. I could feel her pangs of separation. The neighbours, although very few, were very dear to us. We also felt the touch of pain in their hearts. The bus moved on and headed towards the hospital where we had to face a great ordeal. The doc-
